\mathchoice{(\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{(\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{(\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{(\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}=\frac{1}{2}\left(|{\nabla}|(\partial^{\alpha}\mathring{{\bf B}}_{\beta})+(\partial_{\beta}\mathring{{\bf B}}^{\alpha})|{\nabla}|\right)+\frac{1}{2}[|{\nabla}|,\mathring{{\bf B}}\cdot\nabla]\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta},

\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}
is a matrix-valued anti-symmetric 1st order operator of the form

\mathchoice{(\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{(\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{(\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{(\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}=\frac{1}{2}\left(|{\nabla}|(\partial^{\alpha}\mathring{{\bf B}}_{\beta})-(\partial_{\beta}\mathring{{\bf B}}^{\alpha})|{\nabla}|\right),

\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}
is a covector-valued 0th order operator of the form

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}})_{\beta}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial_{\gamma}}{|{\nabla}|}(\partial^{\gamma}\mathring{{\bf B}}_{\beta}+\partial_{\beta}\mathring{{\bf B}}^{\gamma}),

and
\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}_{\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}};\pm}
is a matrix-valued 0th order operator of the form

\mathchoice{(\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}_{\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}};\pm})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{(\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}_{\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}};\pm})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{(\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}_{\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}};\pm})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{(\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}_{\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}};\pm})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}=[\mathchoice{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}},(\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}})\cdot\nabla]-\mathchoice{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}}(\partial_{\beta}(\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}})^{\gamma}).

We also make the obvious observation that each
b_{\pm}
satisfies the divergence-free condition

\nabla\cdot b_{\pm}=0.

(3.7)

Proof.

Note that

\mathchoice{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}=\frac{1}{2}\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}\pm\frac{1}{2}|{\nabla}|^{-1}(\nabla\times)^{\alpha}_{\beta}+\frac{1}{2}|{\nabla}|^{-2}\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla_{\beta}.

Recall the definition of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}
in (
3.3
):

\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}b=\underbrace{\nabla\times((\nabla\times b)\times\mathring{{\bf B}})}_{\mathcal{P}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}b}+\underbrace{\nabla\times((\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}})\times b)}_{\mathcal{Q}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}b}

We first compute

\displaystyle\left(\Pi_{\pm}(D)\mathcal{P}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}b\right)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\left(\Pi_{\pm}(D)\left(\nabla\times((\nabla\times b)\times\mathring{{\bf B}})\right)\right)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{2}\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}\pm\frac{1}{2}|{\nabla}|(\nabla\times)^{\alpha}_{\beta}\right)\left(\nabla\times((\nabla\times b)\times\mathring{{\bf B}})\right)^{\beta}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathring{{\bf B}}\cdot\nabla(\nabla\times)^{\alpha}_{\beta}b^{\beta}-(\partial_{\gamma}\mathring{{\bf B}}^{\alpha})(\nabla\times)^{\gamma}_{\beta}b^{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\pm\frac{1}{2}|{\nabla}|^{-1}(-\Delta)\left((\mathring{{\bf B}}\cdot\nabla)b^{\alpha}-\mathring{{\bf B}}_{\beta}\partial^{\alpha}b^{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\pm\frac{1}{2}|{\nabla}|^{-1}\partial^{\alpha}\partial_{\gamma}\left((\mathring{{\bf B}}\cdot\nabla)b^{\gamma}-\mathring{{\bf B}}_{\beta}\partial^{\gamma}b^{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathring{{\bf B}}\cdot\nabla(\nabla\times)^{\alpha}_{\beta}b^{\beta}\pm|{\nabla}|(\mathring{{\bf B}}\cdot\nabla)\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}b^{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+\frac{1}{2}\left(-(\partial_{\gamma}\mathring{{\bf B}}^{\alpha})(\nabla\times)^{\gamma}_{\beta}b^{\beta}\pm|{\nabla}|(\partial^{\alpha}\mathring{{\bf B}}_{\gamma})\delta^{\gamma}_{\beta}b^{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\pm\frac{1}{2}\partial^{\alpha}|{\nabla}|^{-1}\left(\Delta(\mathring{{\bf B}}_{\beta}b^{\beta})+(\partial_{\beta}\mathring{{\bf B}}^{\gamma})\partial_{\gamma}b^{\beta}-\partial_{\gamma}(\mathring{{\bf B}}_{\beta}\partial^{\gamma}b^{\beta})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathring{{\bf B}}\cdot\nabla|{\nabla}|(\pm b_{\pm}\mp b_{\mp})^{\alpha}\pm|{\nabla}|(\mathring{{\bf B}}\cdot\nabla)(b_{\pm}+b_{\mp})^{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+\frac{1}{2}\left(-(\partial_{\beta}\mathring{{\bf B}}^{\alpha})|{\nabla}|(\pm b_{\pm}\mp b_{\mp})^{\beta}\pm|{\nabla}|(\partial^{\alpha}\mathring{{\bf B}}_{\beta})(b_{\pm}+b_{\mp})^{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\pm\partial^{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial_{\gamma}}{|{\nabla}|}\left((\partial^{\gamma}\mathring{{\bf B}}_{\beta}+\partial_{\beta}\mathring{{\bf B}}^{\gamma})(b_{\pm}+b_{\mp})^{\beta}\right)\right)

After rearranging terms, we obtain

\displaystyle\left(\Pi_{\pm}(D)\mathcal{P}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}b\right)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\pm\frac{1}{2}(\mathring{{\bf B}}\cdot\nabla|{\nabla}|+|{\nabla}|(\mathring{{\bf B}}\cdot\nabla))b_{\pm}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\pm\frac{1}{2}[|{\nabla}|,\mathring{{\bf B}}\cdot\nabla]b_{\mp}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\pm\frac{1}{2}\left(|{\nabla}|(\partial^{\alpha}\mathring{{\bf B}}_{\beta})-(\partial_{\beta}\mathring{{\bf B}}^{\alpha})|{\nabla}|\right)b_{\pm}^{\beta}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\pm\frac{1}{2}\left(|{\nabla}|(\partial^{\alpha}\mathring{{\bf B}}_{\beta})+(\partial_{\beta}\mathring{{\bf B}}^{\alpha})|{\nabla}|\right)b_{\mp}^{\beta}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\pm\partial^{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial_{\gamma}}{|{\nabla}|}\left((\partial^{\gamma}\mathring{{\bf B}}_{\beta}+\partial_{\beta}\mathring{{\bf B}}^{\gamma})(b_{\pm}+b_{\mp})^{\beta}\right)\right)

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\left(\Pi_{\pm}(D)\mathcal{Q}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}b\right)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\left(\Pi_{\pm}(D)\left(\nabla\times((\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}})\times b)\right)\right)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}\left((\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}})\cdot\nabla b^{\beta}-(\partial_{\gamma}(\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}})^{\alpha})b^{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle=(\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}})\cdot\nabla b_{\pm}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+\mathchoice{[\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{[\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{[\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{[\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}},(\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}})\cdot\nabla](b_{\pm}+b_{\mp})^{\beta}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}-\mathchoice{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}}(\partial_{\beta}(\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}})^{\gamma})(b_{\pm}+b_{\mp})^{\beta},

which completes the proof. ∎

4.
Analysis of bicharacteristics for linearized equations

In this section, we assume that
{\bf B}_{0}
is a smooth, time-independent and divergence-free vector field on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. In particular, it need not be a solution to (
E-MHD
).

4.1.
Basic properties of the bicharacteristic flow

The Hamiltonian flow equation associated to the scalar symbol
p_{{\bf B}_{0}}(x,\xi)
is

\left\{\begin{aligned} \dot{X}^{\alpha}=&\partial_{\xi_{\alpha}}p_{{\bf B}_{0}}(X,\Xi)={\bf B}_{0}^{\beta}(X)|{\Xi}|\left(\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}+\frac{\Xi_{\beta}\Xi_{\alpha}}{|{\Xi}|^{2}}\right),\\
\dot{\Xi}_{\alpha}=&-\partial_{x^{\alpha}}p_{{\bf B}_{0}}(X,\Xi)=-\partial_{\alpha}{\bf B}_{0}^{\beta}(X)\Xi_{\beta}|{\Xi}|,\end{aligned}\right.

(4.1)

where
\alpha=1,2,3
. An integral curve
(X,\Xi)(t)
of this flow is called a
bicharacteristic
; its projection
X(t)
to
M
is called a
projected characteristic curve
.

We begin by identifying the possible set of directions of
X(t)
in relation to
{\bf B}_{0}(X(t))
.

Lemma 4.1

(Cone of directions around
{\bf B}_{0}
)
.

Along any bicharacteristic
(X,\Xi)(t)
associated with
\pm p_{{\bf B}_{0}}
, we have

|{\angle(\dot{X}(t),\pm{\bf B}_{0}(X(t)))}|\leq\tan^{-1}\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}.

(4.2)

In particular, we have

|{{\bf B}_{0}(X(t))}||{\Xi(t)}|\leq|{\dot{X}(t)}|.

(4.3)

Moreover, if
|{{\bf B}_{0}-{\bf e}_{3}}|<\frac{1}{2}
, then

2\max\{|{\dot{X}^{1}(t)}|,|{\dot{X}^{2}(t)}|\}\leq|{\Xi(t)}|\leq{12}|{\dot{X}^{3}(t)}|.

(4.4)

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we fix the sign
\pm p_{{\bf B}_{0}}=+p_{{\bf B}_{0}}
and consider
t=0
. Let
(x,\xi)=(X,\Xi)(0)
. Since the case
{\bf B}_{0}(x)=0
is trivial, we may assume that
{\bf B}_{0}(x)\neq 0
. Rotate the (local) coordinate axes so that
{\bf B}_{0}(x)
is aligned with
{\bf e}_{3}=\partial_{x^{3}}
; by a further rotation about the (new)
x^{3}
-axis, we may also set
\xi_{1}=0
. Clearly,
|{\angle(\dot{X}(0),{\bf B}_{0}(x))}|<\frac{\pi}{2}
and

\tan\angle(\dot{X}(0),{\bf B}_{0}(x))=\frac{\dot{X}^{2}(0)}{\dot{X}^{3}(0)}=\frac{\xi_{2}\xi_{3}}{\xi_{2}^{2}+2\xi_{3}^{2}}.

By symmetry, it suffices to compute the maximum of the last expression. By elementary calculus, we have

\max\frac{\xi_{2}\xi_{3}}{\xi_{2}^{2}+2\xi_{3}^{2}}=\max\frac{(\xi_{3}/\xi_{2})}{1+2(\xi_{3}/\xi_{2})^{2}}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}},

where the maximum occurs at
\xi_{3}/\xi_{2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}
. Next, (
4.3
) follows by the computation

|{{\bf B}_{0}(x)}|^{2}|{\xi}|^{2}\leq|{{\bf B}_{0}(x)}|^{2}|{\xi}|^{2}\frac{(\xi_{2}^{2}+2\xi_{3}^{2})^{2}+\xi_{2}^{2}\xi_{3}^{2}}{|{\xi}|^{2}}=|{\dot{X}(0)}|^{2}.

Finally, if
|{{\bf B}_{0}(x)-{\bf e}_{3}}|<\frac{1}{2}
, then

|{\xi}|\leq 2(1+{\bf B}_{0}^{3}(x))|{\xi}|\frac{|{\xi}|^{2}+\xi_{3}^{2}}{|{\xi}|^{2}}{\leq 6{\bf B}_{0}^{3}(x)|{\xi}|\frac{|{\xi}|^{2}+\xi_{3}^{2}}{|{\xi}|^{2}}\leq 12\dot{X}^{3}(0)},

which proves the second inequality of (
4.4
). The first inequality of (
4.4
) is straightforward to prove. ∎

Next, we derive an evolution equation for the magnitude of the canonical momentum
\Xi
.

Lemma 4.2

(Formula for
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|{\Xi}|
)
.

Along any bicharacteristic
(X,\Xi)(t)
associated with
\pm p_{{\bf B}_{0}}
,

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|{\Xi}|=\mp{}^{({\bf B}_{0})}\pi^{\alpha\beta}(X)\Xi_{\alpha}\Xi_{\beta},

(4.5)

where
{}^{({\bf B}_{0})}\pi^{\alpha\beta}=\frac{1}{2}(\partial^{\alpha}{\bf B}_{0}^{\beta}+\partial^{\beta}{\bf B}_{0}^{\alpha})
.

Proof.

For simplicity, we fix the sign
\pm p_{{\bf B}_{0}}=+p_{{\bf B}_{0}}
. Using the Hamiltonian flow equation, we compute

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|{\Xi}|^{2}=2\delta^{\alpha\beta}\Xi_{\alpha}\dot{\Xi}_{\beta}=-2\delta^{\alpha\beta}\xi_{\alpha}\partial_{\beta}{\bf B}_{0}^{\gamma}(X)\Xi_{\gamma}|{\Xi}|=-(\nabla^{\alpha}{\bf B}_{0}^{\gamma}+\nabla^{\gamma}{\bf B}_{0}^{\alpha})(X)\Xi_{\alpha}\Xi_{\gamma}|{\Xi}|.

Recalling the definition
{}^{({\bf B}_{0})}\pi^{\alpha\beta}=\frac{1}{2}(\nabla^{\alpha}{\bf B}_{0}^{\beta}+\nabla^{\beta}{\bf B}_{0}^{\alpha})
, (
4.5
) follows.
∎

4.2.
Quantification of the assumptions

We are now ready to make the qualitative hypotheses in Theorem
1.1
quantitative – these will be the precise parameters on which the lower bound on the lifespan
T
depends. The following definition is central to the precise formulation of our local wellposedness result.

Definition 4.3

(Quantification of asymptotic uniformity, nondegeneracy and nontrapping)
.

Let
s>\frac{7}{2}
and
R\geq 1
. Furthermore, let
\epsilon>0
satisfy
\epsilon<\min\{\epsilon_{0},\alpha_{1}M\}
where
\epsilon_{0},\alpha_{1}>0
are some absolute constants that will be fixed later. We say that
{\bf B}_{0}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
belongs to the space
\mathcal{B}^{s}_{\epsilon}(M,\mu,A,R,L)
if the following holds:

(1)

Size bound.

\|{{\bf B}_{0}-{\bf e}_{3}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s}}<M,

(4.6)

(2)

Nondegeneracy.

\inf_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|{{\bf B}_{0}}|>\mu,

(4.7)

(3)

Takeuchi–Mizohota-type condition.
For every nontrivial bicharacteristic
(X,\Xi)(t)
associated with
p_{{\bf B}_{0}}
, we have

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|{\nabla{\bf B}_{0}(X(t))}||{\Xi(t)}|\,\mathrm{d}t<A,

(4.8)

(4)

\epsilon
-asymptotic uniformity.

\displaystyle\|{\chi_{>R}(|{x^{3}}|)({\bf B}_{0}-{\bf e}_{3})}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s}}<\epsilon,

(4.9)

(5)

R
-nontrapping.
For every nontrivial bicharacteristic
(X,\Xi)(t)
associated with
p_{{\bf B}_{0}}
, we have

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{\{-2R<x^{3}<2R\}}(X(t))|{\dot{X}(t)}|\,\mathrm{d}t<L.

(4.10)

Remark 4.4

(On the definition of
\mathcal{B}^{s}_{\epsilon}(M,\mu,A,R,L)
)
.

Note that
M
,
\mu
and
A
are independent of the choice of
\epsilon
, whereas
R
and
L
depend on
\epsilon
. Observe also that
\mu
,
A
,
R
and
L
are
not
simply controlled in terms of the size bound
M
in (
4.6
) unless
M
is sufficiently small. For this reason, we interpret these parameters as quantifying various properties of
{\bf B}_{0}
in the large data regime. In our precise formulation of the main theorem, we will fix
\epsilon
depending on
M
,
\mu
and
A
, and the time of existence
T
will depend only on the (resulting) parameters
s
,
M
,
\mu
,
A
,
R
,
L
for the initial data
{\bf B}_{0}
.

Up to a choice of a small parameter
\epsilon
, Definition
4.3
makes the qualitative assumptions for
{\bf B}_{0}
in Theorem
1.1
quantitative, as the following lemma and corollary show.

Lemma 4.5

(Qualitative assumptions imply quantitative assumptions)
.

Let
{\bf B}_{0}\in{\bf e}_{3}+\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s}
satisfy (
4.6
) for some
M
, as well as the hypotheses of Theorem
1.1
. Then the following statements hold.

(1)

There exists
\mu>0
such that (
4.7
) holds.

(2)

Given any
\epsilon>0
there exist
R
and
L
such that (
4.9
) and (
4.10
) hold.

(3)

There exists a constant
\epsilon_{0}=\epsilon_{0}(s)>0
such that if (
4.9
) and (
4.10
) hold with
\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}
, then

\displaystyle\|{\chi_{>R}(|{x^{3}}|)({\bf B}_{0}-{\bf e}_{3})}\|_{L^{1}_{x^{3}}L^{\infty}_{x^{1},x^{2}}\cap L^{\infty}}+\|{\chi_{>R}(|{x^{3}}|)\nabla{\bf B}_{0}}\|_{L^{1}_{x^{3}}L^{\infty}_{x^{1},x^{2}}}+\|{\chi_{>R}(|{x^{3}}|)\nabla^{2}{\bf B}_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}}<\frac{1}{100},

(4.11)

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|{\nabla{\bf B}_{0}(X(t))}||{\Xi(t)}|\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim M(1+\mu^{-1}L).

(4.12)

Corollary 4.6
.

Let
{\bf B}_{0}\in{\bf e}_{3}+\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s}
satisfy (
4.6
) for some
M
, as well as the hypotheses of Theorem
1.1
. Then the following statements hold.

(1)

There exist
\mu,A>0
such that (
4.7
) and (
4.8
) hold.

(2)

Given any
\epsilon>0
, there exist
R,L>0
such that
{\bf B}_{0}\in\mathcal{B}^{s}_{\epsilon}(M,\mu,A,R,L)
.

We omit the proof of the corollary, which is obvious.

Proof of Lemma
4.5
.

Statements (1) and (2) follow from
\limsup_{R\to\infty}\|{\chi_{>R}(x^{3})({\bf B}_{0}-{\bf e}_{3})}\|_{\ell^{1}H^{s}}=0
(in view of (
4.6
)), as well as the nondegeneracy and the nontrapping assumptions. For Statement (3), we choose
\epsilon_{0}
sufficiently small compared to the constant in the Sobolev embedding
H^{s-2}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}
so that (
4.11
) holds. To establish (
4.12
), note that

\displaystyle{\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{\{-R\leq x^{3}\leq R\}}(X(t))}|{\nabla{\bf B}_{0}(X(t))}||{\Xi(t)}|\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\mu^{-1}M{\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{\{-R\leq x^{3}\leq R\}}(X(t))}|{\dot{X}(t)}|\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\mu^{-1}ML.

where we used (
4.3
) in the first inequality. On the other hand, in
\{x^{3}>{R}\}
, we use (
4.4
) (which is possible thanks to (
4.11
)) to estimate

\displaystyle{\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{\{R<x^{3}\}}(X(t))}|{\nabla{\bf B}_{0}(X(t))}||{\Xi(t)}|\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}:I\cap(R,\infty)\neq\emptyset}\int_{I}\|{\nabla{\bf B}_{0}(x^{1},x^{2},x^{3})}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x^{1},x^{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{3}\lesssim M.

The region
\{x^{3}<{-R}\}
is treated similarly, which completes the proof of (
4.12
).
∎

We also record some consequences of
{\bf B}_{0}\in\mathcal{B}^{s}_{\epsilon}(M,\mu,A,R,L)
.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
s>\frac{7}{2}
,
M>0
,
\mu>0
,
\epsilon>0
,
R>0
,
L>0
and
{\bf B}_{0}\in\mathcal{B}^{s}_{\epsilon}(M,\mu,A,R,L)
. Provided that
\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}
(where
\epsilon_{0}
is as in Lemma
4.5
.(3)), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t,t^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{|{\Xi(t)}|}{|{\Xi(t^{\prime})}|}\lesssim A,

(4.13)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{12}|{\Xi(t)}|\leq|{\dot{X}^{3}(t)}|\leq|{\dot{X}(t)}|\leq 2|{\Xi(t)}|\qquad\hbox{ if }X^{3}(t)>R\hbox{ or }X^{3}(t)<-R,

(4.14)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|{\dot{X}(t^{\prime})}|\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\begin{cases}X^{3}(t)-X^{3}(0)&\hbox{ if }X^{3}(0)>R\hbox{ or }X^{3}(t)<-R,\\
L+(|{X^{3}(t)}|-R)_{+}+(|{X^{3}(0)}|-R)_{+}&\hbox{ otherwise},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\lesssim L+(|{X^{3}(0)}|-R)_{+}+(|{X^{3}(t)}|-R)_{+}.

(4.15)

Proof.

Estimate (
4.13
) follows from Lemma
4.2
and (
4.8
). Estimate (
4.14
) follows from Lemma
4.1
and (
4.11
). To establish (
4.15
), note that
X(t^{\prime})
for
0\leq t^{\prime}\leq t
does not intersect
\{-R<x^{3}<R\}
if
X^{3}(0)>R
or
X^{3}(t)<-R
. Now (
4.15
) follows from (
4.14
) and (
4.10
). ∎

Importantly, the class
\mathcal{B}^{s}_{\epsilon}(M,\mu,A,R,L)
is stable under small perturbations of
{\bf B}_{0}
in
\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s}
.

Proposition 4.8

(
\mathcal{B}^{s}_{\epsilon}(M,\mu,A,R,L)
is stable)
.

Let
s>\frac{7}{2}
,
M>0
,
\mu>0
,
A>0
,
\epsilon>0
,
R\geq 1
,
L>0
and
\overline{{\bf B}}_{0}\in\mathcal{B}^{s}_{\epsilon}(M,\mu,A,R,L)
. Then the following holds:

(1)

\mathcal{B}^{s}_{\epsilon}(M,\mu,A,R,L)
is an open subset of
\mathcal{B}^{s}(M)
.

(2)

Moreover, there exist
c(s,M,\mu,A),C(s,M,\mu)>0
and if
\epsilon<\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{0}
(where
\epsilon_{0}
is as in Lemma
4.5
) and
{\bf B}_{0}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfies

\|{{\bf B}_{0}-\overline{{\bf B}}_{0}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s}}<c(s,M,\mu,A,\epsilon)e^{-C(s,M,\mu,A)L}

then
{\bf B}_{0}\in\mathcal{B}^{s}_{2\epsilon}(2M,\frac{1}{2}\mu,2{\max}\{A,M\},R,2L)
.

We defer the proof of this proposition until the end of Section
4.3
, as we need to develop some tools for controlling the variation of the bicharacteristics when perturbing
{\bf B}_{0}
.

We also formulate and prove a lemma that will be needed in the proof of Theorem
5.1
(in particular, persistence of regularity).

Lemma 4.9
.

For
s>\frac{7}{2}
,
0<\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}
(where
\epsilon_{0}
is as in Lemma
4.5
.(3)) and
M,\mu,A,R,L>0
, suppose that
{\bf B}\in C_{t}([0,T];\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s})
and
{\bf B}(t)\in\mathcal{B}^{s}_{\epsilon}(M,\mu,A,R,L)
for all
t\in[0,T]
. Then for any
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
, there exist
R^{\prime},L^{\prime}>0
, which are independent of
t
, such that
{\bf B}(t)\in\mathcal{B}^{s}_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(M,\mu,A,R^{\prime},L^{\prime})
for all
t\in[0,T]
.

Proof.

The case
\epsilon^{\prime}\geq\epsilon
is trivial, so it suffices to assume that
\epsilon^{\prime}<\epsilon
. The existence of a
t
-independent
R^{\prime}
such that (
4.9
) holds (with
\epsilon^{\prime}
and
R^{\prime}
) for all
t\in[0,T]
follows from the continuity of the function
\|{\chi_{>R}(x^{3}){\bf B}(t)}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s}}
in
R
and
t
. Then, in view of Lemma
4.7
, it follows that
L^{\prime}=L+C(R^{\prime}-R)_{+}
for some
C>0
works. ∎

4.3.
Variation of bicharacteristics

Here we study the linearization of (
4.1
) with forcing terms
F,G
:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\begin{pmatrix}X^{\prime}\\
\Xi^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\nabla_{x}^{\top}\nabla_{\xi}p_{{\bf B}_{0}}&\nabla_{\xi}^{\top}\nabla_{\xi}p_{{\bf B}_{0}}\\
-\nabla_{x}^{\top}\nabla_{x}p_{{\bf B}_{0}}&-\nabla_{\xi}^{\top}\nabla_{x}p_{{\bf B}_{0}}\end{pmatrix}_{(x,\xi)=(X,\Xi)}\begin{pmatrix}X^{\prime}\\
\Xi^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}F\\
G\end{pmatrix}

(4.16)

Given a bicharacteristic
(X,\Xi)
, we need to analyze (
4.16
) to control the nearby bicharacteristics. The following simple Grönwall estimate for (
4.16
) will be sufficient for our purpose.

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
{\bf B}_{0}\in\mathcal{B}_{\epsilon}^{s}(M,\mu,A,R,L)
for
s>\frac{7}{2}
. We have

\displaystyle|{X^{\prime}(t)}|+\frac{1}{|{\Xi(0)}|}|{\Xi^{\prime}(t)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(|{X^{\prime}(0)}|+\frac{1}{|{\Xi(0)}|}|{\Xi^{\prime}(0)}|+\int_{0}^{t}\left(|{F(t^{\prime})}|+\frac{1}{|{\Xi(0)}|}|{G(t^{\prime})}|\right)\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\right)

(4.17)

\displaystyle\times\exp\left(C\mu^{-1}MA\int_{0}^{t}|{\dot{X}(t^{\prime})}|\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\right).

Proof.

In view of Lemma
4.2
and (
4.8
), note that

\sup_{t,t^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{|{\Xi(t)}|}{|{\Xi(t^{\prime})}|}\lesssim A.

(4.18)

Let
\lambda=|{\Xi(0)}|
. We rescale (
4.16
) in the following way:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\begin{pmatrix}X^{\prime}\\
\frac{1}{\lambda}\Xi^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{\lambda}\nabla_{x}^{\top}\nabla_{\xi}p_{{\bf B}_{0}}&\nabla_{\xi}^{\top}\nabla_{\xi}p_{{\bf B}_{0}}\\
-\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\nabla_{x}^{\top}\nabla_{x}p_{{\bf B}_{0}}&-\frac{1}{\lambda}\nabla_{\xi}^{\top}\nabla_{x}p_{{\bf B}_{0}}\end{pmatrix}_{(x,\xi)=(X,\Xi)}\begin{pmatrix}X^{\prime}\\
\frac{1}{\lambda}\Xi^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\begin{pmatrix}F\\
\frac{1}{\lambda}G\end{pmatrix}.

(4.19)

In view of (
4.18
), the coefficient matrix of this ODE is bounded by
C\lambda^{-1}MA|{\Xi(t)}|
. It follows by Grönwall’s inequality that

\displaystyle|{X^{\prime}(t)}|+\frac{1}{|{\Xi(0)}|}|{\Xi^{\prime}(t)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(|{X^{\prime}(0)}|+\frac{1}{|{\Xi(0)}|}|{\Xi^{\prime}(0)}|+\int_{0}^{t}\left(|{F(t^{\prime})}|+\frac{1}{|{\Xi(0)}|}|{G(t^{\prime})}|\right)\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{\lesssim}\times\exp\left(CMA\int_{0}^{t}|{\Xi(t^{\prime})}|\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\right).

Using
|{\Xi(t^{\prime})}|\leq\mu^{-1}|{\dot{X}(t^{\prime})}|
, the desired inequality follows. ∎

Using the preceding lemma, we obtain the following result concerning the variation of bicharacteristics under the change of the initial conditions.

Proposition 4.11
.

Let
{\bf B}_{0}\in\mathcal{B}^{s}_{\epsilon}(M,\mu,A,R,L)
for
s>\frac{7}{2}
. Given
(x,\xi)\in T^{\ast}\mathbb{R}^{3}
, denote by
(X(t;x,\xi),\Xi(t;x,\xi))
the bicharacteristic (i.e., the solution to (
4.1
)) with the initial conditions

(X(0;x,\xi),\Xi(0;x,\xi))=(x,\xi).

Then the following statements hold.

(1)

We have

\displaystyle|{\partial_{x}X(t;x,\xi)}|+|{\xi}||{\partial_{\xi}X(t;x,\xi)}|+|{\xi}|^{-1}|{\partial_{x}\Xi(t;x,\xi)}|+|{\partial_{\xi}\Xi(t;x,\xi)}|

(4.20)

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\left(C\mu^{-1}MA(L+(|{X^{3}(t)}|-R)_{+}+(|{X^{3}(0)}|-R)_{+})\right).

(2)

Assume, in addition, that
{\bf B}_{0}=P_{<k_{0}}{\bf B}_{0}
. Then we have

\displaystyle|{\xi}|^{|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|}|{\partial_{x}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\xi}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}X(t;x,\xi)}|+|{\xi}|^{|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|-1}|{\partial_{x}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\xi}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\Xi(t;x,\xi)}|

(4.21)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}2^{(|{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}|-1)_{+}k_{0}}A^{(|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|-1)_{+}}\exp\left(C_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mu^{-1}MA(L+(|{X^{3}(t)}|-R)_{+}+(|{X^{3}(0)}|-R)_{+})\right).

Proof.

Observe that
(\partial_{x^{j}}X(t;x,\xi),\partial_{x^{j}}\Xi(t;x,\xi))
solves (
4.16
) with

(X^{\prime}(0),\Xi^{\prime}(0),F,G)=({\bf e}_{j},0,0,0).

Similarly,
(\partial_{\xi_{j}}X(t;x,\xi),\partial_{\xi_{j}}\Xi(t;x,\xi))
solves (
4.16
) with

(X^{\prime}(0),\Xi^{\prime}(0),F,G)=(0,{\bf e}_{j},0,0).

Hence (
4.20
) follows from Lemma
4.10
and (
4.15
). Note that (
4.20
) is simply (
4.21
) with
|{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}|+|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|=1
. We summarize the proof of (
4.21
) in the case
|{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}|+|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|=2
; the general case follows from induction. Note that
(\partial_{x}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\xi}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}X,\partial_{x}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\xi}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\Xi)
solves (
4.16
) with

(\partial_{x}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\xi}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}X(0),\partial_{x}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\xi}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\Xi(0),F,G)=(0,0,F_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}},G_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}),

for some
F_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}},G_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}
, where the initial conditions vanish since
\partial_{x}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\xi}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}x=\partial_{x}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\xi}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\xi=0
if
|{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}|+|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|>1
. Moreover, as in the proof of Lemma
4.10
, we have, for
|{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}}|+|{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}}|\geq 2
,

\displaystyle|{\xi}|^{|{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}}|-2}|{\partial_{x}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}}\partial_{\xi}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}}p_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{0}}}(X,\Xi)}|\lesssim_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}}2^{(|{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}}|-2)_{+}k_{0}}A^{(|{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}}|-2)_{+}}A^{2}M.

(4.22)

When
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=2
and
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=0
,
|F_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}|
and
|\xi|^{-1}|G_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}|
are bounded by

\begin{split}|F_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}|&\lesssim|\nabla_{x}^{2}\nabla_{\xi}p(X,\Xi)||\nabla_{x}X|^{2}+|\xi||\nabla_{x}\nabla_{\xi}^{2}p(X,\Xi)||\nabla_{x}X|(|\xi|^{-1}|\nabla_{x}\Xi|)+|\xi|^{2}|\nabla_{\xi}^{3}p(X,\Xi)|(|\xi|^{-1}|\nabla_{x}\Xi|)^{2}\end{split}

and

\begin{split}|\xi|^{-1}|G_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}|&\lesssim|\xi|^{-1}|\nabla_{x}^{3}p(X,\Xi)||\nabla_{x}X|^{2}+|\nabla_{x}^{2}\nabla_{\xi}p(X,\Xi)||\nabla_{x}X|(|\xi|^{-1}|\nabla_{x}\Xi|)+|\xi||\nabla_{\xi}^{2}\nabla_{x}p(X,\Xi)|(|\xi|^{-1}|\nabla_{x}\Xi|)^{2}\end{split}

where
p=p_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{0}}}
. Then, we simply apply (
4.20
) to bound
|\nabla_{x}X|,|\xi|^{-1}|\nabla_{x}\Xi|
and (
4.22
) to bound the derivatives of
p
. This gives

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{t}\left(|F_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}|+|\xi|^{-1}|G_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}|\right)\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\lesssim_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}2^{k_{0}}\mu^{-1}MA(L+(|{X^{3}(t)}|-R)_{+}+(|{X^{3}(0)}|-R)_{+}),\end{split}

which gives (
4.21
) after applying Lemma
4.10
in this case.
The other cases of
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=1
and
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=0,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=2
can be handled similarly.
∎

We are also ready to give a proof of Proposition
4.8
.

Proof of Proposition
4.8
.

We need to establish the five properties listed in Definition
4.3
for
{\bf B}_{0}
, under the assumption
\|{{\bf B}_{0}-\overline{{\bf B}}_{0}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s}}<\delta
with some choice of
\delta=c(s,M,\mu,A,\epsilon)e^{-C(s,M,\nu,A)L}>0
.

To begin with, (
4.6
) is trivial for
\delta<M
and (
4.7
) is immediate for
\delta<\mu/2
from the embedding
\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s}\subset L^{\infty}
. Similarly, (
4.9
) follows from taking
\delta\leq c(R)\epsilon
. From now on, we shall assume that
\delta
is sufficiently small with respect to
(M,\mu,R,\epsilon)
, so that in particular (
4.6
), (
4.7
) and (
4.9
) holds.

Proof of (
4.10
)
. We now proceed to prove (
4.10
), by taking
\delta
even smaller. We fix some
(x,\xi)
with
\xi\neq 0
and
-2R<x^{3}<2R
. The latter can be assumed (by a translation of
t
variable) without loss of generality since if either
x^{3}\geq 2R
or
x^{3}\leq-2R
,
\dot{X}^{3}\gtrsim 1
as long as
X^{3}(t)\geq 2R
or
X^{3}(t)\leq-2R
holds, respectively. Here
X(t),\Xi(t)
is the bicharacteristic curve corresponding to
\overline{{\bf B}_{0}}
, and we shall take the time interval
\{t_{0}<t<t_{1}\}
where
X^{3}(t_{0})=-2R,X^{3}(t_{1})=2R
. Note that
t_{0}<0<t_{1}
.

Let us introduce
{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}:=\sigma{\bf B}_{0}+(1-\sigma)\overline{{\bf B}}_{0}
and denote
(X^{(\sigma)},\Xi^{(\sigma)})(t;x,\xi)
the bicharacteristics with initial conditions
(x,\xi)
associated with
{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}
. For simplicity, let us write
(\bar{X},\bar{\Xi})=(X^{(0)},\Xi^{(0)})(t;x,\xi)
. Then
(X^{(\sigma)\prime},\Xi^{(\sigma)\prime}):=\partial_{\sigma}(X^{(\sigma)},\Xi^{(\sigma)})
obeys the equation

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\begin{pmatrix}X^{(\sigma)\prime}\\
\Xi^{(\sigma)\prime}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\nabla_{x}^{\top}\nabla_{\xi}p_{{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}}&\nabla_{\xi}^{\top}\nabla_{\xi}p_{{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}}\\
-\nabla_{x}^{\top}\nabla_{x}p_{{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}}&-\nabla_{\xi}^{\top}\nabla_{x}p_{{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}}\end{pmatrix}_{(x,\xi)=(X^{(\sigma)},\Xi^{(\sigma)})}\begin{pmatrix}X^{(\sigma)\prime}\\
\Xi^{(\sigma)\prime}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}\nabla_{\xi}p_{{\bf B}_{0}-\overline{{\bf B}}_{0}}\\
-\nabla_{x}p_{{\bf B}_{0}-\overline{{\bf B}}_{0}}\end{pmatrix}_{(x,\xi)=(X^{(\sigma)},\Xi^{(\sigma)})}

with zero initial conditions. We are going to restrict the variable
t
to
[t_{0},t_{1}]
and estimate
(X^{(\sigma)\prime},\Xi^{(\sigma)\prime})
, under the following
bootstrap assumptions
:

\begin{split}\sup_{\sigma\in[0,1]}\sup_{t:|\bar{X}^{3}(t)|<2R}|X^{\sigma}(t)-\bar{X}(t)|\leq\min\left\{\frac{L}{100},{\frac{1}{4}}\right\},\qquad\sup_{\sigma\in[0,1]}\sup_{t:|\bar{X}^{3}(t)|<2R}\frac{|\Xi^{\sigma}(t)|}{|\bar{\Xi}(t)|}\leq 10.\end{split}

(4.23)

From our assumption
R\geq 1
in Definition
4.3
, (
4.23
) implies in particular that
|X^{\sigma}(t)-\bar{X}(t)|\leq R/4
.
We now proceed as in the proof of Lemma
4.10
. To begin with, we estimate the coefficient matrix, after rescaling the equation as in (
4.19
). Writing for simplicity
p=p_{{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}}(t,X^{(\sigma)},\Xi^{(\sigma)})
, we obtain under (
4.23
) that

\begin{split}|\nabla_{\xi}^{2}p|+|\xi|^{-2}|\nabla^{2}_{x}p|+|\xi|^{-1}|\nabla_{x}\nabla_{\xi}p|\leq 100C_{0}AM|\xi|^{-1}|\bar{\Xi}|.\end{split}

Then, to estimate the forcing terms, we write for simplicity
p_{{\bf B}_{0}-\overline{{\bf B}}_{0}}=p_{{\bf B}_{0}-\overline{{\bf B}}_{0}}(t,X^{(\sigma)},\Xi^{(\sigma)})
and obtain that

\begin{split}|\nabla_{\xi}p_{{\bf B}_{0}-\overline{{\bf B}}_{0}}|\leq C_{1}\delta|\Xi^{(\sigma)}|\leq 10C_{1}\delta|\bar{\Xi}|,\qquad\int_{0}^{t}|\nabla_{\xi}p_{{\bf B}_{0}-\overline{{\bf B}}_{0}}|\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\leq 20C_{1}\delta\mu^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}|\dot{\bar{X}}|\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}.\end{split}

Similarly, using
|\Xi^{(\sigma)}|\leq 10|\bar{\Xi}|
and
|\xi|^{-1}|\bar{\Xi}|\leq A
, we obtain

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{t}|\nabla_{x}p_{{\bf B}_{0}-\overline{{\bf B}}_{0}}|\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\leq 100C_{2}\delta A\mu^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}|\dot{\bar{X}}|\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}.\end{split}

With these bounds, following the proof of Lemma
4.10
gives

\begin{split}|X^{(\sigma)\prime}|+|\xi|^{-1}|\Xi^{(\sigma)\prime}|\leq C(20C_{1}+100C_{2})A\delta\mu^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}|\dot{\bar{X}}|\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\,\exp\left(100CC_{0}AM\mu^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}|\dot{\bar{X}}|\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\right)=:\delta^{*}.\end{split}

(4.24)

By taking
\delta
small in the form

\begin{split}\delta\leq c_{0}\mu(AL(A+L^{-1}))^{-1}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{c_{0}}AM\mu^{-1}L\right),\end{split}

we may obtain in particular that
|X^{(\sigma)\prime}|+|\xi|^{-1}|\Xi^{(\sigma)\prime}|\leq\min\{\frac{1}{100CA},\frac{L}{200}\}
, where
C>0
is the implicit constant from the estimate (
4.13
). This gives for all
0<\sigma\leq 1
that

\begin{split}|\Xi^{(\sigma)}-\bar{\Xi}|\leq|\xi|\int_{0}^{\sigma}|\xi|^{-1}|\Xi^{(\sigma^{\prime})\prime}|\,\mathrm{d}\sigma^{\prime}\leq\frac{|\xi|}{100CA}\leq\frac{|\bar{\Xi}|}{2}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\frac{|\bar{\Xi}|}{2}\leq|\Xi^{(\sigma)}|\leq\frac{3|\bar{\Xi}|}{2}.\end{split}

(4.25)

This in particular justifies the bootstrap assumption (
4.23
) for
\Xi^{(\sigma)}
. Similarly, we have

\begin{split}|X^{(\sigma)}-\bar{X}|\leq\int_{0}^{\sigma}|X^{(\sigma^{\prime})\prime}|\,\mathrm{d}\sigma^{\prime}\leq\min\left\{\frac{L}{200},\frac{1}{100CA}\right\}\end{split}

(4.26)

and this justifies (
4.23
) for
X^{(\sigma)}-\bar{X}
, since
A\geq\frac{1}{C}
follows from (
4.13
). The estimates (
4.25
), (
4.26
) are valid as long as
t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
.

Now we split the integral (
4.10
) for
\sigma=1
as

\left[\int_{-\infty}^{t_{0}}+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}+\int_{t_{1}}^{\infty}\right]\mathbf{1}_{\{-2R<x^{3}<2R\}}(X^{(1),3}(t))|{\dot{X}^{(1)}(t)}|\,\mathrm{d}t.

(4.27)

The last integral in
\{t\geq t_{1}\}
(which may be empty) can be easily estimated as follows. We know at
t=t_{1}
that
X^{(1),3}\geq 2R-\frac{R}{2}>R
and therefore we have that for
t\geq t_{1}
,
\dot{X}^{(1),3}\geq 20|\dot{X}^{(1)}|
. But then

\begin{split}\int_{t_{1}}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{\{-2R<x^{3}<2R\}}(X^{(1)}(t))|{\dot{X}^{(1),3}(t)}|\,\mathrm{d}t\leq 20\int_{2R-\frac{L}{200}<X^{3}(t)<2R}\dot{X}^{3}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t<\frac{L}{4}.\end{split}

The integral in
t\in(-\infty,t_{0})
can be estimated in the same manner. Finally, to estimate the second integral, we write

\begin{split}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\mathbf{1}_{\{-2R<x^{3}<2R\}}(X^{(1),3}(t))|{\dot{X}^{(1)}(t)}|\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\int_{0}^{1}\left[\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}|{\dot{X}^{(\sigma)\prime}(t)}|\,\mathrm{d}t\right]\mathrm{d}\sigma.\end{split}

(4.28)

To estimate the RHS of (
4.28
), we return to the equation for
\dot{X}^{(\sigma)\prime}
:

\begin{split}\dot{X}^{(\sigma)\prime}=\nabla_{x}^{\top}\nabla_{\xi}p_{{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}}(X^{(\sigma)},\Xi^{(\sigma)})X^{(\sigma)\prime}+\nabla_{\xi}^{\top}\nabla_{\xi}p_{{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}}(X^{(\sigma)},\Xi^{(\sigma)})\Xi^{(\sigma)\prime}+\nabla_{\xi}p_{{\bf B}_{0}-\overline{{\bf B}}_{0}}(X^{(\sigma)},\Xi^{(\sigma)}).\end{split}

We note that the last term is bounded by

\begin{split}|\nabla_{\xi}p_{{\bf B}_{0}-\overline{{\bf B}}_{0}}(X^{(\sigma)},\Xi^{(\sigma)})|\leq C\delta|\Xi^{(\sigma)}|\leq C\mu^{-1}\delta|\dot{X}^{(\sigma)}|.\end{split}

For the other terms, using (
4.24
) and (
4.25
) we obtain that

\begin{split}|\nabla_{x}^{\top}\nabla_{\xi}p_{{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}}(X^{(\sigma)},\Xi^{(\sigma)})X^{(\sigma)\prime}|\leq CM\mu^{-1}\delta^{*}|\dot{X}^{(\sigma)}|\end{split}

\begin{split}|\nabla_{\xi}^{\top}\nabla_{\xi}p_{{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}}(X^{(\sigma)},\Xi^{(\sigma)})\Xi^{(\sigma)\prime}|\leq CM|\xi|\delta^{*}\leq CMA\mu^{-1}\delta^{*}|\dot{X}^{(\sigma)}|.\end{split}

By taking
\delta
smaller depending on
\mu,M,A
if necessary (this makes
\delta^{*}
smaller as well), we have

\begin{split}|\dot{X}^{(\sigma)\prime}|\leq\frac{1}{10}|\dot{X}^{(\sigma)}|.\end{split}

This allows us to write

\begin{split}I(\sigma):=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}|{\dot{X}^{(\sigma)}(t)}|\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}|{\dot{X}^{(0)}(t)}|\,\mathrm{d}t+\frac{1}{10}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{0}^{\sigma}|\dot{X}^{(\sigma^{\prime})}(t)|\,\mathrm{d}\sigma^{\prime}\mathrm{d}t<L+\frac{1}{10}\int_{0}^{\sigma}I(\sigma^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}\sigma^{\prime}.\end{split}

Note that
I(0)<L
. We may bootstrap the hypothesis
I(\sigma)<\frac{5}{4}L
; indeed, if we assume that it holds for some
\sigma\in[0,\sigma_{0})
, then

\begin{split}I(\sigma_{0})<L+\frac{1}{10}\sigma_{0}\frac{5}{4}L\leq\frac{9}{8}L.\end{split}

Therefore, we have that
I(1)<\frac{5}{4}L
, which allows us to conclude that

\begin{split}\left[\int_{-\infty}^{t_{0}}+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}+\int_{t_{1}}^{\infty}\right]\mathbf{1}_{\{-2R<x^{3}<2R\}}(X^{(1),3}(t))|{\dot{X}^{(1)}(t)}|\,\mathrm{d}t<2L.\end{split}

Proof of (
4.8
)
. We use the notation from above; fix some
(x,\xi)
and denote
(X^{(\sigma)}(t),\Xi^{(\sigma)}(t))
be the bicharacteristic curve corresponding to
{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}
. The goal is to prove

\begin{split}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|\nabla{\bf B}_{0}^{(1)}(X^{(1)}(t))||\Xi^{(1)}(t)|\,\mathrm{d}t<2\max\{A,M\}.\end{split}

(4.29)

In the region
\{t_{1}<t\}
(the region
\{t<t_{0}\}
can be handled similarly), we proceed as in the proof of Lemma
4.5
; using (
4.4
), we can bound

\displaystyle{\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{\{R<x^{3}\}}(X^{(1)}(t))}|{\nabla{\bf B}^{(1)}_{0}(X^{(1)}(t))}||{\Xi^{(1)}(t)}|\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}:I\cap(R,\infty)\neq\emptyset}\int_{I}\|{\nabla{\bf B}^{(1)}_{0}(x^{1},x^{2},x^{3})}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x^{1},x^{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{3}\lesssim\epsilon.

(4.30)

Therefore, by taking a small absolute constant
\alpha_{1}>0
(recall
\epsilon\leq\alpha_{1}M
from Definition
4.3
), we can guarantee that

\begin{split}\int_{(-\infty,t_{0}]\cup[t_{1},\infty)}|\nabla{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}(X^{(\sigma)}(t))||\Xi^{(\sigma)}(t)|\,\mathrm{d}t\leq M.\end{split}

(4.31)

To handle the integral in the region
\{t_{0}\leq t\leq t_{1}\}
, we write

\begin{split}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\sigma}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}|\nabla{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}(X^{(\sigma)}(t))||\Xi^{(\sigma)}(t)|\,\mathrm{d}t=I+II+III,\end{split}

where

\begin{split}I=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}|\nabla{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}(X^{(\sigma)}(t))|\frac{\Xi^{(\sigma)}(t)\cdot\Xi^{(\sigma)\prime}(t)}{|\Xi^{(\sigma)}(t)|}\,\mathrm{d}t,\end{split}

\begin{split}II=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\frac{\nabla{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}(X^{(\sigma)}(t)):\nabla({\bf B}_{0}-\overline{{\bf B}}_{0})(X^{(\sigma)}(t))}{|\nabla{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}(X^{(\sigma)}(t))|}|\Xi^{(\sigma)}(t)|\,\mathrm{d}t,\end{split}

\begin{split}III=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\frac{1}{2}\frac{X^{(\sigma)\prime}(t)\cdot\nabla|\nabla{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}(X^{(\sigma)}(t))|^{2}}{|\nabla{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}(X^{(\sigma)}(t))|}|\Xi^{(\sigma)}(t)|\,\mathrm{d}t.\end{split}

We may bound each term similarly as in the proof of (
4.12
) from Lemma
4.5
, using (
4.24
). We begin with

\begin{split}|II|\lesssim\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}|\nabla({\bf B}_{0}-\overline{{\bf B}}_{0})(X^{(\sigma)}(t))||\Xi^{(\sigma)}(t)|\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\frac{\delta}{\mu}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}|{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}(X^{(\sigma)}(t))||\Xi^{(\sigma)}(t)|\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\frac{\delta}{\mu}L.\end{split}

Next, from (
4.24
) we obtain
|\Xi^{(\sigma)\prime}(t)|\lesssim A\delta^{*}|\Xi^{(\sigma)}(t)|
, which gives

\begin{split}|I|\lesssim\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}|\nabla{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}(X^{(\sigma)}(t))||\Xi^{(\sigma)\prime}(t)|\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim A\delta^{*}\frac{M}{\mu}L.\end{split}

Lastly, using (
4.24
) we bound

\begin{split}|III|\lesssim\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}|X^{(\sigma)\prime}(t)||\nabla^{2}{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}(X^{(\sigma)}(t))||\Xi^{(\sigma)}(t)|\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\frac{M}{\mu}\delta^{*}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}|{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}(X^{(\sigma)}(t))||\Xi^{(\sigma)}(t)|\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\delta^{*}\frac{M}{\mu}L.\end{split}

Therefore, by taking
\delta
smaller (which makes
\delta^{*}
smaller as well),

\begin{split}\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\sigma}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}|\nabla{\bf B}_{0}^{(\sigma)}(X^{(\sigma)}(t))||\Xi^{(\sigma)}(t)|\,\mathrm{d}t\right|\leq\frac{A}{2},\end{split}

which together with (
4.31
) gives (
4.29
).
This finishes the proof.
∎

5.
Precise formulation and proof of the main theorem

5.1.
Precise formulation of the local wellposedness result

We are now ready to give a precise formulation of the main local wellposedness result.

Theorem 5.1

(Main theorem)
.

Given
s>\frac{7}{2}
,
M>0
,
\mu>0
and
A>0
, there exist positive constants
\epsilon=\epsilon(s,M,\mu,A)
,
c(s,M,\mu,A)
and
C(s,M,\mu,A)
such that the following holds. Consider a vector field
{\bf B}_{0}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfying
\nabla\cdot{\bf B}_{0}=0
. Assume furthermore that

{\bf B}_{0}\in\mathcal{B}^{s}_{\epsilon}(M,\mu,A,R,L)

for some
R,L>0
. Then the Cauchy problem for (
E-MHD
) with
{\bf B}(t=0)={\bf B}_{0}
is locally wellposed on
[0,T]
, where

T=c(s,M,\mu,A)\exp(-C(s,M,\mu,A)L),

in the sense that the following holds:

(1)

Existence and uniqueness.
There exists a unique solution
{\bf B}
with
{\bf B}-{\bf e}_{3}\in\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}[0,T]
.

(2)

Continuous dependence.
The solution map
{\bf B}_{0}\mapsto{\bf B}
is continuous as a map
\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s}\to\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}[0,T]
;

(3)

Weak Lipschitz dependence.
The solution map
{\bf B}_{0}\mapsto{\bf B}
is Lipschitz continuous as a map
\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{2}\to\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{0}[0,T]
;

(4)

Persistence of regularity.
If
{\bf B}_{0}-{\bf e}_{3}\in\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s+m}
for
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, then
{\bf B}-{\bf e}_{3}\in\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s+m}[0,T]
.

(5)

Frequency envelope bound.
Let
c_{k}
be a
\delta_{1}
-admissible frequency envelope
(i.e.,
\{c_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
is a sequence of positive numbers obeying
\max\left\{c_{k}/c_{j},c_{j}/c_{k}\right\}\leq 2^{\delta_{1}|{k-j}|}
) with
0<\delta_{1}<s
. If
\|{P_{k}({\bf B}_{0}-{\bf e}_{3})}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s}}\leq c_{k}
for
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, then
\|{P_{k}({\bf B}-{\bf e}_{3})}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}}\lesssim c_{k}
for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Here,
\delta_{1}
is a universal small positive constant to be determined in the proof below.

As a direct consequence of the above, we have local wellposedness for small initial data.

Corollary 5.2
.

Given
s>\frac{7}{2}
, there exist positive constants
\varepsilon^{*}=\varepsilon^{*}(s)
and
T=T(s)
such that the following holds. For
{\bf B}_{0}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfying
\nabla\cdot{\bf B}_{0}=0
and
\|{{\bf B}_{0}-{\bf e}_{3}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s}}<\varepsilon^{*}
, the Cauchy problem for (
E-MHD
) with
{\bf B}(t=0)={\bf B}_{0}
is locally wellposed on
[0,T]
, in the same sense of Theorem
5.1
.

To obtain Corollary
5.2
from Theorem
5.1
, it suffices to note that for
{\bf B}_{0}
satisfying
\|{{\bf B}_{0}-{\bf e}_{3}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s}}<\varepsilon^{*}
, we have
{\bf B}_{0}\in\mathcal{B}^{s}_{\epsilon}(M,\mu,A,R,L)
with
M=1,\mu=1/2,A=1,R=1,L=10
as long as
\varepsilon^{*}
is taken to be sufficiently small. Then
T>0
is provided by Theorem
5.1
.

5.2.
Paralinearization of (
E-MHD
) and the resulting error

A basic ingredient of the proof is a wellposedness theorem for the
paralinearization of (
3.1
)
, which refers to the following system:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}b-\nabla\times(T_{{\bf B}}\times(\nabla\times b))+\nabla\times(T_{\nabla\times{\bf B}}\times b)

\displaystyle=g,

(5.1)

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot b=\nabla\cdot g

\displaystyle=0,

(5.2)

where

\displaystyle\nabla\times(T_{{\bf B}}\times(\nabla\times b))

\displaystyle=\sum_{k}\nabla\times(P_{<k-10}{\bf B}\times(\nabla\times P_{k}b)),

\displaystyle\nabla\times(T_{\nabla\times{\bf B}}\times b)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k}\nabla\times((\nabla\times P_{<k-10}{\bf B})\times P_{k}b).

We also introduce the
paralinearization error
,

G(b)=\nabla\times(b\times(\nabla\times b))-\nabla\times(T_{b}\times(\nabla\times b))+\nabla\times(T_{\nabla\times b}\times b).

(5.3)

As its name suggests,
G(b)
is precisely the error term that arises when writing (
E-MHD
) (or (
3.1
)) in the form (
5.1
)–(
5.2
) (we omit the straightforward proof):

Lemma 5.3
.

If
{\bf B}
solves (
E-MHD
) on
(0,T)
, then
b:={\bf B}-{\bf e}_{3}
solves (
5.1
)–(
5.2
) with
{\bf B}={\bf e}_{3}+b
and
g=G(b)
on
(0,T)
, and vice versa.

The following two results will be proved in Section
7
:

Proposition 5.4

(Wellposedness of the paralinearized system)
.

Let
s_{1}>\frac{7}{2}
and
0<T\leq 1
. Assume that, for some
M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A,\epsilon_{1},R,L>0
, we have

\displaystyle{\bf B}(0)\in\mathcal{B}^{s_{1}}_{\epsilon_{1}}(M_{1},\mu,A,R,L),

(5.4)

\displaystyle\|{{\bf B}-{\bf e}_{3}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s_{1}}[0,T]}\leq M_{1},

(5.5)

\displaystyle{\|{\partial_{t}{\bf B}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{\infty}H^{s_{1}-2}[0,T]}\leq M_{1}^{\prime}.}

(5.6)

Let
b
and
g
satisfy (
5.1
)–(
5.2
) on
(0,T)
. Then, for any
\sigma\geq 0
, the following holds: if

\epsilon_{1}\leq\epsilon_{(1)}(\sigma,s_{1},M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A),\quad T\leq c_{(1)}(\sigma,s_{1},M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A)\exp(-C_{(1)}(\sigma,s_{1},M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A)L)

then we have

\|{b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{\sigma}[0,T]}\leq C_{(2)}(\sigma,s_{1},M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A)\exp(C_{(2)}(\sigma,s_{1},M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A)L)\left(\|{b_{0}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{\sigma}}+\|{g}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}Y^{\sigma}[0,T]}\right).

(5.7)

Proposition 5.5

(Bounds for the paralinearization error)
.

Let
s_{1}>\frac{7}{2}
and
b,\overline{b}\in\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s_{1}}[0,T]
with

\|{b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s_{1}}[0,T]}<M_{1},\qquad\|{\overline{b}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s_{1}}[0,T]}<M_{1}.

Then for any
\sigma\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\|{G(b)}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}Y^{\sigma}[0,T]}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\sigma,s_{1}}TM_{1}\|{b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{\sigma}[0,T]},

(5.8)

\displaystyle\|{G(b)-G(\overline{b})}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}Y^{\sigma}[0,T]}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\sigma,s_{1}}TM_{1}\|{b-\overline{b}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{\sigma}[0,T]}.

(5.9)

Moreover, for
0\leq\sigma<s_{1}-1
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\nabla\times(T_{b-\overline{b}}\times(\nabla\times b))-\nabla\times(T_{\nabla\times(b-\overline{b})}\times b)}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}Y^{\sigma}}\lesssim_{s_{1}-1-\sigma}T^{\frac{s_{1}-1-\sigma}{s_{1}-1}}M_{1}\|{b-\overline{b}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{\sigma}[0,T]}.

(5.10)

Finally, we also have the fixed time bounds

\|{\nabla\times(T_{b}\times(\nabla\times b))(t)}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s_{1}-2}}+\|{\nabla\times(T_{\nabla\times b}\times b)(t)}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s_{1}-2}}+\|{G(b(t))}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s_{1}-2}}\lesssim_{s_{1}}M_{1}^{2}.

(5.11)

In the next subsection, we will utilize these propositions to prove Theorem
5.1
.

5.3.
Proof of local wellposedness

We now show how Theorem
5.1
follows from Propositions
5.4
and
5.5
. We follow the streamlined approach of
[
54
,
55
,
56
]
(see also
[
39
]
), which does not rely on Nash–Moser or viscosity method.

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

Throughout the proof, we will assume that

{\bf B}_{0}\in\mathcal{B}^{s}_{\epsilon}(M,\mu,A,R,L),\quad T\leq c(s,M,\mu,A)\exp(-C(s,M,\mu,A)L),

(5.12)

where
c(s,M,\mu,A)
and
C(s,M,\mu,A)
, as well as
\epsilon(s,M,\mu,A)
, are to be fixed at the end of Step 2 below.

Step 0: Setup for the proof of existence.
The proof of existence is based on an iteration argument. We begin with
b^{(-1)}=0
. With
{\bf B}^{(n-1)}:=b^{(n-1)}+{\bf e}_{3}
, we define
b^{(n)}
by solving

\displaystyle\partial_{t}b^{(n)}-\nabla\times(T_{{\bf B}^{(n-1)}}\times(\nabla\times b^{(n)}))+\nabla\times(T_{\nabla\times{\bf B}^{(n-1)}}\times b^{(n)})

\displaystyle=G(b^{(n-1)})

(5.13)

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot b^{(n)}

\displaystyle=0,

with the initial conditions
b^{(n)}(0)={\bf B}_{0}-{\bf e}_{3}
.

Note that the difference between two consecutive iterates solves the following equation:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(b^{(n+1)}-b^{(n)})-\nabla\times(T_{{\bf B}^{(n)}}\times(\nabla\times(b^{(n+1)}-b^{(n)})))+\nabla\times(T_{\nabla\times{\bf B}^{(n)}}\times(b^{(n+1)}-b^{(n)}))

(5.14)

\displaystyle=G(b^{(n-1)})-G(b^{(n)})+\nabla\times(T_{b^{(n)}-b^{(n-1)}}\times(\nabla\times b^{(n)}))-\nabla\times(T_{\nabla\times(b^{(n)}-b^{(n-1)})}\times b^{(n)}).

Step 1: Iteration.
Fix a choice of
s_{1}=s_{1}(s)
such that
\frac{7}{2}<s_{1}<s
(e.g.,
s_{1}=\frac{1}{2}(\frac{7}{2}+s)
), and construct a sequence of iterates as above. We choose
M_{1}=C_{1}M
,
\epsilon_{1}=C_{1}\epsilon
, and
M_{1}^{\prime}=C_{1}^{2}M(1+M)
with
C_{1}=C_{1}(s)
a suitably large constant (to be fixed at the end of this step). In particular, by taking
C_{1}
to be sufficiently large, we may ensure that (
5.4
) holds. We also take

c(s,M,\mu,A)\leq c_{(1)}(s,s_{1},M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A),\quad C(s,M,\mu,A){\geq}C_{(1)}(s,s_{1},M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A),

(5.15)

where
c_{(1)}
and
C_{(1)}
are from Proposition
5.4
.

We will propagate the following bounds by induction on
n
:

(1)

\|{{\bf B}^{(n)}-{\bf e}_{3}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s_{1}}[0,T]}\leq M_{1}
.

(2)

\|{{\bf B}^{(n)}-{\bf e}_{3}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}[0,T]}\leq 2C_{(2)}(s,s_{1},M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A)e^{C_{(2)}(s,s_{1},M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A)L}M
.

(3)

\|{\partial_{t}{\bf B}^{(n)}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{\infty}H^{s_{1}-2}[0,T]}\leq M_{1}^{\prime}
.

Indeed, these bounds trivially hold for
{\bf B}^{(-1)}={\bf B}_{0}
. Now, assuming that (1), (2) and (3) hold for
{\bf B}^{(n)}
, we will show that
{\bf B}^{(n+1)}
obeys the same bounds as well. In view of (
5.12
) and (
5.15
), we may apply Propositions
5.4
and
5.5
to (
5.13
) for
b^{(n+1)}={\bf B}^{(n+1)}-{\bf e}_{3}
. We obtain

\|{{\bf B}^{(n+1)}-{\bf e}_{3}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}[0,T]}\leq C_{(2)}(s,s_{1},M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A)e^{C_{(2)}(s,s_{1},M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A)L}(M+C(s,s_{1})TM_{1}M),

where
C(s,s_{1})
is a constant arising from applying Proposition
5.5
.
Taking
c
to be even smaller (so that
T
is small), we may ensure that (2) holds. To establish (1) and (3), we begin by noting that

\|{\partial_{t}{\bf B}^{(n+1)}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{\infty}H^{s_{1}-2}[0,T]}\lesssim(1+M_{1})\|{{\bf B}^{(n+1)}-{\bf e}_{3}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s_{1}}[0,T]}+M_{1}^{2}

by (
5.11
) (in Proposition
5.5
), the induction hypothesis and the simple algebraic computation

\displaystyle\nabla\times(T_{{\bf B}^{(n)}}\times(\nabla\times b^{(n+1)}))-\nabla\times(T_{\nabla\times{\bf B}^{(n)}}\times b^{(n+1)})

\displaystyle=\nabla\times({\bf e}_{3}\times(\nabla\times b^{(n+1)}))+\nabla\times(T_{b^{(n)}}\times(\nabla\times b^{(n+1)}))-\nabla\times(T_{\nabla\times b^{(n)}}\times b^{(n+1)}).

Splitting

\displaystyle{\bf B}^{(n+1)}-{\bf e}_{3}

\displaystyle=P_{<k}({\bf B}^{(n+1)}-{\bf e}_{3})+P_{\geq k}({\bf B}^{(n+1)}-{\bf e}_{3})

\displaystyle=P_{<k}({\bf B}_{0}-{\bf e}_{3})+\int_{0}^{t}P_{<k}\partial_{t}{\bf B}^{(n+1)}(t^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}+P_{\geq k}({\bf B}^{(n+1)}-{\bf e}_{3}),

then applying the preceding bounds and (
2.12
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|{{\bf B}^{(n+1)}-{\bf e}_{3}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s_{1}}[0,T]}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{P_{<k}({\bf B}_{0}-{\bf e}_{0})+\int_{0}^{t}P_{<k}\partial_{t}{\bf B}^{(n+1)}(t^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}}\right\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s_{1}}[0,T]}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+\|{P_{\geq k}({\bf B}^{(n+1)}-{\bf e}_{3})}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s_{1}}[0,T]}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+T^{\frac{1}{2}}2^{\frac{k}{2}})\left\|{P_{<k}({\bf B}_{0}-{\bf e}_{0})}\right\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s_{1}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+(1+T^{\frac{1}{2}}2^{\frac{k}{2}})T2^{2k}\left((1+M_{1})\|{{\bf B}^{(n+1)}-{\bf e}_{3}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s_{1}}[0,T]}+M_{1}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+2^{-(s-s_{1})k}2C_{(2)}(s,s_{1},M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A)e^{C_{(2)}(s,s_{1},M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A)L}M.

Note that
s-s_{1}>0
by our choice. Taking
k
sufficiently large (depending on
s,M,\mu,A
, to handle the term on the last line) and
c(s,M,\mu,A)
smaller (so that
T
is small depending on
k
), and
C_{1}(s)
sufficiently large (so that
M_{1}
and
M_{1}^{\prime}
are large compared to
M
and
M(1+M)
, respectively), we may ensure that (1) and (3) hold.

Next, we establish the convergence of
{\bf B}^{(n)}=b^{(n)}+{\bf e}_{3}
. Indeed, applying Propositions
5.4
and
5.5
to the difference equation (
5.14
) with
\sigma=0
, we obtain

\|{b^{(n+1)}-b^{(n)}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{0}[0,T]}<C_{(2)}(s,s_{1},M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A)e^{C_{(2)}(s,s_{1},M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A)L}C(s,s_{1})TM_{1}\|{b^{(n)}-b^{(n-1)}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{0}[0,T]}.

Taking
c_{(1)}
sufficiently small (so that
T
is small), we may ensure that
{\bf B}^{(n)}=b^{(n)}+{\bf e}_{3}
is a Cauchy sequence in
\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{0}[0,T]
; let us denote the limit by
{\bf B}
. Observing that (1) and (2) hold for the limit
P_{<k}({\bf B}-{\bf e}_{3})
for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, it follows that
{\bf B}-{\bf e}_{3}\in\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}[0,T]
with the bounds (1) and (2). Moreover,
b={\bf B}-{\bf e}_{3}
solves

\displaystyle\partial_{t}b-\nabla\times(T_{{\bf B}}\times(\nabla\times b))+\nabla\times(T_{\nabla\times{\bf B}}\times b)

\displaystyle=G(b)

(5.16)

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot b

\displaystyle=0,

with the initial condition
b(0)={\bf B}_{0}-{\bf e}_{3}
, as desired.

We end this step by noting that, taking
c(s,M,\mu,A)
sufficiently small (so that
T
is small), we have

{\bf B}(t)\in\mathcal{M}^{s_{1}}_{2\epsilon_{1}}(2M_{1},\tfrac{1}{2}\mu,2\max\{A,M_{1}\},R,2L)\quad\hbox{ for all }t\in[0,T].

(5.17)

To see this, fix
t\in[0,T]
. From the splitting

\displaystyle{\bf B}^{(n+1)}-{\bf B}_{0}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}P_{<k}\partial_{t}{\bf B}^{(n+1)}(t^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}+P_{\geq k}({\bf B}^{(n+1)}-{\bf e}_{3})-P_{\geq k}({\bf B}_{0}-{\bf e}_{3}),

we may apply (2) and (3) for
{\bf B}^{(n+1)}
, as well as (
5.12
), to bound

\displaystyle\|{{\bf B}^{(n+1)}(t)-{\bf B}_{0}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s_{1}}[0,T]}\lesssim T2^{2k}M_{1}^{\prime}+2^{-(s-s_{1})k}\left(2C_{(2)}(s,s_{1},M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A)e^{C_{(2)}(s,s_{1},M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A)L}M+M\right).

Taking
k
sufficiently large (depending on
s,M,\mu,A
) and
c(s,M,\mu,A)
small enough (depending on
k
, so that
T
is small), we may apply Proposition
4.8
to conclude (
5.17
).

Step 2: Uniqueness and weak Lipschitz dependence.

Let
{\bf B}
be the solution constructed in Step 1, and let
\overline{{\bf B}}\in\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}[0,T]
be a (possibly different) solution to the initial value problem. We will show that, in fact,
{\bf B}=\overline{{\bf B}}
.

Introducing the shorthands
b={\bf B}-{\bf e}_{3}
and
\overline{b}=\overline{{\bf B}}-{\bf e}_{3}
, note that the difference
b-\overline{b}
solves

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\overline{b}-b)-\nabla\times(T_{{\bf B}}\times(\nabla\times(\overline{b}-b)))+\nabla\times(T_{\nabla\times{\bf B}}\times(\overline{b}-b))

(5.18)

\displaystyle=G(\overline{b})-G(b)+\nabla\times(T_{\overline{b}-b}\times(\nabla\times\overline{b}))-\nabla\times(T_{\nabla\times(\overline{b}-b)}\times\overline{b}).

Let
0\leq t_{0}<t_{1}\leq T
. In view of (
5.17
) at
t=t_{0}
, Propositions
5.4
and
5.5
are applicable to the difference equation (
5.18
) on
[t_{0},t_{1}]
with
\sigma=0
(after making
\epsilon(s,M,\mu,A)
,
c(s,M,\mu,A)
and
C(s,M,\mu,A)^{-1}
smaller if necessary). We obtain

\displaystyle\|{\overline{b}-b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{0}[t_{0},t_{1}]}

\displaystyle\leq C_{(2)}(0,s_{1},M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A)e^{C_{(2)}(0,s_{1},M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu,A)L}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{\leq}}\times(\|{(\overline{b}-b)(t_{0})}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{2}}+(t_{1}-t_{0})C(0,s)(\|{b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}[0,T]}+\|{\overline{b}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}[0,T]})\|{\overline{b}-b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{0}[t_{0},t_{1}]}).

Provided that
b(t_{0})=\overline{b}(t_{0})
and
t_{1}-t_{0}
is sufficiently small (depending not only on
s,M,\mu,A
, but also on
\|{b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}[0,T]}+\|{\overline{b}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}[0,T]}
), it follows that
{\bf B}=\overline{{\bf B}}
on
[t_{0},t_{1}]
. Then from a straightforward continuous induction argument, it follows that
{\bf B}=\overline{{\bf B}}
on
[0,T]
.

By the uniqueness statement that we just established, it follows that any
\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}[0,T]
solution to the initial value problem coincides with the one constructed in Step 1, and thus obeys (1) and (2). Given two such solutions, the weak Lipschitz dependence assertion follows from the preceding difference bound on
[0,T]
.

We point out that, at this point, our choices of
\epsilon(s,M,\mu,A)
,
c(s,M,\mu,A)
and
C(s,M,\mu,A)
are final.

Step 3: Persistence of regularity.

Applying Lemma
4.9
, which is possibly in view of (
5.17
), given any
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
we may find
R^{\prime},L^{\prime}>0
such that

{\bf B}(t)\in\mathcal{B}^{s_{1}}_{\epsilon_{1}^{\prime}}(M_{2},\mu_{2},M_{2},R^{\prime},L^{\prime})\quad\hbox{ for all }t\in[0,T],

where
\mu_{2}=\tfrac{1}{2}\mu
and
M_{2}=2\max\{A,M_{1}\}
.

Let
0\leq t_{0}<t_{1}\leq T
. By essentially
8
8

8

Pedantically, one must repeat Steps 1–2 to construct a solution in
\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{\sigma}
and conclude that it coincides with
{\bf B}
by uniqueness. We omit the details.
applying Propositions
5.4
and
5.5
to (
5.16
) on
[t_{0},t_{1}]
, which is possible if we take
\epsilon^{\prime}
and
t_{1}-t_{0}
sufficiently small depending (in particular) on
\sigma
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|{b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{\sigma}[t_{0},t_{1}]}

\displaystyle\leq C_{(2)}(\sigma,s_{1},M_{2},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu_{2},M_{2})e^{C_{(2)}(\sigma,s_{1},M_{2},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu_{2},M_{2})L^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{\leq}}\times(\|{(b(t_{0})}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{\sigma}}+(t_{1}-t_{0})C(\sigma,s_{1})M_{1}\|{b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{\sigma}[t_{0},t_{1}]}).

Provided that
t_{1}-t_{0}
is sufficiently small depending on
\sigma,s_{1},M_{2},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu_{2},M_{2}
(or equivalently,
\sigma,s,M,\mu,A
), we may absorb the contribution of the last term in the parentheses and conclude that

\displaystyle\|{b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{\sigma}[t_{0},t_{1}]}\leq 2C_{(2)}(\sigma,s_{1},M_{2},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu_{2},M_{2})e^{C_{(2)}(\sigma,s_{1},M_{2},M_{1}^{\prime},\mu_{2},M_{2})L^{\prime}}\|{(b(t_{0})}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{\sigma}}.

Applying this bound repeatedly in time until we exhaust
[0,T]
, the persistence of regularity bound follows.

Step 4: Frequency envelope bound.
The idea is to combine Steps 2 and 3. We remark that while we do not keep track of the dependence of constants on
s,M,\mu,A,\epsilon,R,L
in this step and below, it may be easily recovered by a closer inspection of the argument.

Let
\delta
be any positive constant bigger than
\delta_{1}
. For each
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, denote by
{\bf B}_{<k}
the solution to (
E-MHD
) with
{\bf B}_{<k}(0)=P_{<k}{\bf B}_{0}
, and write
b_{<k}={\bf B}_{<k}-{\bf e}_{3}
. We also introduce the convention
b_{<-1}={\bf B}_{<-1}-{\bf e}_{3}=0
. By Steps 2 and 3, we have

\displaystyle\|{b_{<k}-b_{<k-1}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{P_{k-1}({\bf B}_{0}-{\bf e}_{3})}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-sk}c_{k},

\displaystyle\|{b_{<k}-b_{<k-1}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s+\delta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{P_{<k}({\bf B}_{0}-{\bf e}_{3})}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s+\delta}}\lesssim\sum_{0\leq k^{\prime}<k}2^{\delta k^{\prime}}c_{k^{\prime}}\lesssim 2^{\delta k}c_{k}.

for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, with the convention
P_{-1}=P_{<0}
. Here, we have used Step 2 for the first bound, while Step 3, the triangle inequality and the
\delta_{1}
-admissibility condition have been used in the last inequality. Writing

\displaystyle P_{k_{0}}({\bf B}-{\bf e}_{3})=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}P_{k_{0}}(b_{<k}-b_{<k-1})

it follows that

\displaystyle\|{P_{k_{0}}({\bf B}-{\bf e}_{3})}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}}\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\min\{2^{s(k_{0}-k)},2^{\delta(k-k_{0})}\}c_{k}\lesssim c_{k_{0}},

where we again used the
\delta_{1}
-admissibility condition in the last inequality.

Step 5: Continuous dependence.
The idea is to combine Steps 2 and 4. Given a convergent sequence
{\bf B}^{(n)}_{0}\to{\bf B}_{0}
in
\ell^{1}H^{s}
, observe that there exist
\delta_{1}
-admissible frequency envelopes
c_{k}^{(n)}
,
c_{k}
for
{\bf B}^{(n)}_{0},{\bf B}_{0}
in
\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s}
with the following common upper bounds:

\sup_{n}\sum_{k}(c_{k}^{(n)})^{2}+\sum_{k}c_{k}^{2}\lesssim\|{{\bf B}_{0}}\|_{\ell^{1}H^{s}}^{2},\quad,

and for every
\epsilon>0
, there exists
K\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
such that

\sup_{n}\sum_{k\geq K}(c_{k}^{(n)})^{2}+\sum_{k\geq K}c_{k}^{2}<\epsilon^{2}.

By the preceding arguments, these initial data sets give rise to solutions
{\bf B}^{(n)}
to the initial value problem on a common interval
[0,T]
(we may have to restrict to
n
large enough if necessary). Moreover, given any
\epsilon>0
, we use the frequency envelope bound in Step 4 to find
K\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
such that

\|{P_{\geq K}{\bf B}^{(n)}}\|_{\ell^{1}X^{s}[0,T]}<\frac{\epsilon}{3},\quad\|{P_{\geq K}{\bf B}}\|_{\ell^{1}X^{s}[0,T]}<\frac{\epsilon}{3}

uniformly in
n
. On the other hand, by Step 2, it follows that

\displaystyle\|{P_{<K}({\bf B}^{(n)}-{\bf B})}\|_{\ell^{1}X^{s}[0,T]}\lesssim 2^{sK}\|{P_{<K}({\bf B}^{(n)}-{\bf B})}\|_{\ell^{1}X^{0}[0,T]}\lesssim 2^{sK}\|{P_{<K}({\bf B}^{(n)}_{0}-{\bf B}_{0})}\|_{\ell^{1}L^{2}}.

Hence, taking
n
sufficiently large (depending on
k
), we may ensure that
\|{{\bf B}^{(n)}-{\bf B}}\|_{\ell^{1}X^{s}[0,T]}<\epsilon
. This implies the desired continuous dependence assertion. ∎

6.
Linear and multilinear estimates

In this section, we state and prove linear and multilinear estimates on the function spaces defined in Section
2.2
, which will be useful in Section
7
below.

6.1.
Localization property of Littlewood–Paley projections

We begin with a basic property of the Littlewood–Paley projection
P_{k}
with respect to localization (in physical space) on slabs of the form
\{x^{3}\in I\}
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
j,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
1\leq p,q_{1}^{i},q^{i},r\leq\infty
, where
j+k\geq 0
and
q^{i}\leq q_{1}^{i}
(
i=1,2,3
). Then for any
I_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{2j}
and
\{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}\}=\{1,2,3\}
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\chi_{I_{0}}(x^{3})P_{k}f}\|_{L^{p}L^{q_{1}^{i_{1}}}_{x^{i_{1}}}L^{q_{1}^{i_{2}}}_{x^{i_{2}}}L^{q_{1}^{i_{3}}}_{x^{i_{3}}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{k\sum_{i}(\frac{{1}}{q^{i}}-\frac{{1}}{q_{1}^{i}})}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{j}}\frac{1}{\langle{2^{k}\dist(I_{0},I)}\rangle^{10}}\|{\chi_{I}(x^{3})f}\|_{L^{p}L^{q^{i_{1}}}_{x^{i_{1}}}L^{q^{i_{2}}}_{x^{i_{2}}}L^{q^{i_{3}}}_{x^{i_{3}}}}.

(6.1)

Proof.

The kernel for
P_{k}
obeys

|{K_{k}(x-y)}|\lesssim\frac{2^{3k}}{\langle{2^{k}|{x-y}|}\rangle^{100}}.

Thus

\displaystyle|{\chi_{I_{0}}(x)K_{\ell}(x-y)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{j}}\chi_{I_{0}}(x)\frac{2^{3k}}{\langle{2^{k}|{x-y}|}\rangle^{100}}\chi_{I}(y)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{j}}\frac{2^{3k}}{\langle{2^{k}|{x-y}|}\rangle^{30}}\frac{1}{\langle{2^{k}\dist(I_{0},I)}\rangle^{10}}\chi_{I}(y).

Now the desired statement follows from Young’s inequality.
∎

Corollary 6.2
.

Let
j,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
1\leq p,q_{1}^{i},q^{i},r\leq\infty
, where
j+k\geq 0
and
q^{i}\leq q_{1}^{i}
(
i=1,2,3
). Then for any
\{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}\}=\{1,2,3\}
, we have

\displaystyle\|{P_{k}f}\|_{\ell^{r}_{\mathcal{I}}(L^{p}L^{q_{1}^{i_{1}}}_{x^{i_{1}}}L^{q_{1}^{i_{2}}}_{x^{i_{2}}}L^{q_{1}^{i_{3}}}_{x^{i_{3}}})_{j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{k\sum_{i}(\frac{{1}}{q^{i}}-\frac{{1}}{q_{1}^{i}})}\|{f}\|_{\ell^{r}_{\mathcal{I}}(L^{p}L^{q^{i_{1}}}_{x^{i_{1}}}L^{q^{i_{2}}}_{x^{i_{2}}}L^{q^{i_{3}}}_{x^{i_{3}}})_{j}}.

(6.2)

Proof.

This statement is an easy consequence of Lemma
6.1
and Schur’s test applied to the
I
-summations; the conditions
j+k\geq 0
ensures that these sums are uniformly bounded.
∎

6.2.
Pseudodifferential operator on
X
and
Y

Order
0
classical pseudodifferential operators obey the following boundedness properties in
X^{0}
and
Y^{0}
, as well as their
\ell^{r}_{\mathcal{I}}
-modifications.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{a}\in S^{0}
. Then the following statements hold (where
1\leq r\leq\infty
).

(1)

We have

\displaystyle\|{\mathfrak{a}(x,D)b}\|_{X^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};1000}\|{b}\|_{X^{0}},

(6.3)

\displaystyle\|{\mathfrak{a}(x,D)b}\|_{\ell^{r}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};1000}\|{b}\|_{\ell^{r}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{0}},

(6.4)

\displaystyle\|{\mathfrak{a}(x,D)g}\|_{Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};1000}\|{g}\|_{Y^{0}},

(6.5)

\displaystyle\|{\mathfrak{a}(x,D)g}\|_{\ell^{r}_{\mathcal{I}}Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};1000}\|{g}\|_{\ell^{r}_{\mathcal{I}}Y^{0}}.

(6.6)

(2)

For
k_{1}
satisfying

2^{k_{1}}\geq\frac{[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};1000}}{\|{\mathfrak{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}}},

(6.7)

we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathfrak{a}(x,D)P_{\geq k_{1}}b}\|_{X^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|{\mathfrak{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|{b}\|_{X^{0}},

(6.8)

\displaystyle\|{\mathfrak{a}(x,D)P_{\geq k_{1}}b}\|_{\ell^{r}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|{\mathfrak{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|{b}\|_{\ell^{r}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{0}},

(6.9)

\displaystyle\|{\mathfrak{a}(x,D)P_{\geq k_{1}}g}\|_{Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|{\mathfrak{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|{g}\|_{Y^{0}},

(6.10)

\displaystyle\|{\mathfrak{a}(x,D)P_{\geq k_{1}}g}\|_{\ell^{r}_{\mathcal{I}}Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|{\mathfrak{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|{g}\|_{\ell^{r}_{\mathcal{I}}Y^{0}}.

(6.11)

Proof.

Step 1: Initial reduction.

Let
\widetilde{P}_{k}(\xi)
be a rescaled bump function adapted to the annulus
\{|{\xi}|\simeq 2^{k}\}
for
k\geq 1
(resp. the ball
\{|{\xi}|\simeq 1\}
for
k=0
) such that
\widetilde{P}_{k}(\xi)P_{k}(\xi)=P_{k}(\xi)
, and let
\widetilde{P}_{k}=\widetilde{P}_{k}(D)
. We claim that (
6.3
) and (
6.5
) follow from

\displaystyle\|{P_{k_{0}}\mathfrak{a}(x,D)\widetilde{P}_{k}}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-50|{k_{0}-k}|}[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};500},

(6.12)

\displaystyle\sup_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\sup_{I,I_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\ell}}\|{\chi_{I_{0}}(x^{3})P_{k_{0}}\mathfrak{a}(x,D)\widetilde{P}_{k}\chi_{I}(x^{3})}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-50|{k_{0}-k}|}\frac{1}{\langle{2^{k_{\min}}\dist(I_{0},I)}\rangle^{5}}[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};500}.

(6.13)

Indeed, observe first that (
6.12
) immediately implies, for all
k_{0},k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,

\|{P_{k_{0}}\mathfrak{a}(x,D)P_{k}b}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-50|{k_{0}-k}|}[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};500}\|{P_{k}b}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}.

(6.14)

Next, consider
k_{0},k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
I_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\ell_{0}}
with
\ell_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. We would have

\displaystyle 2^{-\frac{\ell_{0}}{2}}\|{\chi_{I_{0}}(x^{3})P_{k_{0}}\mathfrak{a}(x,D)P_{k}b}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\ell_{0}}}2^{-\frac{\ell_{0}}{2}}\|{\chi_{I_{0}}(x^{3})P_{k_{0}}\mathfrak{a}(x,D)\widetilde{P}_{k}(\chi_{I}(x^{3})P_{k}b)}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\ell_{0}}}2^{-\frac{\ell_{0}}{2}}2^{-50|{k_{0}-k}|}\frac{[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};500}}{\langle{2^{-\ell_{0}}\dist(I_{0},I)}\rangle^{5}}\|{\chi_{I}(x^{3})P_{k}b}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-50|{k_{0}-k}|}[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};500}\|{P_{k}b}\|_{LE}.

Note that, in the second inequality, we used (
6.12
) when
\dist(I_{0},I)=0
and (
6.13
) when
\dist(I_{0},I)\gtrsim 2^{\ell_{0}}
(recall also that
2^{k_{\min}}\geq 1\geq 2^{-\ell_{0}}
). Taking the supremum in
I_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\ell_{0}}
and
\ell_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
(and recalling the definition of
\|{\cdot}\|_{LE}
), we obtain

\displaystyle 2^{\frac{k_{0}}{2}}\|{P_{k_{0}}\mathfrak{a}(x,D)P_{k}b}\|_{LE}\lesssim 2^{-50|{k_{0}-k}|}[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};500}\|{P_{k}b}\|_{LE}.

Then combined with (
6.14
) and Schur’s test (for
k_{0}
and
k
), (
6.3
) follows as desired.

To prove (
6.4
), observe that, for any
I_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{k}
