, a minor variant of the above argument leads to

\|{\chi_{I_{0}^{\prime}}(x^{3})P_{k_{0}}\mathfrak{a}(x,D)P_{k}b}\|_{X_{k}}\lesssim\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\frac{[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};500}}{\langle{2^{-k}\dist(I^{\prime},I_{0}^{\prime})}\rangle^{2}}\|{\chi_{I^{\prime}}(x^{3})P_{k}b}\|_{X_{k}}.

Then by Lemma
2.1
, we have

\|{P_{k_{0}}\mathfrak{a}(x,D)P_{k}b}\|_{\ell^{r}_{\mathcal{I}}X_{k_{0}}}\lesssim 2^{\frac{3}{2}|{k_{0}-k}|}\|{P_{k_{0}}\mathfrak{a}(x,D)P_{k}b}\|_{\ell^{r}_{\mathcal{I}}X_{k}}\lesssim 2^{-(50-\frac{3}{2})|{k_{0}-k}|}[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};500}\|{P_{k}b}\|_{\ell^{r}_{\mathcal{I}}X_{k}},

where we used bound and Schur’s test (in
I_{0}^{\prime},I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{k}
) in the second inequality. By Schur’s test in
k,k_{0}
, (
6.4
) follows.

Finally, estimates (
6.5
) and (
6.6
) follows from duality and Proposition
2.8
.

Step 2: Proof of (
6.12
) and (
6.13
).

To complete the proof of (
6.3
)–(
6.6
), it remains to establish (
6.12
) and (
6.13
). By Proposition
2.3
, we have

\|{P_{k_{0}}\mathfrak{a}(x,D)P_{k}}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}\lesssim[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};30}.

(6.15)

Moreover, recall that the integral kernel for
P_{k_{0}}\mathfrak{a}(x,D)P_{k}
is given by

\displaystyle K_{k_{0},k}(x,y)=P_{k_{0}}\int\mathfrak{a}(x,\xi)\widetilde{P}_{k}(\xi)e^{i\xi\cdot(x-y)}\,\frac{\mathrm{d}\xi}{(2\pi)^{d}}.

Using
(x^{j}-y^{j})e^{i\xi\cdot(x-y)}=-i\partial_{\xi_{j}}e^{i\xi\cdot(x-y)}
and integrating by parts in
\xi
, we have, for
2^{k}|{x-y}|>1
,

|{K_{k_{0},k}(x,y)}|\lesssim\frac{2^{3k}}{\langle{2^{k}(x-y)}\rangle^{10}}[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};100}.

It follows that

\sup_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\sup_{I,I_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\ell}}\|{\chi_{I_{0}}(x^{3})P_{k_{0}}\mathfrak{a}(x,D)\widetilde{P}_{k}\chi_{I}(x^{3})}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle{2^{k}\dist(I_{0},I)}\rangle^{5}}[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};100},

(6.16)

where we used (
6.15
) when
2^{k}\dist(I_{0},I)\lesssim 1
and the kernel bound and Young’s inequality when
2^{k}\dist(I_{0},I)\gtrsim 1
. From these two bounds, (
6.12
) and (
6.13
) follow when
|{k-k_{0}}|<10
.

In the remaining case
|{k-k_{0}}|\geq 10
, we need to obtain factors of
2^{-|{k_{0}-k}|}
by exploiting the frequency imbalance. Let
{\mathchoice{\widetilde{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{P}$}}}{\widetilde{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{P}$}}}{\widetilde{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{P}$}}}{\widetilde{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{P}$}}}}_{k}(\xi)
be a rescaled bump function adapted to the annulus
\{|{\xi}|\simeq 2^{k}\}
for
k\geq 1
(resp. the ball
\{|{\xi}|\simeq 1\}
for
k=0
) such that
{\mathchoice{\widetilde{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{P}$}}}{\widetilde{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{P}$}}}{\widetilde{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{P}$}}}{\widetilde{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{P}$}}}}_{k}(\xi)\widetilde{P}_{k}(\xi)=\widetilde{P}_{k}(\xi)
, and let
{\mathchoice{\widetilde{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{P}$}}}{\widetilde{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{P}$}}}{\widetilde{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{P}$}}}{\widetilde{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{P}$}}}}_{k}={\mathchoice{\widetilde{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{P}$}}}{\widetilde{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{P}$}}}{\widetilde{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{P}$}}}{\widetilde{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{P}$}}}}_{k}(D)
. For any
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we may write (in operator notation)

\displaystyle P_{k_{0}}\mathfrak{a}(x,D)\widetilde{P}_{k}

\displaystyle=2^{\pm 2N(k-k_{0})}P_{k}\left[\left(2^{\pm 2Nk_{0}}(1-\Delta)^{\mp N}\widetilde{P}_{k_{0}}\right)\left((1-\Delta)^{\pm N}\mathfrak{a}(x,D)(1-\Delta)^{\mp N}\right)\left(2^{\mp 2Nk}(1-\Delta)^{\pm N}{\mathchoice{\widetilde{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{P}$}}}{\widetilde{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{P}$}}}{\widetilde{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{P}$}}}{\widetilde{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{P}$}}}}_{k}\right)\right]\widetilde{P}_{k}

\displaystyle=:2^{\pm 2N(k-k_{0})}P_{k}\mathfrak{a}_{k_{0},k,N,\pm}\widetilde{P}_{k}

where we choose
\pm=+
when
k<k_{0}
and
\pm=-
when
k_{0}>k
. By Proposition
2.5
.(1), we have
\mathfrak{a}_{k_{0},k,N,\pm}\in S^{0}
with
[\mathfrak{a}_{k_{0},k,N,\pm}]_{S^{0};100}\lesssim[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};500}
. Applying (
6.15
) and (
6.16
) to
\mathfrak{a}_{k_{0},k,N,\pm}
with a suitable
N
(e.g.,
N=55
), the desired estimates (
6.12
) and (
6.13
) follow.

Step 3: Proof of (2).
We now give a proof of (2), which is a small modification of the preceding argument. As in Step 1, the proof of (
6.8
)–(
6.11
) is reduced to the following bounds: for all
k_{0},k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
with
k\geq k_{1}-3
(with
k_{1}
satisfying (
6.7
)),

\displaystyle\|{P_{k_{0}}\mathfrak{a}(x,D)\widetilde{P}_{k}}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-40|{k_{0}-k}|}\|{\mathfrak{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}},

(6.17)

\displaystyle\sup_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\sup_{I,I_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{\ell}}\|{\chi_{I_{0}}(x^{3})P_{k_{0}}\mathfrak{a}(x,D)\widetilde{P}_{k}\chi_{I}(x^{3})}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-40|{k_{0}-k}|}\frac{1}{\langle{2^{k_{\min}}\dist(I_{0},I)}\rangle^{4}}\|{\mathfrak{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

(6.18)

Observe that the symbol
\mathfrak{a}_{k_{0},k,N,\pm}
from Step 2 obeys
\|{\mathfrak{a}_{k_{0},k,N,\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|{\mathfrak{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}}
. Estimate (
6.17
) is proved by applying Lemma
2.10
instead of Proposition
2.3
to
\mathfrak{a}
and
\mathfrak{a}_{k_{0},k,N,\pm}
in the preceding proof of (
6.12
). To prove (
6.18
), note that we only need to consider
I_{0},I\in\mathcal{I}_{\ell}
with
2^{k_{\min}}\dist(I_{0},I)\gtrsim 1
in view of (
6.17
). In this case, by (
6.13
) and
\dist(I_{0},I)\gtrsim 2^{\ell}
(otherwise,
I_{0}\cap I\neq\emptyset
by our choice of
\mathcal{I}_{\ell_{0}}
),

\displaystyle\|{\chi_{I_{0}}(x^{3})P_{k_{0}}\mathfrak{a}(x,D)\widetilde{P}_{k}\chi_{I}(x^{3})}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-50|{k_{0}-k}|}\frac{1}{\langle{2^{k_{\min}}\dist(I_{0},I)}\rangle^{5}}[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};500}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-40|{k_{0}-k}|}\frac{1}{\langle{2^{k_{\min}}\dist(I_{0},I)}\rangle^{4}}2^{-k}2^{-\ell}[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};500}.

Since
2^{\ell}\geq 1
and
2^{k}\gtrsim\|{\mathfrak{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{-1}[\mathfrak{a}]_{S^{0};500}
by (
6.7
) and
k\geq k_{1}-3
, we obtain (
6.18
) as desired. ∎

We also record here a localization property of the norm
\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}
that will be useful in Section
7.5
below, whose proof is a quick application of Propositions
2.5
and
6.3
.

Proposition 6.4
.

For any
R\geq 1
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we have

\|{(1-\chi_{<2R}(x^{3}))P_{<k}b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}}\lesssim\|{(1-\chi_{<R}(x^{3}))b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}}+(2^{k}R)^{-1}\|{\chi_{<R}b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}}

Proof.

Splitting
b=(1-\chi_{<R}(x^{3}))b+\chi_{<R}(x^{3})b
and observing that
(1-\chi_{<2R}(x^{3}))\chi_{<R}(x^{3})=0
, we see that it suffices to establish the following operator bounds

\displaystyle\|{1-\chi_{<2R}(x^{3})}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}\to\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}}\lesssim 1,\quad\|{P_{<k}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}\to\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}}\lesssim_{s}1,\quad\|{[P_{<k},\chi_{<R}(x^{3})]}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}\to\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s}}\lesssim_{s}2^{-k}R^{-1}.

These follow from Propositions
2.5
.(1) and
6.3
.(1). ∎

6.3.
Core product and commutator estimates

In this subsection, we collect the core product and commutator estimates in the function spaces defined in Section
2.2
(cf.
[
54
]
).

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
T>0
, and consider spaces of functions defined on
(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. For any
j,k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, the following product bounds hold:

\displaystyle\|{P_{\ell}(P_{j}aP_{k}b)}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}Y_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\frac{5}{2}k}2^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell-\frac{1}{2}j}\|{P_{j}a}\|_{\ell^{\infty}_{\mathcal{I}}X_{j}}\|{P_{k}b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}{L^{\infty}L^{2}_{k}}},

(6.19)

\displaystyle\|{P_{\ell}(P_{j}aP_{k}b)}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}Y_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T2^{\frac{3}{2}\ell}\|{P_{j}a}\|_{X_{j}}\|{P_{k}b}\|_{X_{k}}.

(6.20)

Finally, provided that
j<k-8
, the following commutator bound holds:

\|{[P_{k},P_{j}a]b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}Y_{k}}\lesssim 2^{\frac{7}{2}j}2^{-2k}\|{P_{j}a}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{\infty}L^{2}_{j}}\|{P_{[k-3,k+3]}b}\|_{\ell^{\infty}_{\mathcal{I}}X_{k}}

(6.21)

Proof.

Estimate (
6.19
) is proved as follows.

\displaystyle\|{P_{\ell}(P_{j}aP_{k}b)}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}Y_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\ell}}2^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell}\|{\chi_{I}(x^{3})P_{\ell}(P_{j}aP_{k}b)}\|_{{LE^{\ast}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell}2^{\frac{1}{2}k}\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|{\chi_{I^{\prime}}(x^{3})P_{\ell}(P_{j}aP_{k}b)}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}

(by (
2.2
))

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell}2^{\frac{1}{2}k}\sup_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|{\widetilde{\chi}_{I^{\prime}}(x^{3})P_{j}a}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|{\chi_{I^{\prime}}(x^{3})P_{k}b}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}}

(Hölder)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell-\frac{1}{2}j}2^{\frac{5}{2}k}\left(2^{\frac{1}{2}j}\|{P_{j}a}\|_{{LE}}\right)\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|{\chi_{I^{\prime}}(x^{3})P_{k}b}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}

(by (
2.1
) and Corollary
6.2
)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell-\frac{1}{2}j}2^{\frac{5}{2}k}\|{P_{j}a}\|_{\ell^{\infty}_{\mathcal{I}}X_{j}}\|{P_{k}b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{\infty}L^{2}_{k}}.

Next, estimate (
6.20
) is proved as follows.

\displaystyle\|{P_{\ell}(P_{j}aP_{k}b)}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}Y_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\ell}}\|{\chi_{I}(x^{3})P_{\ell}(P_{j}aP_{k}b)}\|_{L^{1}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\ell}}2^{\frac{3}{2}\ell}\|{\chi_{I}(x^{3})P_{\ell}(P_{j}aP_{k}b)}\|_{L^{1}L^{1}}

(Corollary
6.2
)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\frac{3}{2}\ell}\|{P_{\ell}(P_{j}aP_{k}b)}\|_{L^{1}L^{1}}

(Fubini)

\displaystyle\lesssim T2^{\frac{3}{2}\ell}\|{P_{j}a}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}\|{P_{k}b}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}

(Hölder)

\displaystyle\lesssim T2^{\frac{3}{2}\ell}\|{P_{j}a}\|_{X_{j}}\|{P_{k}b}\|_{X_{k}}.

Finally, we prove the commutator bound (
6.21
). Since
j<k-8
, by considering the Fourier supports, it follows that
\nabla[P_{k},P_{j}a]b=\nabla[P_{k},P_{j}a]P_{[k-3,k+3]}b
. Then by
[
54
, (3.21)]
, we have the following representation for the commutator:

\displaystyle\nabla[P_{k},P_{j}a]b=P_{[k-5,k+5]}L(\nabla P_{j}a,P_{[k-3,k+3]}b),

where
L
is a translation invariant bilinear operator of the form

L(a,b)(x)=\int a(x-y)b(x-z)w(y,z)\,\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z,

with
\|{w}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 1
(independent of
k,j
). Since the norms
\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}Y_{k}
,
\ell^{1}X_{j}
and
\ell^{\infty}X_{k}
are translation invariant (in the sense that for each of these norms, its translates are equivalent norms), the desired bound follows from (
6.19
) (with
(j,k,\ell)
set to
(k^{\prime},j,k^{\prime\prime})
with
k^{\prime},k^{\prime\prime}=k+O(1)
). ∎

7.
Analysis of the paralinearized system and the paralinearization error

The goal of this section is to establish Propositions
5.4
and
5.5
. In Subsection
7.2
, Proposition
5.4
is reduced to a similar estimate without the
\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}
-summation structure. The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of this reduced statement, except for the last Subsection
7.7
, where Proposition
5.5
is proved.

7.1.
Assumptions, parameters and conventions

7.1.1.
Global assumptions

Throughout this section, with the exception of Section
7.7
(where Proposition
5.5
is proved), we assume that

{\bf B}_{0}:={\bf B}(0)\in\mathcal{B}^{s_{1}}_{\epsilon}(M,\mu,A,R,L),\quad\|{{\bf B}-{\bf e}_{3}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s_{1}}[0,T]}+\|{\partial_{t}{\bf B}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{\infty}H^{s_{1}-2}[0,T]}\leq M,

which are the hypotheses for Proposition
5.4
, except we introduced the shorthand
M:=\max\{M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime}\}
, and we removed the subscript
1
from the other constants for notational convenience. We will assume that
\epsilon_{(1)}
,
c_{(1)}
,
C_{(1)}
,
c_{(2)}
,
C_{(2)}
depend on
M=\{M_{1},M_{1}^{\prime}\}
instead of
M_{1}
and
M_{1}^{\prime}
separately. For simplicity, we also assume that

A\geq M,\quad L\geq\frac{1}{24}R\geq\frac{1}{24}.

Observe, from the statement of Proposition
5.4
, that making the assumption
A\geq M
does not lose any generality. Moreover,
L\gtrsim R
will be guaranteed by our assumptions on
\epsilon
made below, while
R\geq 1
is guaranteed by definition.

We also fix
s_{0}
such that
\frac{7}{2}<s_{0}<s_{1}
(e.g.,
s_{0}=\frac{1}{2}(s_{1}+\frac{7}{2})
) and let
\epsilon_{0}
be the small positive constant from Lemma
4.5
. Given
k_{(0)}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, note that

\|{{\bf B}_{0}-({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s_{0}}}\lesssim 2^{-(s_{1}-s_{0})k_{(0)}}M.

Therefore, in view of Lemma
4.5
and Corollary
4.6
, there exist
L_{0}\geq\frac{1}{24}R_{0}\geq\frac{1}{24}
that depends only on
M
,
\mu
and
A
, but
not
on
\epsilon
or
k_{(0)}
, such that

({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}\in\mathcal{B}_{\epsilon_{0}}^{s_{0}}(2M,\tfrac{1}{2}\mu,2A,R_{0},L_{0}),

(7.1)

for
k_{(0)}
sufficiently large depending on
s_{1}
,
M
,
\mu
,
A
(but not on
\epsilon
or
L
).
Similarly, we also have

({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}\in\mathcal{B}^{s_{0}}_{2\epsilon}(2M,\tfrac{1}{2}\mu,2A,R,2L),

(7.2)

but for
k_{(0)}
sufficiently large depending on
s_{1}
,
M
,
\mu
,
A

as well as

\epsilon
and
L
.

7.1.2.
Parameter choices

We assume that

\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{(1)},\quad T\leq c_{(1)}e^{-C_{(1)}L},

where
\epsilon_{(1)}
,
c_{(1)}
and
C_{(1)}
will be fixed towards the end of the proof – it will be crucial to make these choice depend only on
\sigma
,
s_{1}
,
M
,
\mu
and
A
, but
not
on
R
and
L
(in particular, letting
\epsilon_{(1)}
depend on
R
or
L
would lead to circular logic). Other important parameters are the frequency cutoff parameter
k_{(0)}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, which was already introduced in (
7.1
) and (
7.2
), as well as another frequency cutoff parameters
k_{(1)}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, which will be used in the application of Proposition
6.3
.(2) (high frequency Calderón–Vaillancourt).

The choices of
\epsilon_{(1)}
,
k_{(0)}
,
k_{(1)}
,
c_{(1)}
,
C_{(1)}
,
c_{(2)}
,
C_{(2)}
will be finalized in Section
7.6
, in the order

\epsilon_{(1)}\to k_{(0)}\to k_{(1)}\to c_{(1)},C_{(1)}\to c_{(2)},C_{(2)},

with the following restrictions (as well as others that will arise in the proof below):

•

\epsilon_{(1)}\leq\epsilon_{0}
, so that
\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}
and Lemma
4.7
is applicable (in particular, this assumption assures
L\geq\frac{1}{24}R
by Corollary
4.6
);

•

k_{(0)}
is sufficiently large so that (
7.1
) and (
7.2
) hold;

•

k_{(1)}\geq k_{(0)}
.

7.1.3.
Notation and conventions

Throughout this section,
we suppress the dependence of implicit constants on
s_{1}
,
s_{0}
and
\sigma
.
Furthermore, given
g=(g_{+},g_{-})^{\top}
and a norm
Z
, we use the shorthand
\|{g}\|_{Z}^{2}:=\sum_{\pm}\|{g_{\pm}}\|_{Z}^{2}
. Unless otherwise specified,
all spacetime norms in this section are taken over
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

7.2.
Physical space localization

The goal of this subsection is to reduce the proof of Proposition
5.4
to establishing the following estimate for the
X^{\sigma}
and
Y^{\sigma}
spaces (without the
\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}
structure):

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
s_{1}>\frac{7}{2}
and
0<T\leq 1
. Assume that
{\bf B}
satisfies the assumptions in Proposition
5.4
.
Let
b
and
g
solve the paralinearized system (
5.1
)–(
5.2
) on
(0,T)
. Then, for any
\sigma\geq 0
, the following holds: if

\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{(1)}(\sigma,s_{1},M,\mu,A),\quad T\leq c_{(1)}(\sigma,s_{1},M,\mu,A)\exp(-C_{(1)}(\sigma,s_{1},M,\mu,A)L)

(7.3)

then we have

\|{b}\|_{X^{\sigma}[0,T]}\leq C(\sigma,s_{1},M,\mu,A)e^{C(\sigma,s_{1},M,\mu,A)L}\left(\|{b(0)}\|_{H^{\sigma}}+\|{g}\|_{Y^{\sigma}[0,T]}\right).

(7.4)

After showing that Proposition
7.1
implies Proposition
5.4
in this subsection, the remainder of this section will be devoted to the proof of Proposition
7.1
.

Proof of Proposition
5.4
assuming Proposition
7.1
.

The proof proceeds in several steps. For simplicity, we shall not write out the dependence of constants in
\sigma,M,\mu,A,R,L,s_{1}
and just write
\lesssim
.

Step 1.
We first relax the constraints
\nabla\cdot b=0
and
\nabla\cdot g=0
. Let us denote by
\Pi_{\mathrm{df}}(D):=I-\Pi_{0}(D)
the Leray projection to the space of divergence-free vector fields in
L^{2}
. To avoid confusion, let
b^{\prime}
and
g^{\prime}
satisfy (
5.1
), possibly without the divergence-free condition (
5.2
). Then we have

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\Pi_{\mathrm{df}}(D)b^{\prime}-\nabla\times(T_{{\bf B}}\times(\nabla\times\Pi_{\mathrm{df}}(D)b^{\prime}))+\nabla\times(T_{\nabla\times{\bf B}}\times\Pi_{\mathrm{df}}(D)b^{\prime})\\
&=\Pi_{\mathrm{df}}(D)g^{\prime}-\nabla\times(T_{\nabla\times{\bf B}}\times\Pi_{0}(D)b^{\prime}),\end{split}

(7.5)

and

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\Pi_{0}(D)b^{\prime}=\Pi_{0}(D)g^{\prime}.\end{split}

We simply propagate the usual Sobolev regularity for the equation for
\Pi_{0}(D)b^{\prime}
: we have
\|{\Pi_{0}(D)b^{\prime}}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{\sigma+1}[0,T]}\lesssim\|{\Pi_{0}(D)b^{\prime}(0)}\|_{H^{\sigma+1}}+\|{\Pi_{0}(D)g^{\prime}}\|_{L^{1}H^{\sigma+1}[0,T]}
for any
\sigma
. From the product estimate in usual Sobolev spaces and
L^{1}H^{\sigma}[0,T]\subseteq Y^{\sigma}[0,T]
, we have

\begin{split}\|{\nabla\times(T_{\nabla\times{\bf B}}\times\Pi_{0}(D)b^{\prime})}\|_{Y^{\sigma}[0,T]}\lesssim T\|{\Pi_{0}(D)b^{\prime}}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{\sigma+1}[0,T]}.\end{split}

Using
T\leq 1
, controlling
X^{\sigma}
of
\Pi_{0}(D)b^{\prime}
by
L^{\infty}H^{\sigma+1}
and applying Proposition
7.1
to (
7.5
) gives

\|{b^{\prime}}\|_{X^{\sigma}[0,T]}\lesssim\left(\|{b^{\prime}(0)}\|_{H^{\sigma}}+\|{\Pi_{0}(D)b^{\prime}(0)}\|_{H^{\sigma+1}}+\|{\Pi_{\mathrm{df}}(D)g^{\prime}}\|_{Y^{\sigma}[0,T]}+\|{\Pi_{0}(D)g^{\prime}}\|_{L^{1}H^{\sigma+1}[0,T]}\right),

(7.6)

for any
\sigma
. In what follows, we shall use the fact that
\Pi_{0}(D)
and
\Pi_{\mathrm{df}}
are bounded operators in
X^{\sigma}
and
Y^{\sigma}
, which follows from Proposition
6.3
.

Step 2.
Again, assume that
b^{\prime}
and
g^{\prime}
satisfy (
5.1
). For any fixed
z_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
, note that

\displaystyle(x^{3}-z_{0})\nabla\times(T_{{\bf B}}\times(\nabla\times b^{\prime}))-\nabla\times(T_{{\bf B}}\times(\nabla\times((x^{3}-z_{0})b^{\prime})))

\displaystyle=\sum_{k}(x^{3}-z_{0})\nabla\times(P_{<k-10}{\bf B}\times(\nabla\times P_{k}b^{\prime}))-\nabla\times(P_{<k-10}{\bf B}\times(\nabla\times P_{k}((x^{3}-z_{0})b^{\prime})))

\displaystyle=\sum_{k}O(P_{<k-10}{\bf B},\nabla P_{k}b^{\prime}),

and the
L^{1}H^{\sigma}[0,T]
norm of the RHS can be bounded by
T\|{b^{\prime}}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{\sigma+1}[0,T]}
. Writing the other term in the
b^{\prime}
equation in a similar way, we note that
(x^{3}-z_{0})b^{\prime}
satisfies (
5.1
) with the RHS of the form

\begin{split}(x^{3}-z_{0})g^{\prime}+\sum_{k}\left(O(P_{<k-10}{\bf B},\nabla P_{k}b^{\prime})+O(P_{<k-10}\nabla{\bf B},P_{k}b^{\prime})\right).\end{split}

Since
T\leq 1
, applying (
7.6
) to the equation for
(x^{3}-z_{0})b^{\prime}
and again to
b^{\prime}
with
\sigma
replaced by
\sigma+1
, we have

\displaystyle\|{(x^{3}-z_{0})b^{\prime}}\|_{X^{\sigma}[0,T]}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{(x^{3}-z_{0})b^{\prime}(0)}\|_{H^{\sigma}}+\|{\Pi_{0}(D)((x^{3}-z_{0})b^{\prime})(0)}\|_{H^{\sigma+1}}+\|{b^{\prime}(0)}\|_{H^{\sigma+1}}+\|{\Pi_{0}(D)b^{\prime}(0)}\|_{H^{\sigma+2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|{\Pi_{\mathrm{df}}(D)((x^{3}-z_{0})g^{\prime})}\|_{Y^{\sigma}[0,T]}+\|{\Pi_{0}(D)((x^{3}-z_{0})g^{\prime})}\|_{L^{1}H^{\sigma+1}[0,T]}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|{\Pi_{\mathrm{df}}(D)g^{\prime}}\|_{Y^{\sigma+1}[0,T]}+\|{\Pi_{0}(D)g^{\prime}}\|_{L^{1}H^{\sigma+2}[0,T]}.

At this point, we may replace
({x^{3}-z_{0}})
with
\langle{{x^{3}-z_{0}}}\rangle
. Similarly, commuting with
(x^{3}-z_{0})^{N}
and using induction on
N
, we obtain that

\begin{split}\|{\langle{x^{3}-z_{0}}\rangle^{N}b^{\prime}}\|_{X^{\sigma}[0,T]}&\lesssim\sum_{m=0}^{N}\left(\|{\langle{x^{3}-z_{0}}\rangle^{m}b^{\prime}(0)}\|_{H^{\sigma+N-m}}+\|{\Pi_{0}(D)(\langle{x^{3}-z_{0}}\rangle^{m}b^{\prime})(0)}\|_{H^{\sigma+N-m+1}}\right)\\
&\qquad+\sum_{m=0}^{N}\left(\|{\Pi_{\mathrm{df}}(D)(\langle{x^{3}-z_{0}}\rangle^{m}g^{\prime})}\|_{Y^{\sigma+N-m}[0,T]}+\|{\Pi_{0}(D)(\langle{x^{3}-z_{0}}\rangle^{m}g^{\prime})}\|_{L^{1}H^{\sigma+N-m+1}[0,T]}\right)\end{split}

(7.7)

holds for any integer
N\geq 1
.

Step 3.
Now, let
b
and
g
satisfy (
5.1
) together with the divergence-free condition (
5.2
). Take
I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{k^{\prime}}
and
I\in\mathcal{I}_{k}
. Then, define
b_{k,I}
as the solution of (
5.1
) with initial data
b_{k,I}(0)=\chi_{I}P_{k}b(0)
and the RHS of (
5.1
) given by
\chi_{I}P_{k}g
. In particular, we have that
b=\sum_{k\geq 0}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}b_{k,I}
.

We claim that for any
N,N^{\prime}\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\|{\chi_{I^{\prime}}P_{k^{\prime}}b_{k,I}}\|_{X^{\sigma}[0,T]}\lesssim(2^{-k}\mathrm{dist}(I,I^{\prime}))^{-N}2^{-N^{\prime}|{k-k^{\prime}}|}\sum_{I^{\prime\prime}}\left(\|{\chi_{I^{\prime\prime}}P_{k}b(0)}\|_{H^{\sigma}}+\|{\chi_{I^{\prime\prime}}P_{k}g}\|_{Y^{\sigma}[0,T]}\right),

(7.8)

where the sum in the RHS is over the intervals
I^{\prime\prime}
which intersect with the support of
\chi_{I}
. To show (
7.8
), we first apply (
7.7
) with
(b^{\prime},g^{\prime},\sigma)
replaced by
(b_{k,I},\chi_{I}P_{k}g,\sigma+N^{\prime})
, where
z_{0}
is taken to be the midpoint of
I
. Furthermore, we assume that
\dist(I,I^{\prime})\geq 100\cdot 2^{k}
and
k^{\prime}\geq k
. (When
k^{\prime}<k
, we put
\sigma-N^{\prime}
in place of
\sigma+N^{\prime}
.) The point is that, since
P_{k}b(0)
and
P_{k}g
are both divergence-free, we gain one derivative in the estimate of the terms involving
\Pi_{0}(D)=\Delta^{-1}\nabla\nabla\cdot
in the RHS of (
7.7
). This gives:

\begin{split}\|{\langle{x^{3}-z_{0}}\rangle^{N}b_{k,I}}\|_{X^{\sigma+N^{\prime}}[0,T]}\lesssim 2^{k(N+N^{\prime})}\sum_{I^{\prime\prime}}(\|{\chi_{I^{\prime\prime}}P_{k}b(0)}\|_{H^{\sigma}}+\|{\chi_{I^{\prime\prime}}P_{k}g}\|_{Y^{\sigma}[0,T]}).\end{split}

Comparing this with (
7.8
), it suffices to show

\begin{split}\|{\chi_{I^{\prime}}P_{k^{\prime}}b_{k,I}}\|_{X^{\sigma}[0,T]}&\lesssim 2^{-k(N+N^{\prime})}(2^{-k}\mathrm{dist}(I,I^{\prime}))^{-N}2^{N^{\prime}({k-k^{\prime}})}\|{\langle{x^{3}-z_{0}}\rangle^{N}b_{k,I}}\|_{X^{\sigma+N^{\prime}}[0,T]}\\
&=\mathrm{dist}(I,I^{\prime})^{-N}2^{-N^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\|{\langle{x^{3}-z_{0}}\rangle^{N}b_{k,I}}\|_{X^{\sigma+N^{\prime}}[0,T]}.\end{split}

To estimate the LHS, we need to bound the
LE
and
L^{\infty}L^{2}
norms. We focus on the
LE
norm bound since the other one is only easier. To this end we need to estimate

\begin{split}\sum_{k_{0}\geq 0}2^{2k_{0}\sigma}2^{2N^{\prime}k^{\prime}}2^{k_{0}}\dist(I,I^{\prime})^{2N}\|{P_{k_{0}}\chi_{I^{\prime}}P_{k^{\prime}}b_{k,I}}\|_{LE}^{2}\lesssim\|{\langle{x^{3}-z_{0}}\rangle^{N}b_{k,I}}\|_{X^{\sigma+N^{\prime}}[0,T]}^{2}.\end{split}

(7.9)

We first note the off-diagonal decay in
|k^{\prime}-k_{0}|
: when
k_{0}>k^{\prime}
, we may write

\begin{split}P_{k_{0}}\chi_{I^{\prime}}P_{k^{\prime}}=2^{-nk_{0}}\widetilde{P}_{k_{0}}\nabla^{n}(\chi_{I^{\prime}}P_{k^{\prime}})\end{split}

and the RHS is a finite sum of operators of the form

\begin{split}2^{-n(k_{0}-k^{\prime})}\widetilde{P}_{k_{0}}\widetilde{\chi}_{I^{\prime}}\widetilde{P}_{k^{\prime}},\end{split}

where we write
\widetilde{P}_{k}
to be an order 0 Fourier multiplier localized at frequencies
\sim 2^{k}
. It will be convenient to allow the precise form of
\widetilde{P}_{k}
to vary from a line to another. Similarly,
\widetilde{\chi}_{I^{\prime}}
denotes some smooth function which retains the spatial localization property of
\chi_{I^{\prime}}
. In the opposite case when
k^{\prime}>k_{0}
, we can commute derivatives outside of
P_{k^{\prime}}
to gain decay of the form
2^{-n|k_{0}-k^{\prime}|}
again.

Now we focus on the case
k_{0}=k^{\prime}
. The general case can be handled similarly, using the decay
2^{-n|k_{0}-k^{\prime}|}
. The corresponding term from (
7.9
) is simply

\begin{split}2^{2k^{\prime}(\sigma+N^{\prime})}2^{k^{\prime}}\dist(I,I^{\prime})^{2N}\|{P_{k^{\prime}}\chi_{I^{\prime}}P_{k^{\prime}}b_{k,I}}\|_{LE}^{2}.\end{split}

We recall that
P_{k^{\prime}}
is bounded in
LE
and note the pointwise bound of
\chi_{I^{\prime}}P_{k^{\prime}}b_{k,I}
:

\begin{split}\dist(I,I^{\prime})^{N}|\chi_{I^{\prime}}P_{k^{\prime}}b_{k,I}(x)|\lesssim_{K}\chi_{I^{\prime}}(x)\int\frac{2^{3k^{\prime}}}{\langle{2^{k^{\prime}}|x-y|}\rangle^{K}}\frac{\dist(I,I^{\prime})^{N}}{\langle{y^{3}-z_{0}}\rangle^{N}}(\langle{y^{3}-z_{0}}\rangle^{N}|b_{k,I}(y)|)\,\mathrm{d}y.\end{split}

In the integral, consider separately the regions
y^{3}\in 2I^{\prime}
and
y^{3}\notin 2I^{\prime}
, where
2I^{\prime}
is the interval having the same center with
I^{\prime}
but twice thicker. In the first case, we simply have
{\dist(I,I^{\prime})^{N}}\lesssim{|y^{3}-z_{0}|^{N}}
and the corresponding integral in
y
is bounded by
\|{\widetilde{P}_{k^{\prime}}(|x^{3}-z_{0}|^{N}b_{k,I})}\|_{LE}
. In the other case when
y^{3}\notin 2I^{\prime}
, notice that whenever
x\in I^{\prime}
, we have
2^{k^{\prime}}|x-y|+|y^{3}-z_{0}|\gtrsim\dist(I,I^{\prime})
. Therefore, this time we can take
K=N+10
and arrive at the same bound.

Estimating similarly the off diagonal terms in (
7.9
) and summing up the estimates, we arrive at (
7.8
).

Step 4.
Given (
7.8
), we have

\begin{split}2^{k^{\prime}\sigma}\|{P_{k^{\prime}}b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X_{k^{\prime}}[0,T]}\lesssim\sum_{I^{\prime}}\sum_{k}(2^{-k}\mathrm{dist}(I,I^{\prime}))^{-N}2^{-N^{\prime}|{k-k^{\prime}}|}\sum_{I}\sum_{I^{\prime\prime}}\left(\|{\chi_{I^{\prime\prime}}P_{k}b(0)}\|_{H^{\sigma}}+\|{\chi_{I^{\prime\prime}}P_{k}g}\|_{Y^{\sigma}[0,T]}\right),\end{split}

and
by Schur’s test (the loss is
O(2^{|{k-k^{\prime}}|})
if
N
is sufficiently large), we deduce that

\displaystyle 2^{k^{\prime}\sigma}\|{P_{k^{\prime}}b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X_{k^{\prime}}[0,T]}\lesssim\sum_{k}2^{-(N^{\prime}-1)|{k-k^{\prime}}|}\left(\|{P_{k}b(0)}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{\sigma}}+\|{P_{k}g}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}Y^{\sigma}[0,T]}\right).

Choosing
N^{\prime}
large enough, we obtain the desired bound. ∎

The remainder of this section (with the exception of Section
7.7
) is devoted to the proof of Proposition
7.1
.

7.3.
Principal diagonalization and conjugation by Bessel potential

In this subsection, we rewrite (
5.1
) as a system of equations for
\tilde{b}_{\pm}=\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}\Pi_{\pm}(D)b
, which has a diagonalized paradifferential principal part.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
b
be a solution to the system (
5.1
)–(
5.2
) on
(0,T)
. Define

\tilde{b}_{\pm}=\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}\Pi_{\pm}(D)b,\quad\tilde{g}_{\pm}=\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}\Pi_{\pm}(D)g.

Then
\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}b=\tilde{b}_{+}+\tilde{b}_{-}
,
\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}g=\tilde{g}_{+}+\tilde{g}_{-}
. Moreover, these solve

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}&0\\
0&-P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

(7.10)

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}&0\\
0&-\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}0&\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}\\
-\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}&0\\
0&-\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}+(\nabla\times({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}})\cdot\nabla\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\nabla\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}&\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}\\
-\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}&-\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{h}_{+}\\
\tilde{h}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

where
P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}
represents the anti-symmetric 2nd order paradifferential operator

P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}b=\frac{1}{2}T_{{\bf B}^{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}|{\nabla}|b+\frac{1}{2}|{\nabla}|\partial_{\alpha}T_{{\bf B}^{\alpha}}^{\ast}=\sum_{k}\left[\frac{1}{2}{\bf B}^{\alpha}_{<k-10}P_{k}\partial_{\alpha}|{\nabla}|b+\frac{1}{2}|{\nabla}|\partial_{\alpha}P_{k}{\bf B}^{\alpha}_{<k-10}\right],

(7.11)

\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}
represents the commutator term

\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}b=[\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma},P_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}^{(2)}]\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}b,

(7.12)

and

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{h}_{+}\\
\tilde{h}_{-}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{g}_{+}\\
\tilde{g}_{-}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{E}_{0;+}\\
\tilde{E}_{0;-}\end{pmatrix},

(7.13)

with
\tilde{E}_{0}
referring to error terms that obey the following bounds:

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{E}_{0;+}}\|_{Y^{0}}+\|{\tilde{E}_{0;-}}\|_{Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(2^{-(s_{0}-\frac{7}{2})k_{(0)}}+T2^{2k_{(0)}})M\sum_{\pm}\|{\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{X^{0}}.

(7.14)

Finally,
\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}{=0}
if
\sigma=0
.

Remark 7.3
.

Notice that except for the second order operator (
7.11
), we have replaced
{\bf B}
in the coefficient by
({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}
in all of the other terms.

We collect here the key estimates for the proof of Proposition
7.2
.

Lemma 7.4
.

The following statements hold.

(1)

Commutator term,
[P_{k},T_{{\bf B}^{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}]
.

\displaystyle\|{\sum_{k}|{\nabla}|[P_{k},{P_{<k-10}{\bf B}}\cdot\nabla]b}\|_{Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\Big(\sum_{j}2^{\frac{7}{2}j}\|{P_{j}{\bf B}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{\infty}L^{2}_{j}}\Big)}\|{{b}}\|_{X^{0}}.

(7.15)

(2)

Commutator term,
\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{{\bf B}}
.
We have
\sum_{k}\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{P_{<k-10}{\bf B}}P_{k}=\dot{\mathfrak{C}}^{\sigma(1)\sharp}_{{\bf B}}+\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}[\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma}_{{\bf B}}]
, where

\displaystyle\|{\dot{\mathfrak{C}}^{\sigma(1)\sharp}_{{{\bf B}}}b}\|_{Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\Big(\sum_{j}2^{\frac{7}{2}j}\|{P_{j}{\bf B}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{\infty}L^{2}_{j}}\Big)}\|{{b}}\|_{X^{0}},

(7.16)

\displaystyle\|{\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}[\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma}_{{{\bf B}}}]b}\|_{Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T{\|{\nabla{\bf B}}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}}\|{b}\|_{X^{0}}.

(7.17)

(3)

Off-diagonal term,
\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{{\bf B}}
.
We have
\sum_{k}\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{P_{<k-10}{\bf B}}P_{k}=\dot{\mathfrak{S}}^{(1)\sharp}_{{\bf B}}+\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}[\mathfrak{S}_{{\bf B}}]
, where

\displaystyle\|{\dot{\mathfrak{S}}^{(1)\sharp}_{{{\bf B}}}b}\|_{Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\Big(\sum_{j}2^{\frac{7}{2}j}\|{P_{j}{\bf B}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{\infty}L^{2}_{j}}\Big)}\|{{b}}\|_{X^{0}},

(7.18)

\displaystyle\|{\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}[\mathfrak{S}^{\sigma}_{{{\bf B}}}]b}\|_{Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T{\|{\nabla{\bf B}}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}}\|{b}\|_{X^{0}}.

(7.19)

Moreover, we have the commutator bound

\displaystyle\|{[\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma},\sum_{k}\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{{P_{<k-10}{\bf B}}}P_{k}]\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}b}\|_{Y^{0}}\lesssim T{\|{\nabla{\bf B}}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}}\|{b}\|_{X^{0}}.

(7.20)

(4)

Diagonal antisymmetric terms,
\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{{\bf B}}
and
(\nabla\times{\bf B})\cdot\nabla
.
We have

\displaystyle\|{\sum_{k}\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{{P_{<k-10}{\bf B}}}P_{k}b}\|_{Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\Big(\sum_{j}2^{\frac{7}{2}j}\|{P_{j}{\bf B}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{\infty}L^{2}_{j}}\Big)}\|{{b}}\|_{X^{0}},

(7.21)

\displaystyle\|{\sum_{k}(\nabla\times{P_{<k-10}{\bf B}})\cdot\nabla P_{k}b}\|_{Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\Big(\sum_{j}2^{\frac{7}{2}j}\|{P_{j}{\bf B}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{\infty}L^{2}_{j}}\Big)}\|{{b}}\|_{X^{0}},

(7.22)

\displaystyle\|{[\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma},\sum_{k}\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{{P_{<k-10}{\bf B}}}P_{k}]\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}b}\|_{Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T{\|{\nabla{\bf B}}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}}\|{b}\|_{X^{0}},

(7.23)

\displaystyle\|{[\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma},\sum_{k}(\nabla\times{P_{<k-10}{\bf B}})\cdot\nabla P_{k}]\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}b}\|_{Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T{\|{\nabla{\bf B}}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}}\|{b}\|_{X^{0}}.

(7.24)

(5)

Divergence term,
\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{{\bf B}}
.
We have

\displaystyle\|{\sum_{k}\nabla\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{{P_{<k-10}{\bf B}}}P_{k}b}\|_{Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\Big(\sum_{j}2^{\frac{7}{2}j}\|{P_{j}{\bf B}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{\infty}L^{2}_{j}}\Big)}\|{P_{k}b}\|_{X^{0}},

(7.25)

\displaystyle\|{[\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma},\sum_{k}\nabla\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{{P_{<k-10}{\bf B}}}P_{k}]\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}b}\|_{Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T{\|{\nabla{\bf B}}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}}\|{b}\|_{X^{0}}.

(7.26)

(6)

Zeroth order remainder term,
\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}_{\nabla\times{\bf B};\pm}
.
We have

\displaystyle\|{\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}\sum_{k}(\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}_{\nabla\times{\bf B}_{<k-10};\pm})P_{k}\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}b}\|_{Y^{0}}\lesssim T{\|{\nabla{\bf B}}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}}\|{b}\|_{X^{0}}.

(7.27)

Proof.

All estimates in this lemma are proved by combining symbolic calculus in Proposition
2.5
with the estimates in Section
6
.

Step 1: Proof of (
7.15
).

This estimate is an immediate consequence of (
6.21
).

Step 2: Proof of (
7.16
)–(
7.17
).

By the definitions of
\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{{\bf B}}
and
P^{(2)}_{{\bf B}}
, and the fact that
P_{k}
commutes with
\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}
and
\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}P_{k}

\displaystyle=\Big[\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma},\sum_{k}P^{(2)}_{P_{<k-10}{\bf B}}\Big]\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}P_{k}

\displaystyle={\frac{1}{2}\Big[\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma},\sum_{k}P_{<k-10}{\bf B}\cdot\nabla|{\nabla}|P_{k}\Big]\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}}+{\frac{1}{2}\Big[\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma},|{\nabla}|\sum_{k}P_{<k-10}{\bf B}\cdot\nabla P_{k}\Big]\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}}.

By Proposition
2.5
.(2)–(3) and Corollary
2.7
, it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Big[\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma},|{\nabla}|\sum_{k}P_{<k-10}{\bf B}\cdot\nabla{P_{k}}\Big]\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}+\frac{1}{2}\Op\left(\sum_{k}P_{<k-10}\partial_{\alpha}{\bf B}^{\beta}i\xi_{\beta}|{\xi}|{P_{k}(\xi)}\right)\Op\left(i^{-1}\partial_{\xi_{\alpha}}\langle{\xi}\rangle^{\sigma}\right)\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}

is bounded in
L^{2}
with the operator norm
O({\|{\nabla{\bf B}}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}})
. Proceeding similarly for the other commutator, and using (
2.14
), we arrive at the following: defining

\dot{\mathfrak{C}}^{\sigma(1)\sharp}_{{\bf B}}:=\sum_{k}\left(P_{<k-10}(\partial_{\alpha}{\bf B}^{\beta})\right)\Op\left({\xi_{\beta}}|{\xi}|\frac{\partial_{\xi_{\alpha}}\langle{\xi}\rangle^{\sigma}}{\langle{\xi}\rangle^{\sigma}}\right)P_{k},

the remainder
\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}[\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma}_{{\bf B}}]:=\sum_{k}\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{P_{<k-10}{\bf B}}P_{k}-\dot{\mathfrak{C}}^{\sigma(1)\sharp}_{{\bf B}}
obeys (
7.17
) by
L^{1}L^{2}\hookrightarrow Y^{0}
, Hölder’s inequality (in
t
) and
X^{0}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}L^{2}
. On the other hand, (
7.16
) follows from (
6.19
).

Step 3: Proof of (
7.27
).

We first recall (from the definition of
\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}_{\nabla\times{\bf B};\pm}
in Proposition
3.2
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\mathchoice{(\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}_{\nabla\times{\bf B}_{<k-10};\pm})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{(\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}_{\nabla\times{\bf B}_{<k-10};\pm})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{(\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}_{\nabla\times{\bf B}_{<k-10};\pm})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{(\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}_{\nabla\times{\bf B}_{<k-10};\pm})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}P_{k}=\sum_{k}[\mathchoice{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}},(\nabla\times{\bf B}_{<k-10})\cdot\nabla]P_{k}-\sum_{k}\mathchoice{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}}(\partial_{\beta}(\nabla\times{\bf B}_{<k-10})^{\gamma})P_{k}.

The desired estimate is clearly true for the second term on the RHS, so it only remains to treat the first term. As in Step 2, consideration of Fourier support properties leads to

\displaystyle\sum_{k}[\mathchoice{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}},(\nabla\times{\bf B}_{<k-10})\cdot\nabla]P_{k}b

\displaystyle=\Big[\mathchoice{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\Pi_{\pm}(D)^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}},\sum_{k}(\nabla\times{\bf B}_{<k-10})P_{[k-5,k+5]}\cdot\nabla\Big]P_{k}b.

We may apply Proposition
2.5
.(2) and (3) to the commutator and conclude that it is bounded on
H^{\sigma}
for any
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
with operator norm
O(C_{\sigma}{\|{\nabla{\bf B}}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}})
. Using
L^{1}H^{\sigma}\hookrightarrow Y^{\sigma}
, Hölder’s inequality,
X^{\sigma}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}H^{\sigma}
,
X^{\sigma}=\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}X^{0}
and
Y^{\sigma}=\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}Y^{0}
, we obtain (
7.27
).

Step 4: Proof of the remaining estimates.

The remaining estimates are proved using the techniques we already used in Steps 1, 2 and 3. Since there are essentially no new ideas, we will simply give a summary of these proofs.

Bounds (
7.21
), (
7.22
) and (
7.25
) – which do not involve any commutators – follow rather immediately from (
6.19
). To prove (
7.18
)–(
7.19
), we begin by recalling (from the definition of
\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{{\bf B}}
in Proposition
3.2
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{P_{<k-10}{\bf B}}P_{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k}\left(|{\nabla}|(\partial^{\alpha}(P_{<k-10}{\bf B})_{\beta})+(\partial^{\alpha}(P_{<k-10}{\bf B})_{\beta})|{\nabla}|\right)P_{k}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k}[|{\nabla}|,P_{<k-10}{\bf B}]P_{k}.

The contribution of
\frac{1}{2}[|{\nabla}|,P_{<k-10}{\bf B}]P_{k}
is treated as in Step 2 using Proposition
2.5
, Proposition
6.3
and (
6.19
) (in particular, this term gives rise to
\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}[\mathfrak{S}_{{\bf B}}]
), whereas the contribution of the first line is handled directly using (
6.19
) (like, say, (
7.21
)). Finally, the commutator bound (
7.20
), (
7.23
) and (
7.26
) are proved by an argument similar to Step 3. ∎

Proof of Proposition
7.2
.

We begin by using Proposition
3.2
to rewrite (
5.1
) as:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\begin{pmatrix}b_{+}\\
b_{-}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{k}P_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}^{(2)}P_{k}&0\\
0&-\sum_{k}P_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}^{(2)}P_{k}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}b_{+}\\
b_{-}\end{pmatrix}

(7.28)

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}0&\sum_{k}\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}P_{k}\\
-\sum_{k}\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}P_{k}&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}b_{+}\\
b_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{k}\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}P_{k}&0\\
0&-\sum_{k}\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}P_{k}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}b_{+}\\
b_{-}\end{pmatrix}+\sum_{k}(\nabla\times{\bf B}_{<k-10})P_{k}\cdot\nabla\begin{pmatrix}b_{+}\\
b_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\nabla\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{k}\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}P_{k}&\sum_{k}\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}P_{k}\\
-\sum_{k}\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}P_{k}&-\sum_{k}\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}P_{k}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}b_{+}\\
b_{-}\end{pmatrix}-E_{0(i)}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}g_{+}\\
g_{-}\end{pmatrix},

with
b_{\pm}=\Pi_{\pm}(D)b
and

\displaystyle E_{0(i)}:=-\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{k}\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}_{\nabla\times{\bf B}_{<k-10};+}P_{k}&\sum_{k}\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}_{\nabla\times{\bf B}_{<k-10};+}P_{k}\\
\sum_{k}\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}_{\nabla\times{\bf B}_{<k-10};-}P_{k}&\sum_{k}\mathfrak{R}^{(0)}_{\nabla\times{\bf B}_{<k-10};-}P_{k}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}b_{+}\\
b_{-}\end{pmatrix}.

Next, introducing the conjugation of
b_{\pm}
by the Bessel potential
\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}
,

\tilde{b}_{\pm}=\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}b_{\pm},\quad\tilde{g}_{\pm}=\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}g_{\pm},

we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{k}P_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}^{(2)}P_{k}&0\\
0&-\sum_{k}P_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}^{(2)}P_{k}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{k}\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}P_{k}&0\\
0&-\sum_{k}\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}P_{k}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}0&\sum_{k}\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}P_{k}\\
-\sum_{k}\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}P_{k}&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{k}\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}P_{k}&0\\
0&-\sum_{k}\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}P_{k}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}+\sum_{k}(\nabla\times{\bf B}_{<k-10})P_{k}\cdot\nabla\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\nabla\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{k}\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}P_{k}&\sum_{k}\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}P_{k}\\
-\sum_{k}\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}P_{k}&-\sum_{k}\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}P_{k}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}-\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}E_{0(i)}-\tilde{E}_{0(ii)}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{g}_{+}\\
\tilde{g}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

where
\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}=[\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma},P_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}^{(2)}]\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}
is the commutator of
\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}
and
P_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}^{(2)}
(conjugated by
\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}
), while
\tilde{E}_{0(ii)}
represents all commutator terms except those involving
P_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}^{(2)}
(clearly, both are zero if
\sigma=0
). By (
7.27
), we have

\|{\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}E_{0(i)}}\|_{Y^{0}}\lesssim TM\sum_{\pm}\|{\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{X^{0}}.

(7.29)

Moreover, by (
7.20
), (
7.23
), (
7.26
), and (
7.24
), we have

\|{\tilde{E}_{0(ii)}}\|_{Y^{0}}{\lesssim TM\sum_{\pm}\|{\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{X^{0}}.}

(7.30)

These two estimates are parts of the proof of the error bound (
5.8
), which is completed below.

To proceed, we replace
\sum_{k}P_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}^{(2)}P_{k}
by its anti-symmetrization
P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}
. Moreover, for all first order terms (i.e., lines 2–4), we split
{\bf B}_{<k-10}=({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}+({\bf B}_{<k-10}-({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}})
. Then we arrive at

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}&0\\
0&-P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{k}\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}P_{k}&0\\
0&-\sum_{k}\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}P_{k}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}0&\sum_{k}\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}P_{k}\\
-\sum_{k}\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}P_{k}&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{k}\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}P_{k}&0\\
0&-\sum_{k}\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}P_{k}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}+\sum_{k}(\nabla\times({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}})P_{k}\cdot\nabla\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\nabla\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{k}\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}P_{k}&\sum_{k}\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}P_{k}\\
-\sum_{k}\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}P_{k}&-\sum_{k}\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}P_{k}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}-\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}E_{0(i)}-\tilde{E}_{0(ii)}-\tilde{E}_{0(iii)}-\tilde{E}_{0(iv)}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{g}_{+}\\
\tilde{g}_{-}\end{pmatrix},

where
\tilde{E}_{0(iii)}
represents the contribution of
{\bf B}_{<k-10}-({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}
in the first order terms and
\tilde{E}_{0(iv)}
is the error generated by replacing
\sum_{k}P_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}^{(2)}P_{k}
by
P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}
, i.e.,

\tilde{E}_{0(iv)}:=\begin{pmatrix}(\sum_{k}P_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}^{(2)}P_{k}-P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp})\tilde{b}_{+}\\
-(\sum_{k}P_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}^{(2)}P_{k}-P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp})\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}.

Defining

\tilde{E}_{0}:=\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}E_{0(i)}+\tilde{E}_{0(ii)}+\tilde{E}_{0(iii)}+\tilde{E}_{0(iv)},

and summing up in
k
on lines 2–4, note that this equation is the same as (
7.10
).

We are left to prove (
5.8
), and in view of (
7.29
) and (
7.30
), it remains to only treat
\tilde{E}_{0(iii)}
and
\tilde{E}_{0(iv)}
. To estimate
\tilde{E}_{0(iii)}
, we further split

{\bf B}_{<k-10}-({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}=({\bf B}_{<k-10}-{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}})+({\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}-({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}).

For the latter, observe that

\displaystyle\|{{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}-({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{\infty}H^{s_{0}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T2^{2k_{(0)}}\|{\partial_{t}{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{\infty}H^{s_{0}-2}}.

Hence, an application of (
7.16
), (
7.17
), (
7.18
) (
7.19
), (
7.21
), (
7.22
), and (
7.25
) gives

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{E}_{0(iii)}}\|_{Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\Big[\sum_{k}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j\in[k_{(0)},k-10)}2^{\frac{7}{2}j}\|{P_{j}{\bf B}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{\infty}L^{2}_{j}}+\sum_{j<k_{(0)}}2^{\frac{7}{2}j}\|{P_{j}({\bf B}-{\bf B}_{0})}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{\infty}L^{2}_{j}}\Big)\|{P_{k}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{X_{k}}\Big]^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(2^{-(s_{0}-\frac{7}{2})k_{(0)}}\|{{\bf B}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s_{0}}}+T2^{2k_{(0)}}\|{\partial_{t}{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{\infty}H^{\frac{7}{2}-2}}\right)\sum_{\pm}\|{\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{X^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(2^{-(s_{0}-\frac{7}{2})k_{(0)}}+T2^{2k_{(0)}})M\sum_{\pm}\|{\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{X^{0}},

which is sufficient for proving (
7.14
).

Finally, to estimate
\tilde{E}_{0(iv)}
, we write

\displaystyle\pm(\sum_{k}P_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}^{(2)}P_{k}-P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp})\tilde{b}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=\pm\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k}|{\nabla}|[P_{k},{\bf B}_{<k-10}\cdot\nabla]\tilde{b}_{\pm}=\pm\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k}|{\nabla}|[P_{k},P_{[k_{(0)},k-10)}{\bf B}\cdot\nabla]\tilde{b}_{\pm},

where we used the fact that
\sum_{k}[P_{k},P_{<k_{(0)}}{\bf B}\cdot\nabla]=[1,P_{<k_{(0)}}{\bf B}\cdot\nabla]=0
. Using (
7.15
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|{(\sum_{k}P_{{\bf B}_{<k-10}}^{(2)}P_{k}-P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp})\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{Y^{0}}\lesssim 2^{-(s_{0}-\frac{7}{2})k_{(0)}}M\|{\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{X^{0}},

which is again acceptable for the proof of (
7.14
). ∎

7.4.
Local smoothing estimate assuming boundedness of energy

Our remaining task is to control the
X^{0}
norm of the solution
\tilde{b}_{\pm}
to (
7.10
) in terms of
\|{\tilde{h}_{\pm}}\|_{Y^{0}}
and the initial data. In this subsection, we reduce the problem to estimating the
L^{\infty}L^{2}
norm of
\tilde{b}_{\pm}
.

Proposition 7.5
.

Let
\tilde{b}_{\pm}
solve (
7.10
). Then we have for all sufficiently large
k_{(1)}
satisfying
k_{(1)}\geq k_{(0)}
,

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}[0,T]}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}[0,T]}^{2}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\left(\|{b}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}[0,T]}^{2}+\|{\tilde{h}}\|_{Y^{0}[0,T]}^{2}\right)

(7.31)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\left(2^{-(s_{0}-\frac{7}{2})k_{(0)}}+e^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}T\right)\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}[0,T]}^{2}.

Here, it is important that the constant in front of
\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}[0,T]}^{2}
is
independent
of
L
(also
R
),
k_{(0)}
and
k_{(1)}
. Note also that the factor in front of
\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}}^{2}
can be made arbitrarily small by first taking
k_{(0)}
large, then taking
T
small. Furthermore, while the constants depend also on
R_{0}
(increasing functions of
R_{0}
), we omit writing out the dependence since we always have
L_{0}\geq\frac{1}{24}R_{0}
.

Proof.

The overall strategy is to multiply (
7.10
) (on the left) by a classical pseudodifferential operator
(\mathfrak{F}\tilde{b}_{+},-\mathfrak{F}\tilde{b}_{-})
and integrate over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, where
\mathfrak{F}(x,D)
is constructed so that the commutator
[P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp},\mathfrak{F}(x,D)]
has a good positivity property.

Step 1: Construction of positive commutator.

In what follows, we will use the shorthand
\mathring{{\bf B}}:=({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}
. Let
\epsilon_{0}
be as in Lemma
4.5
, and let
R_{0},L_{0}>0
be so that
{\bf B}_{0}
lies in
\mathcal{B}^{s_{0}}_{\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{0}}(M,\mu,A,R_{0},L_{0})
. We will assume that
k_{(0)}
is large enough so that
\mathring{{\bf B}}\in\mathcal{B}^{s_{0}}_{\epsilon_{0}}(2M,\frac{1}{2}\mu,2A,R_{0},2L_{0})
.

We begin by introducing a (positive) envelope

F(z):=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\langle{2^{k_{(0)}}(z-z^{\prime})}\rangle^{-100}2^{k_{(0)}}\sup_{\{x^{3}=z^{\prime}\}}|{\nabla\mathring{{\bf B}}}|\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}.

Since
\mathring{{\bf B}}=({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}=P_{<k_{(0)}+3}({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}
, we have

\displaystyle|{\nabla\mathring{{\bf B}}(x^{1},x^{2},x^{3})}|

\displaystyle=|{P_{<k_{(0)}+3}\nabla\mathring{{\bf B}}(x^{1},x^{2},x^{3})}|\lesssim\int 2^{3k_{(0)}}\langle{2^{k_{(0)}}|{x-x^{\prime}}|}\rangle^{-200}|{\nabla\mathring{{\bf B}}(x^{\prime})}|\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\lesssim F(x^{3}).

(7.32)

On the other hand, by Young’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\int F(z)\,\mathrm{d}z\lesssim\|{\nabla\mathring{{\bf B}}}\|_{L^{1}_{x^{3}}L^{\infty}_{x^{1},x^{2}}}\lesssim M,

(7.33)

while for higher derivatives, we have

\displaystyle|{\partial_{z}^{N}F(z)}|\lesssim_{N}2^{Nk_{(0)}}M.

(7.34)

Using
F
and two positive constants
C_{f}
and
C_{\mathrm{med}}
to be determined below, we define

\displaystyle f_{\mathrm{out}}(x^{3}):=12C_{f}\int_{-\infty}^{x^{3}}(1-\chi_{<R_{0}}(z))F(z)\,\mathrm{d}z+12C_{\mathrm{med}}\int_{-\infty}^{x^{3}}R_{0}^{-1}(\chi_{<8R_{0}}-\chi_{<R_{0}})(z)\,\mathrm{d}z.

Recall that, by (
4.4
), we have
|{\partial_{\xi_{3}}p_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}(x,\xi)}|\geq\frac{1}{12}|{\xi}|
if
|{\mathring{{\bf B}}-{\bf e}_{3}}|<\frac{1}{2}
, which holds on
\supp(1-\chi_{<R_{0}}(x^{3}))
. Hence,
f_{\mathrm{out}}
satisfies the positive commutator (more precisely, Poisson bracket) property

\displaystyle\{p_{\mathring{{\bf B}}},f_{\mathrm{out}}\}

\displaystyle\geq|{\xi}|\left[C_{f}(1-\chi_{<R_{0}})(x^{3})F(x^{3})+C_{\mathrm{med}}R_{0}^{-1}(\chi_{<8R_{0}}-\chi_{<R_{0}})(x^{3})\right]>0,

while the following symbol bounds also hold:

\displaystyle|{f_{\mathrm{out}}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{f}M+C_{\mathrm{med}},

\displaystyle|{\partial_{x^{3}}^{N}f_{\mathrm{out}}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}C_{f}2^{(N-1)_{+}k_{(0)}}M+C_{\mathrm{med}}R_{0}^{-N}.

Next, we consider a Doi-type multiplier of the form

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{\mathrm{in}}(x,\xi):=\int_{-\infty}^{0}\chi_{<2R_{0}}(X^{3}(t;x,\xi))|{\Xi(t;x,\xi)}|\,\mathrm{d}t.

where
(X,\Xi)(t;x,\xi)
is the bicharacteristic associated with
\mathring{{\bf B}}
. Note that

\{p_{\mathring{{\bf B}}},\tilde{f}_{\mathrm{in}}(x,\xi)\}=\chi_{<2R_{0}}(x^{3})|{\xi}|,

while by (
4.15
) and Proposition
4.11
, the following symbol bounds hold:

\displaystyle|{\tilde{f}_{\mathrm{in}}(x,\xi)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{-1}L_{0},

\displaystyle|{\partial_{x}\tilde{f}_{\mathrm{in}}(x,\xi)}|+|{|{\xi}|\partial_{\xi}\tilde{f}_{\mathrm{in}}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}},

\displaystyle|{\xi}|^{|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|}|{\partial_{x}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\xi}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\tilde{f}_{\mathrm{in}}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M,\mu,A,\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}2^{(|{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}|-1)_{+}k_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A,\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}L_{0}}.

Using
f_{\mathrm{out}}
and
\tilde{f}_{\mathrm{in}}
, we define

f:=\chi_{>1}(\xi)\left(f_{\mathrm{out}}(x^{3})+C_{f}M\chi_{<4R_{0}}(x^{3})\tilde{f}_{\mathrm{in}}(x,\xi)\right).

(7.35)

Then

\displaystyle\{p_{\mathring{{\bf B}}},f\}

\displaystyle\geq|{\xi}|\chi_{>1}(\xi)C_{f}\left((1-\chi_{<R_{0}}(x^{3}))F(x^{3})+M\chi_{<2R_{0}}(x^{3})\right)

(7.36)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{\geq}}+|{\xi}|\chi_{>1}(\xi)\left( C_{\mathrm{med}}R_{0}^{-1}(\chi_{<8R_{0}}-\chi_{<R_{0}})(x^{3})-C_{f}M(4R_{0})^{-1}\chi_{<1}^{\prime}(\tfrac{x^{3}}{4R_{0}})\sup_{x,\xi}|{\tilde{f}_{\mathrm{in}}}|\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{\geq}}-\mathfrak{R}[\{p_{\mathring{{\bf B}}},f\}],

where the remainder
\mathfrak{R}[\{p_{\mathring{{\bf B}}},f\}]
is given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}[\{p_{\mathring{{\bf B}}},f\}]:=\partial_{x^{\alpha}}p_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\frac{\xi_{\alpha}}{|{\xi}|}\chi^{\prime}_{>1}(\xi)\left(f_{\mathrm{out}}(x^{3})+C_{f}M\chi_{<4R_{0}}(x^{3})\tilde{f}_{\mathrm{in}}(x,\xi)\right).

Given
C_{f}
, to be fixed in Step 2 below, we choose
C_{\mathrm{med}}=CC_{f}M\mu^{-1}L_{0}
, where
C
is a sufficiently large absolute constant that makes the second line in (
7.36
) nonnegative.
Observe that we have the following symbol bounds for
f
:

\displaystyle|{f}|

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{f}M(1+\mu^{-1}L_{0}),

(7.37)

\displaystyle|{\partial_{x}f}|+|{\langle{\xi}\rangle\partial_{\xi}f}|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M,\mu,A}C_{f}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}},

(7.38)

\displaystyle\langle{\xi}\rangle^{|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|}|{\partial_{x}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\xi}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}f}|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M,\mu,A,\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}C_{f}2^{(|{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}|-1)_{+}k_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A,\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}L_{0}},

(7.39)

where we used
R_{0}\lesssim L_{0}
. Note also that the remainder term obeys the symbol bound

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{R}[\{p_{\mathring{{\bf B}}},f\}]]_{S^{0};N}\lesssim_{M,\mu,A,N}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}.

(In fact, it is infinitely smoothing, but we need not use this stronger property.)

The positive commutator property of the symbol
f
will be used to bound the contribution of the first order terms in (
7.10
). To control the
LE
norm, we also need the following additional construction. For
I\in\mathcal{I}_{\ell}
,
I\cap(-10R_{0},10R_{0})=\emptyset
, define

f_{I}=\int_{-\infty}^{x^{3}}2^{-\ell}\chi_{I}^{2}(z)\,\mathrm{d}z.

(7.40)

Because of
I\cap(-10R_{0},10R_{0})=\emptyset
, we have

\left\{f_{I},p_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\right\}\gtrsim 2^{-\ell}\chi_{I}^{2}(x^{3})|{\xi}|.

(7.41)

Moreover, since
\ell\geq 0
, note that
f_{I}
obeys the bounds

|{\partial_{x}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}f_{I}}|\lesssim_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}2^{-|{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}|\ell}\lesssim_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}1.

(7.42)

Step 2: Positive commutator identity.

Form a symmetric operator

\mathfrak{F}_{I}:=e^{f}(D,x)e^{f_{I}}(x^{3})e^{f_{I}}(x^{3})e^{f}(x,D).

We multiply (
7.10
) by
(\mathfrak{F}_{I}\tilde{b}_{+},-\mathfrak{F}_{I}\tilde{b}_{-})
and integrate over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. From the principal part
\pm P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}\tilde{b}_{\pm}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle{\partial_{t}\tilde{b}_{\pm}\pm P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}\tilde{b}_{\pm},\pm\mathfrak{F}_{I}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\pm\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\langle{\tilde{b}_{\pm},\mathfrak{F}_{I}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle{\tilde{b}_{\pm},[\mathfrak{F}_{I},P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}]\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\pm\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\langle{e^{f_{I}}(x)e^{f}(x,D)\tilde{b}_{\pm},e^{f_{I}}(x)e^{f}(x,D)\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle{\tilde{b}_{\pm},[\mathfrak{F}_{I},P_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}^{(2)}]\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle+\langle{\tilde{b}_{\pm},\tilde{E}_{I;\pm}}\rangle,

where

\displaystyle\tilde{E}_{I;\pm}=\frac{1}{2}[\mathfrak{F}_{I},P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}-P_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}^{(2)}]\tilde{b}_{\pm}.

(7.43)

For the ensuing argument, it will be convenient to introduce the shorthands

\tilde{B}_{I;\pm}:=e^{f_{I}}(x^{3})\tilde{B}_{\pm},\quad\tilde{B}_{\pm}:=e^{f}(x,D)\tilde{b}_{\pm},\quad\tilde{H}_{I;\pm}:=e^{f_{I}}(x^{3})\tilde{H}_{\pm},\quad\tilde{H}_{\pm}:=e^{f}(x,D)\tilde{h}_{\pm}.

The main term in the preceding multiplier identity is the commutator term
\frac{1}{2}\langle{\tilde{b}_{\pm},[\mathfrak{F}_{I},P_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}^{(2)}]\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\rangle
, which we move to the LHS. Observe that both
\mathfrak{F}_{I}
and
P^{(2)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}
are pseudodifferential operators with classical symbols
\mathfrak{F}_{I}(x,\xi)
and
P^{(2)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}(x,\xi)
with bounds

[\mathfrak{F}_{I}(x,\xi)]_{S^{0};N}\lesssim_{M,\mu,A,N}e^{C_{f}}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}},\quad[P^{(2)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}(x,\xi)]_{S^{2};N}\lesssim_{M,N}e^{Ck_{(0)}},

where we used Proposition
2.5
.(1), Proposition
2.8
.(1) and the symbol bounds (
7.37
)–(
7.39
) and (
7.42
) for
\mathfrak{F}_{I}
, and
\mathring{{\bf B}}=({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}
for
P^{(2)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}
. By Proposition
2.3
, Proposition
2.5
.(1), Proposition
2.8
.(1), and the positivity bounds (
7.36
) and (
7.41
), we have

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle{\tilde{b}_{\pm},[\mathfrak{F}_{I},P_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}^{(2)}]\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle{\tilde{b}_{\pm},\{e^{2(f+f_{I})},p_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\}(x,D)\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle+O(C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2})

\displaystyle=\left\langle{e^{f_{I}}(x^{3})e^{f}(x,D)\tilde{b}_{\pm},\{p_{\mathring{{\bf B}}},f+f_{I}\}(x,D)e^{f_{I}}(x^{3})e^{f}(x,D)\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+O(e^{C_{f}}C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2})

\displaystyle\geq\left\langle{\tilde{B}_{I;\pm},\left(C_{f}(1-\chi_{<R_{0}}(x^{3}))F(x^{3})+C_{f}M\chi_{<2R_{0}}(x^{3})+2^{-\ell}\chi_{I}^{2}(x^{3})\right)\langle{D}\rangle\tilde{B}_{I;\pm}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}-C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{f}}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

Next, we use equation (
7.10
) to rewrite
(\partial_{t}\pm P^{(2)\sharp}_{{\bf B}})\tilde{b}_{\pm}
on the LHS in terms of lower order terms and
\tilde{h}_{\pm}
. By Proposition
2.5
.(1) and Proposition
2.8
.(1), we see that
\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}
,
\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}
,
\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}
,
(\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}})\cdot\nabla
and
\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}
are pseudodifferential operators with classical symbols satisfying

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}(x,\xi)]_{S^{1};N}+[\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}(x,\xi)]_{S^{1};N}+[\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}(x,\xi)]_{S^{1};N}+[(\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}})(x)\cdot(i\xi)]_{S^{1};N}+[\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}(x,\xi)]_{{S^{0}};N}\lesssim_{M,N}e^{Ck_{(0)}}.

Hence, by Proposition
2.3
, Proposition
2.5
.(1) and Proposition
2.8
.(1), we compute

\displaystyle\left\langle{\pm\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\tilde{b}_{\pm},\pm\mathfrak{F}_{I}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle{e^{f+f_{I}}(x,D)\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\tilde{b}_{\pm},e^{f+f_{I}}(x,D)\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle{\tilde{B}_{I;\pm},\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\tilde{B}_{I;\pm}}\right\rangle+O(C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{f}}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}).

Similarly, we derive

\displaystyle\left\langle{\pm\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\tilde{b}_{\mp},\pm\mathfrak{F}_{I}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle{\tilde{B}_{I;\mp},\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\tilde{B}_{I;\pm}}\right\rangle+O(C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{f}}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}).

In view of (
7.32
) and
\|{\nabla\mathring{{\bf B}}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim M
, choosing
C_{f}
sufficiently large (as a universal constant) ensures that

\displaystyle C_{f}(1-\chi_{<R_{0}}(x^{3}))F(x^{3})+C_{f}M\chi_{<2R_{0}}(x^{3})\geq|{\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}(x,\xi)}|+|{\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}(x,\xi)}|,

which implies, by Proposition
2.3
, Cauchy–Schwarz and Proposition
2.5
, that

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\left\langle{\tilde{B}_{I;\pm},\left(C_{f}(1-\chi_{<R_{0}}(x^{3}))F(x^{3})+C_{f}M\chi_{<2R_{0}}(x^{3})+2^{-\ell}\chi_{I}^{2}(x^{3})\right)\langle{D}\rangle\tilde{B}_{I;\pm}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{\pm}\left\langle{\tilde{B}_{I;\pm},\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\tilde{B}_{I;\pm}}\right\rangle+\sum_{\pm}\left\langle{\tilde{B}_{I;\mp},\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\tilde{B}_{I;\pm}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{\pm}\left\langle{\tilde{B}_{I;\pm},2^{-\ell}\langle{D}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{I}^{2}(x^{3})\langle{D}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{B}_{I;\pm}}\right\rangle-C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{f}}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{\pm}\|{2^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell}\chi_{I}\langle{D}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}(e^{f_{I}}(x^{3})\tilde{B}_{\pm})}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{f}}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Now that we fixed
C_{f}
, we will stop keeping track of the dependence of constants on
C_{f}
. For the anti-symmetric operator
\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle{\pm\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\tilde{b}_{\pm},\pm\mathfrak{F}_{I}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle{\tilde{b}_{\pm},[\mathfrak{F}_{I},\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}]\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle=O(C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}).

and similarly,

\displaystyle\left\langle{(\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}})\cdot\nabla\tilde{b}_{\pm},\pm\mathfrak{F}_{I}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=O(C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}).

Finally, since
\nabla\cdot\tilde{b}_{\pm}=0
, we also have

\displaystyle\left\langle{\pm\nabla\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}(\tilde{b}_{+}+\tilde{b}_{-}),\pm\mathfrak{F}_{I}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle{\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}(\tilde{b}_{+}+\tilde{b}_{-}),[\nabla\cdot,\mathfrak{F}_{I}]\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle=O(C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}).

In conclusion, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\|{2^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell}\chi_{I}\langle{D}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}(e^{f_{I}}\tilde{B}_{\pm})}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\partial_{t}\left(\langle{\tilde{B}_{+},e^{2f_{I}}(x^{3})\tilde{B}_{+}}\rangle-\langle{\tilde{B}_{-},e^{2f_{I}}(x^{3})\tilde{B}_{-}}\rangle\right)+\sum_{\pm}\langle{\tilde{b}_{\pm},\tilde{E}_{I;\pm}}\rangle

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+{\langle{\tilde{B}_{\pm},\tilde{H}_{I;\pm}}\rangle}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Integrating this inequality on
t\in(0,T)
and using the duality between
X^{0}
and
Y^{0}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\|{2^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell}\chi_{I}\langle{D}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}(e^{f_{I}}\tilde{B}_{\pm})}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{e^{2f_{I}}\tilde{B}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}+\|{e^{f_{I}}\tilde{B}}\|_{X^{0}}\|{e^{f_{I}}\tilde{H}}\|_{Y^{0}}

(7.44)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}}\|{\tilde{E}_{I}}\|_{Y^{0}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}T\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}.

where, here and below, all spacetime norms are restricted to
(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Recall, from (
7.42
), that
|{f_{I}}|\lesssim 1
. Taking the supremum over
I\in\mathcal{I}_{\ell}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and adding a suitable multiple of
\|{\tilde{B}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}
on both sides, we arrive at the key inequality

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{B}}\|_{X^{0}}^{2}\lesssim\|{\tilde{B}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\tilde{H}}\|_{Y^{0}}^{2}+\sup_{\ell\geq\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\ell}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}}\|{\tilde{E}_{I}}\|_{Y^{0}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}T\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}.

(7.45)

Step 3: Error bound.

In this step, our goal is to prove the following bound for the error term in (
7.44
)

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{E}_{I;\pm}}\|_{Y^{0}}\leq C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\left(2^{-(s_{0}-\frac{7}{2})k_{(0)}}+e^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}T\right)\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}}.

(7.46)

where an important point is that
C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}
is independent of
k_{(0)}
.

We begin by rewriting
\tilde{E}_{I;\pm}
as

\displaystyle\tilde{E}_{I;\pm}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left([\mathfrak{F}_{I},P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}-P_{{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}}]+[\mathfrak{F}_{I},P_{{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}-({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}^{(2)}]\right)\tilde{b}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}[\mathfrak{F}_{I},P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}-P_{{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}}^{(2)\sharp}]\tilde{b}_{\pm}+\frac{1}{2}[\mathfrak{F}_{I},P_{{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}}^{(2)\sharp}-P_{{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}}^{(2)}]\tilde{b}_{\pm}+\frac{1}{2}[\mathfrak{F}_{I},P_{{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}-({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}^{(2)}]\tilde{b}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=:\tilde{E}_{I,1;\pm}+\tilde{E}_{I,2;\pm}+\tilde{E}_{I,3;\pm}.

To estimate
\tilde{E}_{I,1;\pm}
, we write out
P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}-P_{{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}}^{(2)\sharp}=\frac{1}{2}T_{{\bf B}_{\geq k_{(0)}}^{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}|{\nabla}|+\frac{1}{2}|{\nabla}|\partial_{\alpha}T_{{\bf B}_{\geq k_{(0)}}^{\alpha}}
and treat each term separately. For the contribution of the first term, we apply Proposition
2.5
to exploit the commutator structure and derive

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{F}_{I},T_{{\bf B}_{\geq k_{(0)}}^{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}|{\nabla}|]\tilde{b}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=\Op\left(i^{-1}\partial_{\xi_{\beta}}\mathfrak{F}_{I}\right)T_{\partial_{x^{\beta}}{\bf B}_{\geq k_{(0)}}^{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}|{\nabla}|\tilde{b}_{\pm}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}-\Op\left(\partial_{x^{\beta}}\mathfrak{F}_{I}\right)\Op\left(i^{-1}\partial_{\xi_{\beta}}\left(\sum_{k}P_{<k-10}{\bf B}_{\geq k_{(0)}}^{\alpha}(x)P_{k}(\xi)i\xi_{\alpha}|{\xi}|\right)\right)\tilde{b}_{\pm}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+O_{L^{1}L^{2}}(C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}T\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}})

\displaystyle=\Op\left(i^{-1}\partial_{\xi_{\beta}}\mathfrak{F}_{I}\right)P_{\geq k_{(1)}}T_{\partial_{x^{\beta}}{\bf B}_{\geq k_{(0)}}^{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}|{\nabla}|\tilde{b}_{\pm}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}-\Op\left(\partial_{x^{\beta}}\mathfrak{F}_{I}\right)P_{\geq k_{(1)}}\Op\left(i^{-1}\partial_{\xi_{\beta}}\left(\sum_{k}P_{<k-10}{\bf B}_{\geq k_{(0)}}^{\alpha}(x)P_{k}(\xi)i\xi_{\alpha}|{\xi}|\right)\right)\tilde{b}_{\pm}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+O_{L^{1}L^{2}}(C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}T\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}),

where we used Proposition
2.3
,
\|{2^{2k_{(1)}}P_{\geq k_{(1)}}\langle{D}\rangle^{-2}}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}\lesssim 1
and Hölder’s inequality for the last identity. Using (
6.19
), we have

\displaystyle\|{\langle{D}\rangle^{-1}(T_{\partial_{x^{\beta}}{\bf B}_{\geq k_{(0)}}^{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}|{\nabla}|\tilde{b}_{\pm})}\|_{Y^{0}}+\|{\Op\big(i^{-1}\partial_{\xi_{\beta}}\big(\sum_{k}P_{<k-10}{\bf B}_{\geq k_{(0)}}^{\alpha}(x)P_{k}(\xi)i\xi_{\alpha}|{\xi}|\big)\big)\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{Y^{0}}\lesssim M2^{-(s_{0}-\frac{7}{2})k_{(0)}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}},

whereas by Proposition
6.3
.(2) (i.e., the high frequency Calderón–Vaillancourt trick) and the symbol bounds (
7.37
)–(
7.39
) and (
7.42
), we have

\displaystyle\|{\Op\left(i^{-1}\partial_{\xi_{\beta}}\mathfrak{F}_{I}\right)P_{\geq k_{(1)}}\langle{D}\rangle}\|_{Y^{0}\to Y^{0}}+\|{\Op\left(\partial_{x^{\beta}}\mathfrak{F}_{I}\right)P_{\geq k_{(1)}}}\|_{Y^{0}\to Y^{0}}\lesssim_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}},

provided that
k_{(1)}
is sufficiently large depending on
M
,
\mu
,
A
,
L_{0}
and
k_{(0)}
. The contribution of
\frac{1}{2}|{\nabla}|\partial_{\alpha}T_{{\bf B}_{\geq k_{(0)}}^{\alpha}}
is treated similarly. In conclusion, we have

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{E}_{I;1,\pm}}\|_{Y^{0}}\leq C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}2^{-(s_{0}-\frac{7}{2})k_{(0)}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}T\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}}.

To estimate
\tilde{E}_{I,2;\pm}
, we simply note that

P_{{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}}^{(2)\sharp}-P_{{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}}^{(2)}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k}(P_{\geq k-10}{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}^{\alpha})\partial_{\alpha}|{\nabla}|P_{k}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k}P_{k}|{\nabla}|\partial_{\alpha}(P_{\geq k-10}{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}^{\alpha})

where both summands are trivial if
k>k_{(0)}+20
(since
P_{\geq k-10}{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}=0
). Then without using the commutator structure, we may bound

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{E}_{I;2,\pm}}\|_{Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{\mathfrak{F}_{I}(x,D)(P_{{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}}^{(2)\sharp}-P_{{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}}^{(2)})\widetilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{1}L^{2}}+\|{(P_{{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}}^{(2)\sharp}-P_{{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}}^{(2)})\mathfrak{F}_{I}(x,D)\widetilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{1}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M,\mu,A}T2^{2k_{(0)}}M\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}},

where we used Proposition
2.3
, the symbol bounds (
7.37
)–(
7.39
) and (
7.42
) and
\|{P_{k}|{\nabla}|\partial_{\alpha}}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{2k}
.

Finally, to estimate
\tilde{E}_{I,3;\pm}
, we again start by applying Proposition
2.5
to write

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{F}_{I},P_{{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}-({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}^{(2)}]\tilde{b}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=\Op\left(i^{-1}\partial_{\xi_{\beta}}\mathfrak{F}_{I}\right)P_{\partial_{x^{\beta}}({\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}-({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}})}^{(2)}\tilde{b}_{\pm}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}-\Op\left(\partial_{x^{\beta}}\mathfrak{F}_{I}\right)\Op\left(i^{-1}\partial_{\xi_{\beta}}(({\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}^{\alpha}-({\bf B}_{0}^{\alpha})_{<k_{(0)}})i\xi_{\alpha}|{\xi}|)\right)\tilde{b}_{\pm}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+O_{L^{1}L^{2}}(C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}T\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}).

By (
6.19
), we have

\displaystyle\|{\langle{D}\rangle^{-1}(P_{\partial_{x^{\beta}}({\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}-({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}})}^{(2)}\tilde{b}_{\pm})}\|_{Y^{0}}+\|{\Op\left(i^{-1}\partial_{\xi_{\beta}}(({\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}^{\alpha}-({\bf B}_{0}^{\alpha})_{<k_{(0)}})i\xi_{\alpha}|{\xi}|)\right)\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j<k_{(0)}}2^{\frac{7}{2}j}\|{P_{j}({\bf B}-{\bf B}_{0})}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{\infty}L^{2}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T2^{2k_{(0)}}\|{\partial_{t}{\bf B}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{\infty}H^{\frac{7}{2}-2}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}},

which is bounded by
T2^{2k_{(0)}}M\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}}
. Moreover, by Proposition
6.3
.(1) and the symbol bounds (
7.37
)–(
7.39
) and (
7.42
), we have

\displaystyle\|{\Op\left(i^{-1}\partial_{\xi_{\beta}}\mathfrak{F}_{I}\right)\langle{D}\rangle}\|_{Y^{0}\to Y^{0}}+\|{\Op\left(\partial_{x^{\beta}}\mathfrak{F}_{I}\right)}\|_{Y^{0}\to Y^{0}}\lesssim_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{E}_{I;3,\pm}}\|_{Y^{0}}\leq C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}T\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}},

which concludes the proof of (
7.46
).

Step 4: Returning to
\tilde{b}
.

We are now ready to conclude the proof. We claim that

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\|{\tilde{B}}\|_{X^{0}}^{2}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\|{b}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2},

(7.47)

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{B}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\|{b}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2},

(7.48)

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{H}}\|_{Y^{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\|{\tilde{h}}\|_{Y^{0}}^{2}.

(7.49)

Indeed, combining (
7.45
) and (
7.46
) with (
7.47
)–(
7.49
), we obtain the desired estimate (
7.31
) for
\tilde{b}
.

It remains to prove (
7.47
)–(
7.49
). In what follows, we shall freely use the classical symbolic calculus (i.e, Proposition
2.5
.(1)), boundedness properties of
\Op(\mathfrak{a})
for
\mathfrak{a}\in S^{0}
(i.e., Proposition
2.3
