and Proposition
6.3
), and the symbol bounds (
7.37
)–(
7.39
) for
f
. We shall also freely use the simple bound

\|{b}\|_{LE}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\langle{D}\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}b}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}},

(7.50)

which follows from Littlewood–Paley theory and Hölder in time (see also (
2.12
)).

To prove (
7.47
), we begin by noting that

\displaystyle\|{(1-e^{-f}(x,D)e^{f}(x,D))P_{\geq k_{(1)}}}\|_{X^{0}\to X^{0}}\lesssim_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}2^{-k_{(1)}},

which follows from Proposition
2.5
.(1), the symbol bounds and Proposition
6.3
.
Hence, using
\tilde{B}_{\pm}=e^{f}(x,D)\tilde{b}_{\pm}
and
\tilde{b}_{\pm}=\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}b_{\pm}
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{b}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=e^{-f}(x,D)\tilde{B}_{\pm}+(1-e^{-f}(x,D)e^{f}(x,D))P_{<k_{(1)}}\tilde{b}_{\pm}+O_{X^{0}}(Ce^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}2^{-k_{(1)}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}})

\displaystyle=e^{-f}(x,D)P_{\geq k_{(1)}}\tilde{B}_{\pm}+e^{-f}(x,D)P_{<k_{(1)}}e^{f}(x,D)\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}b_{\pm}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+(1-e^{-f}(x,D)e^{f}(x,D))P_{<k_{(1)}}\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}b_{\pm}+O_{X^{0}}(Ce^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}2^{-k_{(1)}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}})

\displaystyle=e^{-f}(x,D)P_{\geq k_{(1)}}\tilde{B}_{\pm}+O_{X^{0}}(Ce^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}(1+T^{\frac{1}{2}})\|{b}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}})+O_{X^{0}}(Ce^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}2^{-k_{(1)}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}})

Taking
k_{(1)}
sufficiently large depending on
M
,
\mu
,
A
,
L_{0}
and
k_{(0)}
, we may apply Proposition
6.3
.(2) to the first term on the last line, and also absorb the last term into the LHS. Thus (
7.47
) follows.

To prove (
7.48
), we begin by noting that

\displaystyle\tilde{B}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=e^{f}(x,D)P_{\geq k_{(1)}}\tilde{b}_{\pm}+e^{f}(x,D)P_{<k_{(1)}}\tilde{b}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=e^{f}(x,D)P_{\geq k_{(1)}}\tilde{b}_{\pm}+e^{f}(x,D)P_{<k_{(1)}}\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}b_{\pm}

\displaystyle=e^{f}(x,D)P_{\geq k_{(1)}}\tilde{b}_{\pm}+O_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}(Ce^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\|{b}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}).

Taking
k_{(1)}
sufficiently large depending on
M
,
\mu
,
A
,
L_{0}
and
k_{(0)}
, we may apply Lemma
2.10
to the first term on the last line, which proves (
7.48
).

Finally, (
7.49
) is a quick consequence of Proposition
6.3
.(1). ∎

7.5.
Renormalization and boundedness of energy

Next, we establish the
L^{\infty}L^{2}
bound for
\tilde{b}
that was assumed in the previous subsection. We begin with the
L^{\infty}L^{2}
bound for
b
, which is a straightforward consequence of the conservative structure of (
7.10
) without
\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}
.

Proposition 7.6
.

Let
b
be a solution to the system (
5.1
)–(
5.2
) on
(0,T)
. Then
b_{\pm}=\Pi_{\pm}(D)b
obeys

\sum_{\pm}\|{b_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}[0,T]}^{2}\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\left(\|{b_{\pm}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{h_{\pm}}\|_{Y^{0}[0,T]}\|{b_{\pm}}\|_{X^{0}[0,T]}\right),

(7.51)

where

\begin{pmatrix}h_{+}\\
h_{-}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\Pi_{+}(D)g\\
\Pi_{-}(D)g\end{pmatrix}+O_{Y^{0}}(2^{-(s_{0}-\frac{7}{2})k_{(0)}}+T2^{2k_{(0)}})M\sum_{\pm}\|{b_{\pm}}\|_{X^{0}}.

Proof.

By Proposition
7.2
,
b_{\pm}
obey (
7.10
) with
\sigma=0
, i.e.,
\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}=0
and
E_{1}^{\sigma}=0
. It also follows that
h_{\pm}
are of the form above. To prove the
L^{\infty}L^{2}
estimate, we multiply (
7.10
) to the left by
(b_{+},b_{-})
and integrate over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, or in other words, take the inner product with
(b_{+},b_{-})^{\top}
with respect to
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3};\mathbb{R}^{3}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Observe that the contribution of the second, third, fourth, fifth and sixth terms on the LHS of (
7.10
) vanish, since the operator in front of
(b_{+},b_{-})^{\top}
is anti-symmetric with respect to
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3};\mathbb{R}^{3}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Moreover, the contribution of the last term on the LHS of (
7.10
) also vanishes since
b_{\pm}
are divergence-free (i.e.,
\langle{b_{\pm}\cdot\nabla(\cdots)}\rangle=-\langle{\nabla\cdot b_{\pm},(\cdots)}\rangle=0
). We end up with the following identity:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\left(\|{b_{+}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{b_{+}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\langle{h_{+},b_{+}}\rangle+\langle{h_{-},b_{-}}\rangle.

Now the desired conclusion follows by integrating in
t
on
(0,T)
. ∎

Next, we prove the
L^{\infty}L^{2}
bound for
\tilde{b}_{\pm}=\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}b_{\pm}
, which is the main result of this subsection.

Proposition 7.7
.

Let
\tilde{b}_{\pm}
solve (
7.10
). Then we have for all sufficiently large
k_{(1)}
satisfying
k_{(1)}\geq k_{(0)}
that

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}[0,T]}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}\left(\|{\tilde{b}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\tilde{h}}\|_{Y^{0}[0,T]}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}[0,T]}\right)

(7.52)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+Ce^{\sigma CA}\epsilon\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}}^{2}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}2^{-(s_{0}-\frac{7}{2})k_{(0)}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|^{2}_{X^{0}[0,T]}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}T\|{\tilde{b}}\|^{2}_{X^{0}[0,T]}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}2^{\sigma k_{(1)}}\|{\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}[0,T]}^{2}.

The most important point is that the constant in front of
\epsilon\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}}^{2}
is
independent
of
L
(and
R
). Coupled with Propositions
7.5
and
7.6
, we will thus be able to take
\epsilon
small enough to absorb this term into the LHS; see Section
7.6
below.

Proof.

As in the proof of Proposition
7.5
, we will use the shorthand
\mathring{{\bf B}}:=({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}
. We divide the proof into several steps.

Step 1: Conjugation by
O_{\pm}
.
We start from (
7.10
), but now with
\sigma>0
. In order to remove
\pm\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}
, we conjugate the equation for
\tilde{b}_{\pm}
by a time-independent pseudodifferential operator
O_{\pm}(x,D)
(to be constructed below). We first record the equation obtained by commuting
O_{+}
with the principal operator
\pm P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}
:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\begin{pmatrix}O_{+}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
O_{-}\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}&0\\
0&-P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}O_{+}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
O_{-}\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}[O_{+},P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}]&0\\
0&-[O_{-},P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}]\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}O_{+}\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}&0\\
0&-O_{-}\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}0&O_{+}\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\\
-O_{-}\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}O_{+}\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}&0\\
0&-O_{-}\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}O_{+}(\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}})\cdot\nabla&0\\
0&O_{-}(\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}})\cdot\nabla\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}O_{+}&0\\
0&O_{-}\end{pmatrix}\nabla\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}&\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\\
-\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}&-\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}O_{+}\tilde{h}_{+}\\
O_{-}\tilde{h}_{-}\end{pmatrix}.

For the commutator of
O_{\pm}
and
P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}
, let us write (in the operator notation)

\displaystyle\pm[O_{\pm},P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}]\tilde{b}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=\pm\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k}[O_{\pm},{\bf B}_{<k-10}^{\alpha}P_{k}\partial_{\alpha}|{\nabla}|]\tilde{b}_{\pm}\pm\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k}[O_{\pm},|{\nabla}|\partial_{\alpha}P_{k}{\bf B}_{<k-10}^{\alpha}]\tilde{b}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=\pm\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k}[O_{\pm},({\bf B}_{<k-10}-{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}})^{\alpha}P_{k}\partial_{\alpha}|{\nabla}|]\tilde{b}_{\pm}\pm\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k}[O_{\pm},|{\nabla}|\partial_{\alpha}P_{k}({\bf B}_{<k-10}-{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}})^{\alpha}]\tilde{b}_{\pm}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+[O_{\pm},P^{(2)}_{{\bf B}_{<k_{(0)}}-\mathring{{\bf B}}}]\tilde{b}_{\pm}+[O_{\pm},P^{(2)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}]\tilde{b}_{\pm}.

Commuting
O_{+}
,
O_{-}
with all the other terms in the equation, we arrive at

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\begin{pmatrix}O_{+}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
O_{-}\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}&0\\
0&-P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}O_{+}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
O_{-}\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

(7.53)

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}\left([O_{+},P_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}^{(2)}]+O_{+}\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\right)\tilde{b}_{+}\\
-\left([O_{-},P_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}^{(2)}]+O_{-}\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\right)\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}O_{+}\tilde{b}_{-}\\
-\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}O_{-}\tilde{b}_{+}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}&0\\
0&-\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}O_{+}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
O_{-}\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}(\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}})\cdot\nabla&0\\
0&(\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}})\cdot\nabla\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}O_{+}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
O_{-}\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\nabla\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}O_{+}&\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}O_{+}\\
-\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}O_{-}&-\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}O_{-}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}-\tilde{E}_{O;1}-\tilde{E}_{O;2}=\begin{pmatrix}O_{+}\tilde{h}_{+}\\
O_{-}\tilde{h}_{-}\end{pmatrix},

where

\displaystyle\tilde{E}_{O;1}

\displaystyle=-\begin{pmatrix}[O_{+},P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}-P_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}^{(2)}]\tilde{b}_{+}\\
-[O_{-},P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}-P_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}^{(2)}]\tilde{b}_{+}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\tilde{E}_{O;2}

\displaystyle=-\begin{pmatrix}0&[O_{+},\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}]\\
-[O_{-},\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}]&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}[O_{+},\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}]&0\\
0&-[O_{-},\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}]\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}-\begin{pmatrix}[O_{+},(\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}})\cdot\nabla]&0\\
0&[O_{-},(\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}})\cdot\nabla]\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}-\left[\begin{pmatrix}O_{+}&0\\
0&O_{-}\end{pmatrix},\nabla\right]\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}&\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\\
-\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}&-\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}-\nabla\begin{pmatrix}[O_{+},\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}]&[O_{+},\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}]\\
-[O_{-},\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}]&-[O_{-},\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}]\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{b}_{+}\\
\tilde{b}_{-}\end{pmatrix}.

Step 2: Construction of
O_{\pm}=e^{\psi_{\pm}}
.

The energy estimate will be proved by multiplying (
7.53
) by
(O_{+}\tilde{b}_{+},O_{-}\tilde{b}_{-})
(to the left) and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
as in Proposition
7.6
(see also Step 4 below). We will list some properties that
O_{\pm}
need to satisfy for this argument to work, and show that such
O_{\pm}
exist.

We assume that
O_{\pm}(x,\xi)=e^{\psi_{\pm}(x,\xi)}
, where
\psi_{\pm}
is real-valued and

\psi_{\pm}(x,\xi)=\psi_{\pm}(x,-\xi).

(7.54)

This ensures that given a real-valued
b
,
O_{\pm}b
is also real-valued.

A key requirement for
\psi_{\pm}
is that the contribution of the third term on the LHS of (
7.53
) is acceptable for the energy estimate. We require the following bound to hold for any
0<\tau\leq T
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau}\left(\left\langle{\left([O_{+},P_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}^{(2)}]+O_{+}\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\right)\tilde{b}_{+},O_{+}\tilde{b}_{+}}\right\rangle-\left\langle{\left([O_{-},P_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}^{(2)}]+O_{-}\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\right)\tilde{b}_{-},O_{-}\tilde{b}_{-}}\right\rangle\right)\,\mathrm{d}t

(7.55)

\displaystyle\geq-\left(C_{M,\mu,A}\epsilon+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}T\right)\sum_{\pm}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}}^{2},

where it is crucial that
C_{M,\mu,A}
does
not
depend on
R
,
k_{(0)}
and
k_{(1)}
.

Another important requirement for
\psi_{\pm}
is that the fourth term on the LHS of (
7.53
) is acceptable in the energy estimate. In the region
\{-R<x^{3}<R\}
, we require that

\psi_{+}(x,\xi)=\psi_{-}(x,\xi)\quad\hbox{ for }-4R<x^{3}<4R.

(7.56)

This condition ensures that the off-diagonal terms
\pm\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}O_{\pm}\tilde{b}_{\mp}
are cancelled in this region, where
\mathring{{\bf B}}
may potentially be large. Globally, we require that the following bound holds for any
0<\tau\leq T
:

\left|{\int_{0}^{\tau}\left(\langle{\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}O_{+}\tilde{b}_{-},O_{+}\tilde{b}_{+}}\rangle-\langle{\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}O_{-}\tilde{b}_{+},O_{-}\tilde{b}_{-}}\rangle\right)\,\mathrm{d}t}\right|\lesssim_{M,\mu,A}\epsilon\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}}^{2}+e^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}T\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}.

(7.57)

Finally, we require the symbols
O_{\pm}
to satisfy

\displaystyle|{O_{\pm}}|

\displaystyle\leq e^{C\sigma A},

(7.58)

\displaystyle|{\partial_{x}O_{\pm}}|+|{\langle{\xi}\rangle\partial_{\xi}O_{\pm}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}e^{C\sigma A},

(7.59)

\displaystyle\langle{\xi}\rangle^{|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|}|{\partial_{x}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\xi}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}O_{\pm}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M,\mu,A,\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A,\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}L}2^{(|{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}|-1)_{+}k_{(0)}}e^{C\sigma A},

(7.60)

where
C
is a
universal
constant. That the RHS of (
7.58
) is independent of
\epsilon
,
R
,
L
and
k_{(0)}
will be key to proving (
7.55
) and (
7.57
) (in combination with Lemma
2.10
and Proposition
6.3
). Moreover, that the RHS of (
7.59
) is independent of
k_{(0)}
will be crucial to the estimate of the commutator term
E_{3}
(see Step 3 below).

We now construct
O_{\pm}(x,\xi)=e^{\psi_{\pm}(x,\xi)}
with the properties listed above. Let us write
\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}
for the principal symbol of
-i\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}

\displaystyle:=-\{\langle{\xi}\rangle^{\sigma},\mathring{{\bf B}}(x)\cdot\xi|{\xi}|\}\langle{\xi}\rangle^{-\sigma}=-\sigma\partial_{\alpha}\mathring{{\bf B}}^{\beta}(x)\frac{\xi_{\alpha}\xi_{\beta}}{\langle{\xi}\rangle^{2}}|{\xi}|.

We define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\psi}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\chi_{<16R}(X^{3}(-t;x,\xi))\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}(X(-t;x,\xi),\Xi(-t;x,\xi))\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\phantom{:=}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\chi_{<16R}(X^{3}(t;x,\xi))\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}(X(t;x,\xi),\Xi(t;x,\xi))\,\mathrm{d}t,

so that
\widetilde{\psi}
is real-valued and

\{p_{\mathring{{\bf B}}},\widetilde{\psi}\}(x,\xi)=\chi_{<16R}(x^{3})\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}(x,\xi).

Moreover, note that
(X,\Xi)(t;x,-\xi)=(X,-\Xi)(t;x,\xi)
and
\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}(x,\xi)=\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}(x,-\xi)
. Therefore,

\widetilde{\psi}(x,-\xi)=\widetilde{\psi}(x,\xi).

Hence, (
7.54
) holds. Next, recall from (
7.2
) that
\mathring{{\bf B}}=({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}\in\mathcal{B}^{s_{0}}_{\epsilon}(2M,\frac{1}{2}\mu,2A,R,2L)
. In view of (
4.8
), we have the symbol bound

\left|{\widetilde{\psi}(x,\xi)}\right|\leq 2\sigma A.

By Lemma
4.1
, (
4.10
), Lemma
4.7
, Proposition
4.11
.(1) and the bound
\|{\nabla\mathring{{\bf B}}}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|{\nabla^{2}\mathring{{\bf B}}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim M
, we have

\left|{\partial_{x}\widetilde{\psi}(x,\xi)}\right|+\left|{|{\xi}|\partial_{\xi}\widetilde{\psi}(x,\xi)}\right|\lesssim_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}\quad\hbox{ for }-32R<x^{3}<32R,

where the implicit constant is
independent of
k_{(0)}
.
For higher derivatives, we also use Proposition
4.11
.(2) and we obtain

\displaystyle|{\xi}|^{|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|}\left|{\partial_{x}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\xi}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\widetilde{\psi}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M,\mu,A,\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A,\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}L}2^{(|{\alpha}|-1)_{+}k_{(0)}}\quad\hbox{ for }-32R<x^{3}<32R.

The drawback of
\widetilde{\psi}
is that the symbol bounds are only good in
\{-32R<x^{3}<32R,\,|{\xi}|>1\}
. To finally produce
\psi_{\pm}
, we localize
\widetilde{\psi}
and add a
\pm
-dependent correction in the following way:

\psi_{\pm}(x,\xi):=\chi_{>1}(\xi)\left(\chi_{<16R}(x^{3})\widetilde{\psi}(x,\xi)\pm\sigma C_{0}Aq(x^{3})\right),

where
C_{0}
is a positive
universal
constant that will be chosen below, and

\displaystyle q(z)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{z}\left(R^{-1}\tilde{\chi}_{(8R,16R)}(-z^{\prime})+R^{-1}\tilde{\chi}_{(8R,16R)}(z^{\prime})\right)\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime},

\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}_{(8R,16R)}(z)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\chi}_{(1,2)}((8R)^{-1}z).

and
\tilde{\chi}_{(1,2)}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
satisfies
\tilde{\chi}_{(1,2)}(z)=1
for
z\in(1,2)
and
\supp\tilde{\chi}_{(1,2)}\subseteq(\frac{1}{2},4)
. Observe that
q(z)=0
for
z\in(-4R,4R)
, so (
7.56
) holds.

From the construction (and since
R\geq 1
), it follows that

\displaystyle|{\psi_{\pm}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+C_{0})\sigma A,

\displaystyle\left|{\partial_{x}\psi_{\pm}(x,\xi)}\right|+\left|{\langle{\xi}\rangle\partial_{\xi}\psi_{\pm}(x,\xi)}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L},

\displaystyle\langle{\xi}\rangle^{|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|}\left|{\partial_{x}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\xi}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\psi_{\pm}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M,\mu,A,\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}2^{(|{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}|-1)_{+}k_{(0)}},

which implies the desired symbol bounds (
7.58
)–(
7.60
) for
O_{\pm}=e^{\psi_{\pm}}
. To complete the construction, it remains to prove (
7.55
) and (
7.57
), and to fix the positive universal constant
C_{0}
.

Let us first fix the choice of
C_{0}
and prove (
7.55
). Consider the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\pm\left(\{p_{\mathring{{\bf B}}},\psi_{\pm}\}-\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mp\chi_{>1}(\xi)(1-\chi_{<16R})\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+\chi_{>1}(\xi)\{p_{\mathring{{\bf B}}},x^{3}\}\left(\sigma C_{0}Aq^{\prime}(x^{3})\pm\chi^{\prime}_{<16R}(x^{3})\widetilde{\psi}(x,\xi)\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+\chi_{>1}^{\prime}(\xi)\{p_{\mathring{{\bf B}}},|{\xi}|\}\left(\chi_{<16R}(x^{3})\widetilde{\psi}(x,\xi)\pm\sigma C_{0}Aq(x^{3})\right)

\displaystyle=:\mp(1-\chi_{<16R})\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}+\mathfrak{q}_{\pm}^{(1)}+\mathfrak{e}_{\pm}.

Writing
\chi^{\prime}_{<16R}(x^{3})=(16R)^{-1}\chi^{\prime}_{<1}(\frac{x^{3}}{16R})
, it is evident that
\chi^{\prime}_{<16R}(x^{3})
is supported in
(-16R,-8R)\cup(8R,16R)
and is of size
O(R^{-1})
. Therefore, by fixing
C_{0}
to be a sufficiently large
universal
constant, we may ensure that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}_{\pm}^{(1)}=\chi_{>1}(\xi)\{p_{\mathring{{\bf B}}},x^{3}\}\left(\sigma C_{0}Aq^{\prime}(x^{3})\pm(16R)^{-1}\chi^{\prime}_{<1}(\tfrac{x^{3}}{16R})\widetilde{\psi}(x,\xi)\right)>0.

Moreover, by the symbol bounds for
\widetilde{\psi}
, it follows that
\mathfrak{q}_{\pm}^{(1)}\in S^{1}
with
[\mathfrak{q}_{\pm}^{(1)}]_{S^{1};N}\lesssim_{M,\mu,A,N}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}2^{(N-1)_{+}k_{(0)}}
.
Next, in view of its
\xi
-support property, we see that
\mathfrak{e}_{\pm}\in S^{0}
(in fact,
\mathfrak{e}_{\pm}\in S^{-\infty}
) with
[\mathfrak{e}_{\pm}]_{S^{0};N}\lesssim_{M,\mu,A,N}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}2^{(N-1)_{+}k_{(0)}}
. By Propositions
2.3
,
2.5
and
2.8
, it follows that

\displaystyle\pm\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\langle{\left([O_{\pm},P_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}^{(2)}]+O_{\pm}\mathfrak{C}^{\sigma(1)}_{({\bf B}_{0})_{<k_{(0)}}}\right)\tilde{b}_{\pm},O_{\pm}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\pm\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\langle{\Op{e^{\psi_{\pm}}}\left(\{p_{\mathring{{\bf B}}},\psi_{\pm}\}-\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\right)\tilde{b}_{\pm},O_{\pm}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t+O(C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}e^{Ck_{(0)}}T\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2})

\displaystyle=\mp\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\langle{\Op\left({e^{\psi_{\pm}}}(1-\chi_{<16R}(x^{3}))\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\right)\tilde{b}_{\pm},O_{\pm}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\langle{\Op(\mathfrak{q}_{\pm}^{(1)})\tilde{b}_{\pm},O_{\pm}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+O(C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}e^{Ck_{(0)}}T\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2})

\displaystyle=\mp\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\langle{\Op\left({e^{2\psi_{\pm}}}(1-\chi_{<16R}(x^{3}))\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\right)\tilde{b}_{\pm},\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\langle{\Op({e^{2\psi_{\pm}}}\mathfrak{q}_{\pm}^{(1)})\tilde{b}_{\pm},\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+O(C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}e^{Ck_{(0)}}T\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2})

By the positivity property, the symbol bounds and Proposition
2.3
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\langle{\Op({e^{2\psi_{\pm}}}\mathfrak{q}_{\pm}^{(1)})\tilde{b}_{\pm},\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t\geq-C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}e^{Ck_{(0)}}T\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}.

To deal with the first term, we first peel off
{e^{2\psi_{\pm}}}
using the high frequency Calderòn–Vaillancourt trick. More precisely, we estimate

\displaystyle\left|{\mp\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\langle{\Op\left({e^{2\psi_{\pm}}}(1-\chi_{<16R}(x^{3}))\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\right)\tilde{b}_{\pm},\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\Op\left({e^{2\psi_{\pm}}}\right){P_{\geq k_{(1)}}}\Op\left((1-\chi_{<16R}(x^{3}))\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\right)\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\|_{Y^{0}}\|{\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{X^{0}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}{+T\left\|{\Op\left(e^{2\psi_{\pm}}\right)P_{<k_{(1)}}\Op\left((1-\chi_{<16R}(x^{3}))\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\right)\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}\|{\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}T\|{\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{C\sigma A}\left\|{\Op\left((1-\chi_{<16R}(x^{3}))\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\right)\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\|_{Y^{0}}\|{\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{X^{0}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}T\|{\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}.

In the first inequality, we used Proposition
2.5
. In the last inequality, we used Proposition
6.3
.(2) (high frequency Calderòn–Vaillancourt trick) and the symbol bound (
7.58
) for the contribution of
P_{\geq k_{(1)}}
, and that
\|{2^{-k_{(1)}}P_{<k_{(1)}}\langle{D}\rangle}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}\lesssim 1
,
\langle{D}\rangle^{-1}\in S^{-1}
and
(1-\chi_{<16R}(x^{3}))\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\in S^{1}
for the contribution of
P_{<k_{(1)}}
. Next, observe that

\displaystyle\Op\left((1-\chi_{<16R}(x^{3}))\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\right)\tilde{b}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=\sigma(1-\chi_{<16R}(x^{3}))(\partial_{\alpha}\mathring{{\bf B}}^{\beta})(x)\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\beta}}{\langle{D}\rangle^{2}}|{D}|\tilde{b}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=\sigma(1-\chi_{<16R}(x^{3}))\left(\partial_{\alpha}\left((1-\chi_{<8R}(x^{3}))\mathring{{\bf B}}^{\beta}(x)\right)\right)\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\beta}}{\langle{D}\rangle^{2}}|{D}|\tilde{b}_{\pm}

and that
\|{(1-\chi_{<8R}(x^{3}))\mathring{{\bf B}}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s_{0}}}\lesssim\epsilon
by Proposition
6.4
(here, we have taken
k_{(0)}
to be sufficiently small depending on
\epsilon
). By (
6.19
), it follows that

\displaystyle\left\|{\Op\left((1-\chi_{<16R}(x^{3}))\mathfrak{c}^{\sigma(1)}\right)\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\right\|_{Y^{0}}\lesssim\epsilon\|{\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{X^{0}},

which implies (
7.55
).

Finally, we prove (
7.57
). Using Propositions
2.3
,
2.5
and
2.8
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau}\left(\langle{\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}O_{+}\tilde{b}_{-},O_{+}\tilde{b}_{+}}\rangle-\langle{\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}O_{-}\tilde{b}_{+},O_{-}\tilde{b}_{-}}\rangle\right)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\langle{\tilde{b}_{-},\left(O_{+}^{\ast}\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}O_{+}-O_{-}^{\ast}\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}O_{-}\right)\tilde{b}_{+}}\right\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\langle{\tilde{b}_{-},\Op\left((e^{2\psi_{+}}-e^{2\psi_{-}})\mathfrak{s}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}(x,\xi)\right)\tilde{b}_{+}}\right\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t+O(C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}e^{Ck_{(0)}}T\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}),

where
\mathfrak{s}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}(x,\xi)
is the principal symbol of
\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}
given by

\mathchoice{(\mathfrak{s}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{(\mathfrak{s}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{(\mathfrak{s}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{(\mathfrak{s}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}})^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}=\frac{1}{2}|{\xi}|\left((\partial_{\alpha}\mathring{{\bf B}}^{\beta})+(\partial_{\beta}\mathring{{\bf B}}^{\alpha})\right)+\frac{1}{2}\{|{\xi}|,\mathring{{\bf B}}\cdot\xi\}\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}.

Observe that, by (
7.56
),

(e^{2\psi_{-}}-e^{2\psi_{+}})\mathfrak{s}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}(x,\xi)=(e^{2\psi_{-}}-e^{2\psi_{+}})\mathfrak{s}^{(1)}_{(1-\chi_{<2R}(x^{3}))\mathring{{\bf B}}}(x,\xi).

As in the proof of (
7.55
), we now peel off
(e^{2\psi_{-}}-e^{2\psi_{+}})(x,\xi)
using Proposition
2.5
, (
7.58
) and the high frequency Calderòn–Vaillancourt trick (i.e., Proposition
6.3
.(2)):

\displaystyle\left|{\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\langle{\tilde{b}_{-},\Op\left((e^{2\psi_{+}}-e^{2\psi_{-}})\mathfrak{s}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}\right)\tilde{b}_{+}}\right\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t}\right|

\displaystyle\leq e^{C\sigma A}\left\|{\Op\left(\mathfrak{s}^{(1)}_{(1-\chi_{<2R}(x^{3}))\mathring{{\bf B}}}\right)\tilde{b}_{+}}\right\|_{Y^{0}}\|{\tilde{b}_{-}}\|_{X^{0}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}T\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}

Again as before, note that
\|{(1-\chi_{<2R}(x^{3}))\mathring{{\bf B}}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s_{0}}}\lesssim\epsilon
by Proposition
6.4
. By (
6.19
), it follows that

\displaystyle\left\|{\Op\left(\mathfrak{s}^{(1)}_{(1-\chi_{<2R}(x^{3}))\mathring{{\bf B}}}\right)\tilde{b}_{+}}\right\|_{Y^{0}}\lesssim\epsilon\|{\tilde{b}_{+}}\|_{X^{0}},

which completes the proof of (
7.57
).

Step 3: Bounds for
\tilde{E}_{O;1}
and
\tilde{E}_{O;2}
.

For
\tilde{E}_{O;1}
and
\tilde{E}_{O;2}
, we shall prove

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{E}_{O;1}}\|_{Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}e^{C\sigma A}\left(2^{-(s_{0}-\frac{7}{2})k_{(0)}}+e^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}T\right)\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}},

(7.61)

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{E}_{O;2}}\|_{Y^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}T\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}}.

(7.62)

Estimate (
7.61
) is proved in exactly the same manner as (
7.46
), using the symbol bounds (
7.58
)–(
7.60
) (as before, it is crucial that the bound (
7.59
) for
\partial_{x}O_{\pm}
and
\langle{\xi}\rangle\partial_{\xi}O_{\pm}
do not involve
k_{(0)}
).

To prove (
7.62
), observe that all the operators in
\tilde{E}_{O;2}
has order
0
by the standard pseudodifferential calculus (i.e., Proposition
2.5
.(1)). In view of the symbol bounds (
7.58
)–(
7.60
), as well as those for
\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}
,
\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}
and
\mathfrak{U}^{(0)}
, which involve
M
and
k_{(0)}
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{E}_{O;2}}\|_{L^{1}L^{2}}\lesssim_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}T\sum_{\pm}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}},

which implies (
7.62
).

Step 4: Completion of the proof.

We multiply (
7.53
) on the left by
(O_{+}\tilde{b}_{+},O_{-}\tilde{b}_{-})
and integrating on
(0,\tau)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, where
0<\tau\leq T
. Using (
7.55
), (
7.57
), (
7.61
) and (
7.62
), as well as the antisymmetry of the operators
P_{{\bf B}}^{(2)\sharp}
,
\mathfrak{A}^{(1)}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}
and
(\nabla\times\mathring{{\bf B}})\cdot\nabla
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\|{O_{\pm}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\|{O_{\pm}\tilde{b}_{\pm}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sum_{\pm}\|{O_{\pm}^{\ast}O_{\pm}\tilde{h}_{\pm}}\|_{Y^{0}}\|{\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{X^{0}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+T\sum_{\pm}\|{\nabla\cdot(O_{\pm}\tilde{b}_{\pm})}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}\|{\mathfrak{U}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}^{(0)}O_{\pm}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+Ce^{\sigma CA}\epsilon\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}}^{2}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}2^{-(s_{0}-\frac{7}{2})k_{(0)}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|^{2}_{X^{0}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}T\|{\tilde{b}}\|^{2}_{X^{0}}.

Using (
7.58
)–(
7.60
) and Propositions
2.3
and
6.3
, we may peel off
O_{\pm}
and
O_{\pm}^{\ast}
. Moreover,
\|{\mathfrak{U}_{\mathring{{\bf B}}}^{(0)}}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}\lesssim M
and, by
\nabla\cdot b_{\pm}=0
and Proposition
2.5
(as well as (
7.58
)–(
7.60
) and Proposition
2.3
),

\|{\nabla\cdot O_{\pm}b_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}=\|{[\nabla\cdot,O_{\pm}]b_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}\lesssim_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}T\|{b_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}.

Putting all these together, we finally arrive at:

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\|{O_{\pm}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}\left(\|{\tilde{b}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\tilde{h}}\|_{Y^{0}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}}\right)

(7.63)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}2^{-(s_{0}-\frac{7}{2})k_{(0)}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|^{2}_{X^{0}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}T\|{\tilde{b}}\|^{2}_{X^{0}}.

To return to
\tilde{b}_{\pm}
, we note as in Step 4 in the proof of Proposition
7.5
that (recall that
O_{\pm}=e^{\psi_{\pm}}
)

\displaystyle\|{(1-e^{-\psi_{\pm}}(x,D)e^{\psi_{\pm}}(x,D))P_{\geq k_{(1)}}}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}\lesssim_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}2^{-k_{(1)}},

which follows from Proposition
2.5
.(1), the symbol bounds (
7.58
)–(
7.60
) and Proposition
2.3
. Similarly, but only using the fact that
1-e^{-\psi_{\pm}}(x,D)e^{\psi_{\pm}}(x,D)
is bounded on
L^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\|{(1-e^{-\psi_{\pm}}(x,D)e^{\psi_{\pm}}(x,D))P_{<k_{(1)}}\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}\lesssim_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}2^{\sigma k_{(1)}}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|{e^{-\psi_{\pm}}(x,D)O_{\pm}(x,D)\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}2^{-k_{(1)}}\|{\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}2^{\sigma k_{(1)}}\|{\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|{e^{-\psi_{\pm}}(x,D)P_{\geq k_{(1)}}O_{\pm}(x,D)\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|{e^{-\psi_{\pm}}(x,D)P_{<k_{(1)}}O_{\pm}(x,D)\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}2^{-k_{(1)}}\|{\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}2^{\sigma k_{(1)}}\|{\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{C\sigma A}\|{O_{\pm}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}2^{-k_{(1)}}\|{\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}2^{\sigma k_{(1)}}\|{\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}},

where we used Lemma
2.10
(high frequency Calderòn–Vaillancourt) to peel off
e^{-\psi_{\pm}}(x,D)P_{\geq k_{(1)}}
, and that
\|{2^{-\sigma k_{(1)}}\langle{D}\rangle^{\sigma}P_{<k_{(1)}}}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}\lesssim 1
, as well as Propositions
2.3
and
2.5
, to estimate
e^{-\psi_{\pm}}(x,D)P_{<k_{(1)}}O_{\pm}(x,D)\tilde{b}_{\pm}
in terms of
\langle{D}\rangle^{-\sigma}\tilde{b}_{\pm}
. Using (
7.63
) to bound
\|{O_{\pm}\tilde{b}_{\pm}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}
, the proof is complete. ∎

7.6.
Proof of Proposition
7.1

In this subsection, we complete the proof of Proposition
7.1
, simply by combining Propositions
7.5
,
7.6
and
7.7
. We need to prove the estimate (
7.4
), under a choice of
\epsilon_{(1)}
and
T
satisfying (
7.3
).

To begin with, applying the
L^{\infty}L^{2}
estimate on
b
(
7.51
) to the RHS of (
7.31
) gives

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}[0,T]}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}[0,T]}^{2}

(7.64)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\left(\|{b(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{h}\|_{Y^{0}[0,T]}\|{b}\|_{X^{0}[0,T]}+\|{\tilde{h}}\|_{Y^{0}[0,T]}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\left(2^{-(s_{0}-\frac{7}{2})k_{(0)}}+e^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}T\right)\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}[0,T]}^{2}.

Then, note that first by taking
k_{(0)}=k_{(0)}(M,\mu,A,L)
sufficiently large and
T=c_{(1)}e^{-C_{(1)}L}
sufficiently small so that
c_{(1)}=c_{(1)}(\sigma,s_{1},M,\mu,A,k_{(0)},k_{(1)})
and
C_{(1)}=C_{(1)}(\sigma,s_{1},M,\mu,A)
, we can absorb the last term on the RHS to the LHS. Moreover, we have
\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}[0,T]}\geq\|{b}\|_{X^{0}[0,T]}
and
\|{\tilde{h}}\|_{Y^{0}[0,T]}\geq\|{h}\|_{Y^{0}[0,T]}
simply because
\sigma\geq 0
, and therefore we can absorb the
\|{h}\|_{Y^{0}[0,T]}\|{b}\|_{X^{0}[0,T]}
term using
\epsilon
-Young inequality as well. Next, recall from the definition of
\tilde{h}
(
7.13
) that we have

\begin{split}\|{\tilde{h}}\|_{Y^{0}[0,T]}\lesssim\|{\tilde{g}}\|_{Y^{0}[0,T]}+(2^{-(s_{0}-\frac{7}{2})k_{(0)}}+T2^{2k_{(0)}})M\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}},\end{split}

which is (
7.14
). Notice that by taking
k_{(0)}
larger and then
T
smaller if necessary, we can absorb the contribution of
\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}[0,T]}
to the LHS as well; this gives

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}[0,T]}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}[0,T]}^{2}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L_{0}}\left(\|{b(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\tilde{g}}\|_{Y^{0}[0,T]}^{2}\right).

(7.65)

We now apply (
7.51
) to the RHS of (
7.52
), which gives

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}[0,T]}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}\left(\|{\tilde{b}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\tilde{h}}\|_{Y^{0}[0,T]}\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}[0,T]}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+Ce^{\sigma CA}\epsilon\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}}^{2}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}2^{-(s_{0}-\frac{7}{2})k_{(0)}}\|{\tilde{b}}\|^{2}_{X^{0}[0,T]}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{C(k_{(0)}+k_{(1)})}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}T\|{\tilde{b}}\|^{2}_{X^{0}[0,T]}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+C_{M,\mu,A}e^{Ck_{(0)}}e^{C_{M,\mu,A}L}2^{\sigma k_{(1)}}\left(\|{b(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{h}\|_{Y^{0}[0,T]}\|{b}\|_{X^{0}[0,T]}\right).

We now apply the previous estimate to the RHS of (
7.65
), and take
\epsilon_{(1)}=\epsilon_{(1)}(M,\mu,A)
sufficiently small to absorb
\epsilon\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}}^{2}
-term to the LHS. The other terms involving
\|{\tilde{b}}\|_{X^{0}[0,T]}
can be handled similarly as in the above. This finishes the proof. ∎

7.7.
Proof of Proposition
5.5

In this subsection, we will establish Proposition
5.5
. In what follows, we will omit writing the time interval
[0,T]
.

Proof of (
5.8
).

We begin by proving the identity

G(b)=\nabla\times\sum_{k}P_{[k-10,k+10]}b\times(\nabla\times P_{k}b).

(7.66)

By definition, we have

\displaystyle G(b)=\nabla\times\left[b\times(\nabla\times b)-T_{b}\times(\nabla\times b)+T_{\nabla\times b}\times b\right].

Starting with the first two terms in the brackets, we compute

\displaystyle b\times(\nabla\times b)-T_{b}\times(\nabla\times b)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k}P_{>k+10}b\times(\nabla\times P_{k}b)+\sum_{k}P_{[k-10,k+10]}b\times(\nabla\times P_{k}b)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j}P_{j}b\times(\nabla\times P_{<j-10}b)+\sum_{k}P_{[k-10,k+10]}b\times(\nabla\times P_{k}b),

and observe that the first term on the last line is precisely
T_{\nabla\times b}\times b
.

We use (
6.20
) to estimate the
\nabla\times(\hbox{RHS of \eqref{eq:paralin-err-decomp}})
as follows:

\displaystyle\|{\nabla\times\sum_{k}P_{[k-10,k+10]}b\times(\nabla\times P_{k}b)}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}Y^{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\ell}\sum_{k:k>\ell-20}T2^{(\frac{5}{2}+\sigma)\ell}\|{P_{[k-10,k+10]}b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X_{k}}\|{P_{k}b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X_{k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s_{0}}}\|{b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{\sigma}}\sum_{\ell}\sum_{k:k>\ell-20}T2^{(\frac{5}{2}+\sigma)\ell}2^{-(s_{0}+\sigma-1)k}

\displaystyle\lesssim T\|{b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s_{0}}}\|{b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{\sigma}},

which proves the desired estimate. ∎

Proof of (
5.9
).

By (
7.66
), we have

\displaystyle G(b)-G(\overline{b})=\nabla\times\sum_{k}\left(P_{k-5\leq\cdot\leq k+5}b\times(\nabla\times P_{k}(b-\overline{b}))+P_{k-5\leq\cdot\leq k+5}(b-\overline{b})\times(\nabla\times P_{k}\overline{b})\right).

Then the desired estimate follows from (
6.20
) as in the proof of (
5.8
).
∎

Proof of (
5.10
).

We begin with
\nabla\times(T_{b-\overline{b}}\times(\nabla\times b))
. Introducing a parameter
m
to be determined later, we write

\displaystyle\nabla\times(T_{b-\overline{b}}\times(\nabla\times b))

\displaystyle=\nabla\times\sum_{k}P_{<k-m}(b-\overline{b})\times(\nabla\times P_{k}b)+\nabla\times\sum_{k}P_{[k-m,k-10)}(b-\overline{b})\times(\nabla\times P_{k}b).

By our conventions (see Section
2.1
),
P_{<k-m}(b-\overline{b})=0
and
P_{[k-m,k-10)}=P_{<k-10}
unless
k>m
. For the first term, we use (
6.19
) (with
(a,b)=(b,b-\overline{b})
) and estimate

\displaystyle\|{\nabla\times\sum_{k}P_{<k-m}(b-\overline{b})\times(\nabla\times P_{k}b)}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}Y^{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\sum_{k}\Big(2^{(\sigma+1)k}\sum_{j<k-m}2^{\frac{5}{2}j}\|{P_{j}(b-\overline{b})}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X_{j}}\|{P_{k}b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X_{k}}\Big)^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\sum_{k}\Big(2^{(\sigma+1)k}\sum_{j<k-m}2^{\frac{5}{2}j}\|{P_{j}(b-\overline{b})}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X_{j}}\|{P_{k}b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X_{k}}\Big)^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-(s_{0}-1-\sigma)m}\|{b-\overline{b}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{\sigma}}\Big(\sum_{k}\Big(2^{s_{0}k}\|{P_{k}b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X_{k}}\sum_{j<k-m}2^{-(s_{0}-\frac{7}{2})j}\Big)^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-(s_{0}-1-\sigma)m}M\|{b-\overline{b}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{\sigma}}.

For the second term, we use (
6.20
) to estimate

\displaystyle\|{\nabla\times\sum_{k}P_{[k-m,k-10)}(b-\overline{b})\times(\nabla\times P_{k}b)}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}Y^{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T\Big(\sum_{k}\Big(2^{(\sigma+\frac{7}{2})k}\sum_{j\in[k-m,k-10)}\|{P_{j}(b-\overline{b})}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X_{j}}\|{P_{k}b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X_{k}}\Big)^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T2^{\sigma m}\|{b-\overline{b}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{\sigma}}\Big(\sum_{k}\Big(2^{-(s_{0}-\frac{7}{2})k}2^{s_{0}k}\|{P_{k}b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X_{k}}\Big)^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T2^{\sigma m}M_{0}\|{b-\overline{b}}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{\sigma}}.

Choosing
m
so that
T=2^{-(s_{0}-1)m}
, the desired estimate follows for
\nabla\times(T_{b-\overline{b}}\times(\nabla\times b))
.

The remaining term
\nabla\times(T_{\nabla\times(b-\overline{b})}\times b)
is handled similarly (in fact, the numerology is more favorable compared to the previous case since
\nabla\times
now falls on
b-\overline{b}
) and will be omitted. ∎

Proof of (
5.11
).

The desired bounds for the paraproduct terms
\nabla\times(T_{b}\times(\nabla\times b))
and
\nabla\times(T_{\nabla\times b}\times b)
follow by writing out these expressions, then estimating the first (i.e., low frequency) factor of
b
in
L^{\infty}
(to which
\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s_{1}}
embeds) and the second (i.e., high frequency) factor of
b
in
\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s_{1}}
. For
G(b)
, we use (
7.66
) and bound (using Littlewood–Paley theory, Corollary
6.2
and Hölder),

\displaystyle\|{\nabla\times\sum_{k}P_{[k-10,k+10]}b\times(\nabla\times P_{k}b)}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}H^{s_{1}-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\ell}\sum_{k:k>\ell-20}2^{(s_{1}-1)\ell}\|{P_{\ell}(P_{[k-10,k+10]}b\times(\nabla\times P_{k}b))}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\ell}\sum_{k:k>\ell-20}2^{(s_{1}-1)\ell}2^{\frac{3}{2}\ell}2^{k}\|{P_{[k-10,k+10]}b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{2}}\|{P_{k}b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s_{1}}}^{2}\sum_{\ell}\sum_{k:k>\ell-20}2^{(s_{1}+\frac{1}{2})\ell}2^{-(2s_{1}-1)k}\lesssim\|{b}\|_{\ell^{1}_{\mathcal{I}}X^{s_{1}}}^{2},

where we used
s_{1}>\frac{1}{2}
in the last inequality.
∎
